    pub reasoning: String,
}

/// Chat completions, streamed: the gateway's, or a model's own server's.
pub trait ChatClient {
    /// Sends `request` to the server listening on `addr`, and times the
    /// stream of its reply.
    fn stream(&self, addr: SocketAddr, request: &ChatRequest) -> Result<Reply>;
}

/// Containers and their images, in podman's local storage.
pub trait Containers {
    fn image_exists(&self, image: &str) -> Result<bool>;
    /// Pulls `image` from its registry.
    fn pull(&self, image: &str) -> Result<()>;
    /// The host PIDs of the running containers whose name starts with
    /// `prefix`.
    fn pids(&self, prefix: &str) -> Result<Vec<u32>>;
}
</file>

<file path="src/application/toolchain.rs">
//! The build toolchain: the checks of `doctor`'s Toolchain section, which
//! also gate a build, and the toolchain they settle. The remedies give the
//! commands of `docs/toolchain.md`.

use crate::domain::build::{Toolchain, c_compiler_beside};
use crate::domain::diagnostics::{Check, Status};
use crate::domain::machine::{
    BROKEN_TOOLKIT, CudaRepository, CudaToolkit, DRIVER_PACKAGES, Gpu, HostCompiler, Probed, Tool,
    Version, cuda_repository_id, cuda_repository_url, is_unversioned_toolkit, pick_host_compiler,
    toolkit_problem, versioned_toolkit,
};

use super::ports::HostProbe;

/// Driver packages named in a warning before the count stands for the rest.
const NAMED_PACKAGES: usize = 3;

/// The Toolchain checks, and the toolchain a build compiles with when none
/// of them failed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Inspection {
    pub checks: Vec<Check>,
    pub toolchain: Option<Toolchain>,
}

/// Checks the CUDA toolkit against `gpu`'s driver, NVIDIA's repositories
/// and the installed toolkit packages, the host compiler against the
/// toolkit, and the build tools against their minimums.
pub fn inspect(host: &dyn HostProbe, gpu: Option<&Gpu>) -> Inspection {
    let toolkit = host.cuda_toolkit();
    let repositories = host.cuda_repositories();
    let setup = Setup {
        driver_cuda: gpu.map(|gpu| gpu.cuda_version),
        fedora: host.fedora_release().found().copied(),
        repositories: repositories.found().map(Vec::as_slice),
    };
    let (compiler, picked) = compiler_check(host, &toolkit);
    let mut checks = vec![
        toolkit_check(&toolkit, &setup),
        repository_check(&repositories),
        package_check(&host.toolkit_packages(), &toolkit, &setup),
        compiler,
        minimum_check(host, "CMake", Tool::Cmake, "sudo dnf install cmake"),
        minimum_check(host, "Git", Tool::Git, "sudo dnf install git"),
    ];
    let failed = checks.iter().any(|check| check.status == Status::Fail);
    let toolchain = match (failed, toolkit.found(), picked) {
        (false, Some(toolkit), Some((compiler, c_compiler))) => {
            match Toolchain::new(toolkit.nvcc.clone(), toolkit.version, compiler, c_compiler) {
                Ok(toolchain) => Some(toolchain),
                Err(err) => {
                    checks.push(Check::fail("Toolchain paths", err.to_string(), None));
                    None
                }
            }
        }
        _ => None,
    };
    Inspection { checks, toolchain }
}

/// What the toolkit remedy depends on.
struct Setup<'a> {
    driver_cuda: Option<Version>,
    fedora: Option<u32>,
    /// `None` when the repositories could not be read.
    repositories: Option<&'a [CudaRepository]>,
}

impl Setup<'_> {
    /// The versioned toolkit package matching the driver.
    fn package(&self) -> String {
        match self.driver_cuda {
            Some(cuda) => format!("cuda-toolkit-{}-{}", cuda.major, cuda.minor),
            None => "cuda-toolkit-<major>-<minor>".to_owned(),
        }
    }

    /// Installs the toolkit matching the driver from NVIDIA's repository for
    /// the running Fedora release, adding the repository and its driver
    /// exclusion first when they are missing (`docs/toolchain.md` §2).
    fn toolkit_remedy(&self) -> Option<String> {
        if self
            .driver_cuda
            .is_some_and(|cuda| cuda.release() == BROKEN_TOOLKIT)
        {
            return fix(
                "update the NVIDIA driver to one supporting CUDA 13.3 or newer, then install that toolkit",
            );
        }
        let install = format!("sudo dnf install {}", self.package());
        let Some(fedora) = self.fedora else {
            return fix(format!(
                "add NVIDIA's CUDA repository for your Fedora release with its driver packages excluded (docs/toolchain.md §2), then: {install}"
            ));
        };
        let id = cuda_repository_id(fedora);
        let repository = self
            .repositories
            .and_then(|repositories| repositories.iter().find(|repo| repo.id == id));
        let mut steps = Vec::new();
        if repository.is_none() {
            steps.push(format!(
                "sudo dnf config-manager addrepo --from-repofile={}",
                cuda_repository_url(fedora)
            ));
        }
        if repository.is_none_or(|repo| !repo.driver_packages.is_empty()) {
            steps.push(exclude_command(&id));
        }
        steps.push(install);
        fix(numbered(&steps))
    }
}

/// Excludes the driver packages from a CUDA repository.
fn exclude_command(id: &str) -> String {
    format!(
        "sudo dnf config-manager setopt {id}.exclude='{}'",
        DRIVER_PACKAGES.join(",")
    )
}

/// Steps one per line, numbered when there are several.
fn numbered(steps: &[String]) -> String {
    if steps.len() == 1 {
        return steps[0].clone();
    }
    steps
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .map(|(i, step)| format!("{}. {step}", i + 1))
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join("\n")
}

fn toolkit_check(toolkit: &Probed<CudaToolkit>, setup: &Setup) -> Check {
    const NAME: &str = "CUDA toolkit";
    let toolkit = match toolkit {
        Probed::Found(toolkit) => toolkit,
        Probed::Missing => {
            return Check::fail(
                NAME,
                "nvcc not found on the PATH nor in /usr/local/cuda/bin",
                setup.toolkit_remedy(),
            );
        }
        Probed::Failed(err) => {
            return Check::fail(NAME, format!("nvcc: {err}"), setup.toolkit_remedy());
        }
    };

    let found = format!("{} ({})", toolkit.version, toolkit.nvcc.display());
    match toolkit_problem(toolkit.version, setup.driver_cuda) {
        Some(problem) => Check::fail(NAME, format!("{found}: {problem}"), setup.toolkit_remedy()),
        None => Check::ok(NAME, found),
    }
}

/// An enabled CUDA repository offering driver packages would replace the
/// RPM Fusion driver with the first driver NVIDIA publishes before it.
fn repository_check(repositories: &Probed<Vec<CudaRepository>>) -> Check {
    const NAME: &str = "CUDA repositories";
    let repositories = match repositories {
        Probed::Found(repositories) => repositories,
        Probed::Missing => return Check::skip(NAME, "dnf not found"),
        Probed::Failed(err) => return Check::warn(NAME, format!("dnf: {err}"), None),
    };
    if repositories.is_empty() {
        return Check::ok(NAME, "none enabled");
    }
    let offering: Vec<&CudaRepository> = repositories
        .iter()
        .filter(|repo| !repo.driver_packages.is_empty())
        .collect();
    if offering.is_empty() {
        let ids: Vec<&str> = repositories.iter().map(|repo| repo.id.as_str()).collect();
        return Check::ok(
            NAME,
            format!("{}: driver packages excluded", ids.join(", ")),
        );
    }
    let detail = offering
        .iter()
        .map(|repo| {
            let packages = &repo.driver_packages;
            let mut named = packages[..packages.len().min(NAMED_PACKAGES)].join(", ");
            if packages.len() > NAMED_PACKAGES {
                named.push_str(", …");
            }
            format!(
                "{} offers {} driver packages ({named})",
                repo.id,
                packages.len()
            )
        })
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join("; ");
    let remedy: Vec<String> = offering
        .iter()
        .map(|repo| exclude_command(&repo.id))
        .collect();
    Check::warn(
        NAME,
        format!("{detail}: an update could replace the RPM Fusion driver"),
        Some(remedy.join("\n")),
    )
}

/// An unversioned toolkit meta-package follows NVIDIA's newest release, so
/// an update can install a toolkit newer than the driver.
fn package_check(
    packages: &Probed<Vec<String>>,
    toolkit: &Probed<CudaToolkit>,
    setup: &Setup,
) -> Check {
    const NAME: &str = "Toolkit packages";
    let packages = match packages {
        Probed::Found(packages) => packages,
        Probed::Missing => return Check::skip(NAME, "rpm not found"),
        Probed::Failed(err) => return Check::warn(NAME, format!("rpm: {err}"), None),
    };
    let unversioned: Vec<&str> = packages
        .iter()
        .map(String::as_str)
        .filter(|package| is_unversioned_toolkit(package))
        .collect();
    if unversioned.is_empty() {
        let versioned: Vec<&str> = packages
            .iter()
            .map(String::as_str)
            .filter(|package| versioned_toolkit(package).is_some())
            .collect();
        let detail = if versioned.is_empty() {
            "no toolkit meta-package installed".to_owned()
        } else {
            versioned.join(", ")
        };
        return Check::ok(NAME, detail);
    }
    // Keep the installed toolkit's release, or take the driver's.
    let keep = match toolkit.found() {
        Some(toolkit) => format!(
            "cuda-toolkit-{}-{}",
            toolkit.version.major, toolkit.version.minor
        ),
        None => setup.package(),
    };
    Check::warn(
        NAME,
        format!(
            "{} installed: it follows NVIDIA's newest release, which can outrun the driver",
            unversioned.join(", ")
        ),
        fix(numbered(&[
            format!("sudo dnf install {keep}"),
            format!("sudo dnf remove {}", unversioned.join(" ")),
        ])),
    )
}

/// The host compiler check, and the C++ and C compilers a build uses: the
/// newest GCC `nvcc` accepts, or the default `g++` when its limit is
/// unknown.
fn compiler_check(
    host: &dyn HostProbe,
    toolkit: &Probed<CudaToolkit>,
) -> (Check, Option<(HostCompiler, std::path::PathBuf)>) {
    const NAME: &str = "Host compiler";
    let compilers = match host.host_compilers() {
        Probed::Found(compilers) if !compilers.is_empty() => compilers,
        Probed::Found(_) | Probed::Missing => {
            let check = Check::fail(
                NAME,
                "no g++ found on the PATH",
                fix("sudo dnf install gcc-c++"),
            );
            return (check, None);
        }
        Probed::Failed(err) => return (Check::fail(NAME, format!("g++: {err}"), None), None),
    };
    let found = compiler_list(&compilers);
    let Some(toolkit) = toolkit.found() else {
        let check = Check::skip(
            NAME,
            format!("{found}; the GCC limit of nvcc needs the CUDA toolkit"),
        );
        return (check, None);
    };
    let (check, picked) = match &toolkit.max_gcc_major {
        Probed::Found(max) => match pick_host_compiler(&compilers, *max) {
            Some(picked) => (
                Check::ok(
                    NAME,
                    format!(
                        "{} {}; nvcc {} accepts GCC {max} at most (found {found})",
                        picked.command, picked.version, toolkit.version
                    ),
                ),
                Some(picked),
            ),
            None => (
                Check::fail(
                    NAME,
                    format!(
                        "nvcc {} accepts GCC {max} at most (found {found})",
                        toolkit.version
                    ),
                    fix(format!("sudo dnf install gcc{max}-c++")),
                ),
                None,
            ),
        },
        Probed::Missing => (
            Check::warn(
                NAME,
                format!(
                    "{found}; no host_config.h in the toolkit, so the GCC limit of nvcc is unknown"
                ),
                None,
            ),
            compilers.first(),
        ),
        Probed::Failed(err) => (
            Check::warn(
                NAME,
                format!("{found}; cannot read the GCC limit of nvcc: {err}"),
                None,
            ),
            compilers.first(),
        ),
    };
    let Some(picked) = picked else {
        return (check, None);
    };
    match c_compiler_beside(&picked.path).filter(|c| host.is_file(c)) {
        Some(c_compiler) => (check, Some((picked.clone(), c_compiler))),
        None => (
            Check::fail(
                NAME,
                format!(
                    "no C compiler beside {} ({})",
                    picked.command,
                    picked.path.display()
                ),
                fix(format!(
                    "sudo dnf install {}",
                    picked.command.replacen("g++", "gcc", 1).replace('-', "")
                )),
            ),
            None,
        ),
    }
}

fn minimum_check(host: &dyn HostProbe, name: &str, tool: Tool, remedy: &str) -> Check {
    let minimum = tool.minimum().unwrap_or(Version::new(0, 0, 0));
    match host.tool_version(tool) {
        Probed::Found(version) if version >= minimum => Check::ok(name, version.to_string()),
        Probed::Found(version) => Check::fail(
            name,
            format!("{version} is older than the {minimum} required"),
            fix(remedy),
        ),
        Probed::Missing => Check::fail(name, format!("{} not found", tool.command()), fix(remedy)),
        Probed::Failed(err) => Check::fail(name, format!("{}: {err}", tool.command()), None),
    }
}

fn compiler_list(compilers: &[HostCompiler]) -> String {
    compilers
        .iter()
        .map(|compiler| format!("{} {}", compiler.command, compiler.version))
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join(", ")
}

fn fix(remedy: impl Into<String>) -> Option<String> {
    Some(remedy.into())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

    use super::*;
    use crate::application::fakes::{FakeHost, gpu, gxx, toolkit};
    use crate::domain::machine::CudaRepository;

    fn check<'a>(inspection: &'a Inspection, name: &str) -> &'a Check {
        inspection
            .checks
            .iter()
            .find(|check| check.name == name)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no check named {name}"))
    }

    fn toolkit_remedy(host: &FakeHost, gpu: Option<&Gpu>) -> String {
        let inspection = inspect(host, gpu);
        check(&inspection, "CUDA toolkit")
            .remedy
            .clone()
            .expect("a remedy")
    }

    const EXCLUDE: &str = "sudo dnf config-manager setopt cuda-fedora44-x86_64.exclude='nvidia-*,kmod-nvidia*,xorg-x11-*nvidia*,libnvidia-*,dnf-plugin-nvidia'";

    #[test]
    fn the_remedy_adds_the_repository_of_the_running_release_with_its_exclusion() {
        let remedy = toolkit_remedy(&FakeHost::before_the_upgrade(), Some(&gpu()));
        assert_eq!(
            remedy,
            format!(
                "1. sudo dnf config-manager addrepo --from-repofile=https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/repos/fedora44/x86_64/cuda-fedora44.repo\n\
                 2. {EXCLUDE}\n\
                 3. sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-13-4"
            )
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_remedy_skips_what_is_already_set_up() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        host.toolkit = Probed::Missing;
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_remedy(&host, Some(&gpu())),
            "sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-13-4"
        );
        host.repositories = Probed::Found(vec![CudaRepository {
            id: "cuda-fedora44-x86_64".into(),
            driver_packages: vec!["nvidia-driver".into()],
        }]);
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_remedy(&host, Some(&gpu())),
            format!("1. {EXCLUDE}\n2. sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-13-4")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_remedy_follows_the_fedora_release() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::before_the_upgrade();
        host.fedora = Probed::Found(45);
        let remedy = toolkit_remedy(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        assert!(
            remedy.contains("/fedora45/x86_64/cuda-fedora45.repo"),
            "{remedy}"
        );
        assert!(
            remedy.contains("setopt cuda-fedora45-x86_64.exclude="),
            "{remedy}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn unreadable_repositories_give_every_step() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::before_the_upgrade();
        host.repositories = Probed::Failed("dnf crashed".into());
        assert!(
            toolkit_remedy(&host, Some(&gpu())).starts_with("1. sudo dnf config-manager addrepo")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn without_fedora_or_driver_the_remedy_points_at_the_document() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::before_the_upgrade();
        host.fedora = Probed::Missing;
        let remedy = toolkit_remedy(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        assert!(remedy.contains("docs/toolchain.md"), "{remedy}");
        assert!(
            remedy.ends_with("sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-13-4"),
            "{remedy}"
        );

        let remedy = toolkit_remedy(&FakeHost::before_the_upgrade(), None);
        assert!(
            remedy.ends_with("3. sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-<major>-<minor>"),
            "{remedy}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_driver_stuck_at_cuda_13_2_must_be_updated_first() {
        let mut stuck = gpu();
        stuck.cuda_version = Version::new(13, 2, 0);
        let remedy = toolkit_remedy(&FakeHost::before_the_upgrade(), Some(&stuck));
        assert!(remedy.starts_with("update the NVIDIA driver"), "{remedy}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_repository_offering_driver_packages_warns() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        host.repositories = Probed::Found(vec![CudaRepository {
            id: "cuda-fedora44-x86_64".into(),
            driver_packages: vec![
                "kmod-nvidia-open-dkms".into(),
                "nvidia-driver".into(),
                "nvidia-settings".into(),
                "xorg-x11-nvidia".into(),
            ],
        }]);
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        let repositories = check(&inspection, "CUDA repositories");
        assert_eq!(repositories.status, Status::Warn);
        assert_eq!(
            repositories.detail,
            "cuda-fedora44-x86_64 offers 4 driver packages (kmod-nvidia-open-dkms, nvidia-driver, nvidia-settings, …): an update could replace the RPM Fusion driver"
        );
        assert_eq!(repositories.remedy.as_deref(), Some(EXCLUDE));
        assert!(
            inspection.toolchain.is_some(),
            "a warning does not refuse a build"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn repositories_without_driver_packages_pass() {
        let inspection = inspect(&FakeHost::healthy(), Some(&gpu()));
        assert_eq!(
            check(&inspection, "CUDA repositories").detail,
            "cuda-fedora44-x86_64: driver packages excluded"
        );
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        host.repositories = Probed::Found(vec![]);
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        assert_eq!(
            check(&inspection, "CUDA repositories").detail,
            "none enabled"
        );
        host.repositories = Probed::Missing;
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        assert_eq!(check(&inspection, "CUDA repositories").status, Status::Skip);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unversioned_toolkit_package_warns_with_its_replacement() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        host.toolkit_packages = Probed::Found(vec![
            "cuda-toolkit".into(),
            "cuda-toolkit-13".into(),
            "cuda-toolkit-13-4".into(),
        ]);
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        let packages = check(&inspection, "Toolkit packages");
        assert_eq!(packages.status, Status::Warn);
        assert!(
            packages
                .detail
                .starts_with("cuda-toolkit, cuda-toolkit-13 installed")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            packages.remedy.as_deref(),
            Some(
                "1. sudo dnf install cuda-toolkit-13-4\n2. sudo dnf remove cuda-toolkit cuda-toolkit-13"
            )
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn versioned_toolkit_packages_pass() {
        let inspection = inspect(&FakeHost::healthy(), Some(&gpu()));
        let packages = check(&inspection, "Toolkit packages");
        assert_eq!(
            (packages.status, packages.detail.as_str()),
            (Status::Ok, "cuda-toolkit-13-4")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_healthy_machine_settles_the_toolchain() {
        let inspection = inspect(&FakeHost::healthy(), Some(&gpu()));
        assert!(
            inspection.checks.iter().all(|c| c.status == Status::Ok),
            "{:#?}",
            inspection.checks
        );
        let toolchain = inspection.toolchain.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(toolchain.nvcc(), Path::new("/usr/local/cuda-13.4/bin/nvcc"));
        assert_eq!(toolchain.toolkit(), Version::new(13, 4, 92));
        assert_eq!(toolchain.compiler().command, "g++-15");
        assert_eq!(toolchain.c_compiler(), Path::new("/usr/bin/gcc-15"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn any_failed_check_leaves_no_toolchain() {
        let mut broken = FakeHost::healthy();
        broken.toolkit = Probed::Found(toolkit(Version::new(13, 2, 86)));
        let mut old_git = FakeHost::healthy();
        old_git.git = Probed::Found(Version::new(2, 17, 0));
        let mut no_cmake = FakeHost::healthy();
        no_cmake.cmake = Probed::Missing;
        for host in [broken, old_git, no_cmake] {
            let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
            assert!(inspection.checks.iter().any(|c| c.status == Status::Fail));
            assert_eq!(inspection.toolchain, None);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_c_compiler_fails_the_compiler_check() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        host.files.remove(Path::new("/usr/bin/gcc-15"));
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        let compiler = check(&inspection, "Host compiler");
        assert_eq!(compiler.status, Status::Fail);
        assert_eq!(
            compiler.detail,
            "no C compiler beside g++-15 (/usr/bin/g++-15)"
        );
        assert_eq!(compiler.remedy.as_deref(), Some("sudo dnf install gcc15"));
        assert_eq!(inspection.toolchain, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unknown_gcc_limit_builds_with_the_default_compiler() {
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        let mut kit = toolkit(Version::new(13, 4, 92));
        kit.max_gcc_major = Probed::Failed("unreadable".into());
        host.toolkit = Probed::Found(kit);
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        assert_eq!(check(&inspection, "Host compiler").status, Status::Warn);
        let toolchain = inspection.toolchain.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(toolchain.compiler().command, "g++");
        assert_eq!(toolchain.c_compiler(), Path::new("/usr/bin/gcc"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_non_utf8_toolkit_path_is_reported() {
        use std::ffi::OsStr;
        use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt;
        let mut host = FakeHost::healthy();
        let mut kit = toolkit(Version::new(13, 4, 92));
        kit.nvcc = PathBuf::from(OsStr::from_bytes(b"/opt/cuda-\xff/bin/nvcc"));
        host.toolkit = Probed::Found(kit);
        let inspection = inspect(&host, Some(&gpu()));
        let last = inspection.checks.last().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            (last.name.as_str(), last.status),
            ("Toolchain paths", Status::Fail)
        );
        assert_eq!(inspection.toolchain, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_compiler_list_names_every_compiler() {
        let compilers = [
            gxx("g++", Version::new(16, 2, 1)),
            gxx("g++-15", Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
        ];
        assert_eq!(compiler_list(&compilers), "g++ 16.2.1, g++-15 15.3.1");
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/bench.rs">
//! The bench: what a run sends through the gateway, scenario by scenario,
//! and what each request gives back.
//!
//! The scenarios follow the measurement protocol of `docs/stack.md` §9. Each
//! request is measured twice: by the client, which times the stream, and by
//! llama-server, whose `timings` object tells how many prompt tokens came from
//! the cache and how fast the rest went.

use std::fmt;
use std::iter::Sum;
use std::ops::Add;
use std::time::Duration;

use crate::domain::stats::Summary;

/// The scenarios of a run, in the order it takes them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Scenario {
    /// Generation speed at several context depths, each context prefilled
    /// once so the repetitions measure generation only.
    Depth,
    /// Prefill speed on a prompt the cache has never seen: an agent's first
    /// request.
    Prefill,
    /// An agent's session, its history sent back at every turn: the reuse of
    /// the prompt cache.
    Session,
    /// The gateway's own cost: the same request through llama-swap and
    /// straight to llama-server.
    Overhead,
}

impl Scenario {
    pub const ALL: [Self; 4] = [Self::Depth, Self::Prefill, Self::Session, Self::Overhead];

    pub fn name(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Depth => "depth",
            Self::Prefill => "prefill",
            Self::Session => "session",
            Self::Overhead => "overhead",
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Scenario {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.name())
    }
}

/// The depths at which generation is measured when they fit below the
/// context's limit: 8K, 32K and 64K tokens.
pub const DEPTHS: [u32; 3] = [8_192, 32_768, 65_536];
/// Tokens each request of the depth and session scenarios generates.
pub const GENERATE: u32 = 512;
/// The fresh prompt of the prefill scenario: 40K tokens, the size of an
/// agent's first request (`docs/stack.md` §9).
pub const PREFILL: u32 = 40_960;
/// Tokens a prefill request generates: one, since only the time to it counts.
pub const PREFILL_GENERATE: u32 = 1;
/// How many times each measure is made, by default.
pub const REPEAT: u32 = 5;
/// Requests the overhead scenario sends each way: the difference it measures
/// is in milliseconds, against seconds for the other scenarios.
pub const OVERHEAD_REQUESTS: u32 = 50;
/// Tokens each request of the overhead scenario generates.
pub const OVERHEAD_GENERATE: u32 = 16;

/// A context depth at which generation is measured.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Depth {
    /// Tokens of the prompt.
    pub tokens: u32,
    /// Whether it is the context's limit: its size less the tokens to
    /// generate.
    pub at_limit: bool,
}

/// The shape of an agent's session.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SessionPlan {
    /// Turns of a session.
    pub turns: u32,
    /// Tokens of the first prompt: an agent's system prompt and task.
    pub opening: u32,
    /// Tokens each later turn adds after the reply: a tool's output, a
    /// message.
    pub turn: u32,
}

impl SessionPlan {
    /// Ten turns, opening on 8K tokens and adding 1K at each turn.
    pub const DEFAULT: Self = Self {
        turns: 10,
        opening: 8_192,
        turn: 1_024,
    };

    /// The most context a session can fill: its last prompt and reply, every
    /// reply at full length and kept in the history.
    pub fn peak(&self, generate: u32) -> u64 {
        u64::from(self.opening)
            + u64::from(self.turns.saturating_sub(1)) * u64::from(self.turn)
            + u64::from(self.turns) * u64::from(generate)
    }
}

/// What a run sends, scenario by scenario.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Plan {
    /// How many times each measure is made.
    pub repeat: u32,
    /// Tokens each request of the depth and session scenarios generates.
    pub generate: u32,
    /// Ascending, the last one at the context's limit.
    pub depths: Vec<Depth>,
    /// Tokens of the prefill scenario's prompt.
    pub prefill: u32,
    pub session: SessionPlan,
    /// Requests the overhead scenario sends each way.
    pub overhead: u32,
}

/// Why no plan fits a model.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum PlanError {
    #[error("each measure must be made at least once")]
    NoRepetition,
    #[error(
        "the {scenario} scenario needs a context of {needed} tokens; the model's holds {ctx_size}"
    )]
    ContextTooSmall {
        scenario: Scenario,
        needed: u64,
        ctx_size: u32,
    },
}

impl Plan {
    /// The default plan for a model whose context holds `ctx_size` tokens,
    /// each measure made `repeat` times. The prefill prompt shrinks to the
    /// context's limit when it does not fit; a session that does not fit is
    /// an error.
    pub fn new(ctx_size: u32, repeat: u32) -> Result<Self, PlanError> {
        if repeat == 0 {
            return Err(PlanError::NoRepetition);
        }
        let limit = ctx_size.saturating_sub(GENERATE);
        if limit == 0 {
            return Err(PlanError::ContextTooSmall {
                scenario: Scenario::Depth,
                needed: u64::from(GENERATE) + 1,
                ctx_size,
            });
        }
        let session = SessionPlan::DEFAULT;
        if session.peak(GENERATE) > u64::from(ctx_size) {
            return Err(PlanError::ContextTooSmall {
                scenario: Scenario::Session,
                needed: session.peak(GENERATE),
                ctx_size,
            });
        }
        Ok(Self {
            repeat,
            generate: GENERATE,
            depths: depths(limit),
            prefill: PREFILL.min(ctx_size - PREFILL_GENERATE),
            session,
            overhead: OVERHEAD_REQUESTS,
        })
    }
}

/// [`DEPTHS`] below `limit`, then `limit` itself.
fn depths(limit: u32) -> Vec<Depth> {
    DEPTHS
        .iter()
        .filter(|&&tokens| tokens < limit)
        .map(|&tokens| Depth {
            tokens,
            at_limit: false,
        })
        .chain([Depth {
            tokens: limit,
            at_limit: true,
        }])
        .collect()
}

/// Who speaks a message.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Role {
    System,
    User,
    Assistant,
}

impl Role {
    /// The name chat completions give the role.
    pub fn name(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::System => "system",
            Self::User => "user",
            Self::Assistant => "assistant",
        }
    }
}

/// A message of a conversation the bench sends.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Message {
    pub role: Role,
    pub content: String,
    /// An assistant's reasoning, sent back in the history as OpenCode does.
    pub reasoning: Option<String>,
}

impl Message {
    pub fn system(content: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            role: Role::System,
            content: content.into(),
            reasoning: None,
        }
    }

    pub fn user(content: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            role: Role::User,
            content: content.into(),
            reasoning: None,
        }
    }

    /// An assistant's reply, with its reasoning when there was any.
    pub fn assistant(content: impl Into<String>, reasoning: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        let reasoning = reasoning.into();
        Self {
            role: Role::Assistant,
            content: content.into(),
            reasoning: (!reasoning.is_empty()).then_some(reasoning),
        }
    }
}

/// What the client timed of one streamed request.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct ClientTimings {
    /// From sending the request to the first content or reasoning token.
    pub ttft: Duration,
    /// From sending the request to the end of the stream.
    pub total: Duration,
    /// The gap before each token after the first, zero for tokens that
    /// arrived together; see [`arrival_gaps`].
    pub gaps: Vec<Duration>,
}

/// Draft tokens of speculative decoding (MTP): proposed, and accepted by the
/// model.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Drafts {
    pub proposed: u64,
    pub accepted: u64,
}

impl Drafts {
    /// The share of proposed tokens accepted; `None` when none was proposed.
    pub fn acceptance(self) -> Option<f64> {
        (self.proposed > 0).then(|| self.accepted as f64 / self.proposed as f64)
    }
}

impl Add for Drafts {
    type Output = Self;

    fn add(self, other: Self) -> Self {
        Self {
            proposed: self.proposed + other.proposed,
            accepted: self.accepted + other.accepted,
        }
    }
}

impl Sum for Drafts {
    fn sum<I: Iterator<Item = Self>>(drafts: I) -> Self {
        drafts.fold(Self::default(), Add::add)
    }
}

/// llama-server's `timings` object for one request.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct ServerTimings {
    /// Prompt tokens taken from the cache.
    pub cache_n: u64,
    /// Prompt tokens evaluated.
    pub prompt_n: u64,
    pub prompt_ms: f64,
    /// Tokens generated.
    pub predicted_n: u64,
    pub predicted_ms: f64,
    /// `None` when no draft was proposed: llama-server leaves `draft_n` and
    /// `draft_n_accepted` out then.
    pub drafts: Option<Drafts>,
}

impl ServerTimings {
    /// Prompt tokens evaluated per second.
    pub fn prefill_speed(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        rate(self.prompt_n, self.prompt_ms)
    }

    /// Tokens generated per second.
    pub fn generation_speed(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        rate(self.predicted_n, self.predicted_ms)
    }
}

/// `count` per second over `ms` milliseconds; `None` for no count or no time.
fn rate(count: u64, ms: f64) -> Option<f64> {
    (count > 0 && ms > 0.0).then(|| count as f64 * 1000.0 / ms)
}

/// The `usage` object of an OpenAI-compatible response.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Usage {
    pub prompt_tokens: u64,
    pub completion_tokens: u64,
}

/// One measured request.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Sample {
    pub client: ClientTimings,
    /// `None` from a server that does not send llama-server's `timings`.
    pub server: Option<ServerTimings>,
    pub usage: Option<Usage>,
}

impl Sample {
    pub fn ttft_ms(&self) -> f64 {
        millis(self.client.ttft)
    }

    pub fn total_ms(&self) -> f64 {
        millis(self.client.total)
    }

    /// Tokens generated per second: the server's figure, or else the client's,
    /// from the tokens after the first over the time after it.
    pub fn generation_speed(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match self.server {
            Some(server) => server.generation_speed(),
            None => {
                let usage = self.usage?;
                let after_first = self.client.total.checked_sub(self.client.ttft)?;
                rate(
                    usage.completion_tokens.saturating_sub(1),
                    millis(after_first),
                )
            }
        }
    }

    /// Prompt tokens evaluated per second, as the server reports it.
    pub fn prefill_speed(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        self.server?.prefill_speed()
    }

    /// Prompt tokens taken from the cache.
    pub fn cached(&self) -> Option<u64> {
        self.server.map(|server| server.cache_n)
    }

    /// Prompt tokens evaluated.
    pub fn evaluated(&self) -> Option<u64> {
        self.server.map(|server| server.prompt_n)
    }
}

fn millis(duration: Duration) -> f64 {
    duration.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0
}

/// Tokens closer than this arrived together: a decoding step takes tens of
/// milliseconds, and the drafts it accepts are sent at once.
pub const BURST: Duration = Duration::from_millis(1);

/// The gaps between the arrivals of tokens in `samples`, in milliseconds.
/// With speculative decoding, tokens come in bursts, one per decoding step
/// (on the 27B with MTP, two gaps in three were under a millisecond): a
/// gap under [`BURST`] continues a burst and is left out, so the median is
/// the time of a step and the p99 its stalls.
pub fn arrival_gaps(samples: &[Sample]) -> Option<Summary> {
    Summary::of(
        samples
            .iter()
            .flat_map(|sample| sample.client.gaps.iter())
            .filter(|&&gap| gap >= BURST)
            .map(|&gap| millis(gap)),
    )
}

/// The drafts of `samples` together; `None` when none reported any.
pub fn drafts(samples: &[Sample]) -> Option<Drafts> {
    let reported: Vec<Drafts> = samples
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|sample| sample.server?.drafts)
        .collect();
    (!reported.is_empty()).then(|| reported.into_iter().sum())
}

/// Generation at one depth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct DepthResult {
    pub depth: Depth,
    /// The request that filled the context: a fresh prefill at this depth.
    pub fill: Sample,
    /// The repetitions on the filled context.
    pub samples: Vec<Sample>,
}

impl DepthResult {
    /// Tokens generated per second.
    pub fn generation(&self) -> Option<Summary> {
        Summary::of(self.samples.iter().filter_map(Sample::generation_speed))
    }

    /// Time to the first token, the context in the cache, in milliseconds.
    pub fn ttft(&self) -> Option<Summary> {
        Summary::of(self.samples.iter().map(Sample::ttft_ms))
    }
}

/// Prefills of a fresh prompt.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct PrefillResult {
    pub samples: Vec<Sample>,
}

impl PrefillResult {
    /// Prompt tokens evaluated per second.
    pub fn prefill(&self) -> Option<Summary> {
        Summary::of(self.samples.iter().filter_map(Sample::prefill_speed))
    }

    /// Time to the first token, in milliseconds.
    pub fn ttft(&self) -> Option<Summary> {
        Summary::of(self.samples.iter().map(Sample::ttft_ms))
    }
}

/// Sessions of the session scenario.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct SessionResult {
    /// Each session's turns, in order.
    pub sessions: Vec<Vec<Sample>>,
}

/// One turn across the sessions of a run.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct TurnSummary {
    /// From 1.
    pub turn: usize,
    /// Time to the first token, in milliseconds.
    pub ttft: Option<Summary>,
    /// Prompt tokens taken from the cache.
    pub cached: Option<Summary>,
    /// Prompt tokens evaluated.
    pub evaluated: Option<Summary>,
}

impl SessionResult {
    /// Each turn, summarised across the sessions that reached it.
    pub fn turns(&self) -> Vec<TurnSummary> {
        let turns = self.sessions.iter().map(Vec::len).max().unwrap_or(0);
        (0..turns)
            .map(|index| {
                let samples: Vec<&Sample> = self
                    .sessions
                    .iter()
                    .filter_map(|session| session.get(index))
                    .collect();
                let tokens = |count: fn(&Sample) -> Option<u64>| {
                    Summary::of(samples.iter().filter_map(|s| count(s)).map(|n| n as f64))
                };
                TurnSummary {
                    turn: index + 1,
                    ttft: Summary::of(samples.iter().map(|s| s.ttft_ms())),
                    cached: tokens(Sample::cached),
                    evaluated: tokens(Sample::evaluated),
                }
            })
            .collect()
    }

    /// Whether every session took more tokens from the cache at each turn
    /// than at the one before: the cache reused across turns. `false` when a
    /// turn has no server timings, or no session has two turns.
    pub fn cache_grows(&self) -> bool {
        let mut sessions = self.sessions.iter().filter(|s| s.len() > 1).peekable();
        sessions.peek().is_some()
            && sessions.all(|session| {
                let cached: Option<Vec<u64>> = session.iter().map(Sample::cached).collect();
                cached.is_some_and(|cached| cached.windows(2).all(|pair| pair[1] > pair[0]))
            })
    }
}

/// The same request, sent through the gateway and straight to llama-server.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct OverheadResult {
    pub gateway: Vec<Sample>,
    pub direct: Vec<Sample>,
}

/// What the gateway adds to a request, in milliseconds: the difference of the
/// medians.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Overhead {
    pub ttft_ms: f64,
    pub total_ms: f64,
}

impl OverheadResult {
    pub fn added(&self) -> Option<Overhead> {
        let median = |samples: &[Sample], measure: fn(&Sample) -> f64| {
            Summary::of(samples.iter().map(measure)).map(|summary| summary.median)
        };
        Some(Overhead {
            ttft_ms: median(&self.gateway, Sample::ttft_ms)?
                - median(&self.direct, Sample::ttft_ms)?,
            total_ms: median(&self.gateway, Sample::total_ms)?
                - median(&self.direct, Sample::total_ms)?,
        })
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn ms(value: u64) -> Duration {
        Duration::from_millis(value)
    }

    fn timings(cache_n: u64, prompt_n: u64, prompt_ms: f64) -> ServerTimings {
        ServerTimings {
            cache_n,
            prompt_n,
            prompt_ms,
            predicted_n: 100,
            predicted_ms: 2000.0,
            drafts: None,
        }
    }

    fn sample(ttft: u64, total: u64, server: Option<ServerTimings>) -> Sample {
        Sample {
            client: ClientTimings {
                ttft: ms(ttft),
                total: ms(total),
                gaps: Vec::new(),
            },
            server,
            usage: None,
        }
    }

    fn cached(cache_n: u64) -> Sample {
        sample(100, 1000, Some(timings(cache_n, 50, 25.0)))
    }

    #[test]
    fn scenarios_have_the_names_runs_use() {
        let names: Vec<&str> = Scenario::ALL.iter().map(|s| s.name()).collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["depth", "prefill", "session", "overhead"]);
        assert_eq!(Scenario::Overhead.to_string(), "overhead");
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_assistant_message_keeps_its_reasoning_when_there_is_some() {
        assert_eq!(Message::assistant("Hi.", "").reasoning, None);
        let reply = Message::assistant("Hi.", "Greet back.");
        assert_eq!(reply.reasoning.as_deref(), Some("Greet back."));
        assert_eq!(reply.role.name(), "assistant");
        assert_eq!(Message::system("Be brief.").role.name(), "system");
        assert_eq!(Message::user("Hello").role.name(), "user");
    }

    #[test]
    fn depths_reach_the_limit_of_the_context() {
        // The 27B: 98304 tokens of context, less the 512 to generate.
        let plan = Plan::new(98_304, REPEAT).unwrap();
        let depths: Vec<(u32, bool)> = plan.depths.iter().map(|d| (d.tokens, d.at_limit)).collect();
        assert_eq!(
            depths,
            [
                (8_192, false),
                (32_768, false),
                (65_536, false),
                (97_792, true)
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_depth_beyond_the_limit_is_skipped() {
        let depths: Vec<u32> = Plan::new(32_768, 1)
            .unwrap()
            .depths
            .iter()
            .map(|d| d.tokens)
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(depths, [8_192, 32_256]);
        // A standard depth equal to the limit is measured once, as the limit.
        let at_limit = Plan::new(65_536 + GENERATE, 1).unwrap().depths;
        assert_eq!(at_limit.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(
            at_limit[2],
            Depth {
                tokens: 65_536,
                at_limit: true
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_prefill_prompt_shrinks_to_a_small_context() {
        assert_eq!(Plan::new(98_304, 1).unwrap().prefill, PREFILL);
        assert_eq!(Plan::new(32_768, 1).unwrap().prefill, 32_767);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_default_plan_measures_each_scenario_five_times() {
        let plan = Plan::new(131_072, REPEAT).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(plan.repeat, 5);
        assert_eq!(plan.generate, GENERATE);
        assert_eq!(plan.session, SessionPlan::DEFAULT);
        assert_eq!(plan.overhead, OVERHEAD_REQUESTS);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_session_fills_its_last_prompt_and_reply() {
        // 8192 + 9 turns of 1024 + 10 replies of 512.
        assert_eq!(SessionPlan::DEFAULT.peak(512), 22_528);
        let one = SessionPlan {
            turns: 1,
            opening: 100,
            turn: 50,
        };
        assert_eq!(one.peak(10), 110);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_plan_that_does_not_fit_is_refused() {
        assert_eq!(Plan::new(98_304, 0), Err(PlanError::NoRepetition));
        assert_eq!(
            Plan::new(GENERATE, 1),
            Err(PlanError::ContextTooSmall {
                scenario: Scenario::Depth,
                needed: u64::from(GENERATE) + 1,
                ctx_size: GENERATE
            })
        );
        let error = Plan::new(16_384, 1).unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(
            error.to_string(),
            "the session scenario needs a context of 22528 tokens; the model's holds 16384"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn speeds_come_from_the_server_timings() {
        let timings = ServerTimings {
            cache_n: 0,
            prompt_n: 40_960,
            prompt_ms: 20_480.0,
            predicted_n: 512,
            predicted_ms: 10_240.0,
            drafts: None,
        };
        assert_eq!(timings.prefill_speed(), Some(2000.0));
        assert_eq!(timings.generation_speed(), Some(50.0));
        let sample = sample(20_500, 30_800, Some(timings));
        assert_eq!(sample.prefill_speed(), Some(2000.0));
        assert_eq!(sample.generation_speed(), Some(50.0));
        assert_eq!((sample.ttft_ms(), sample.total_ms()), (20_500.0, 30_800.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_speed_over_no_time_or_no_token_is_unknown() {
        let mut timings = timings(0, 0, 0.0);
        assert_eq!(timings.prefill_speed(), None);
        timings.predicted_n = 0;
        assert_eq!(timings.generation_speed(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn without_timings_the_client_measures_generation() {
        let mut sample = sample(500, 2500, None);
        assert_eq!(sample.generation_speed(), None);
        sample.usage = Some(Usage {
            prompt_tokens: 1000,
            completion_tokens: 101,
        });
        // 100 tokens after the first, in the 2 s after it.
        assert_eq!(sample.generation_speed(), Some(50.0));
        assert_eq!(sample.prefill_speed(), None);
        assert_eq!(sample.cached(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn drafts_add_up_across_requests() {
        let with = |proposed, accepted| {
            let mut timings = timings(0, 1, 1.0);
            timings.drafts = Some(Drafts { proposed, accepted });
            sample(1, 2, Some(timings))
        };
        let samples = [with(200, 120), cached(0), with(100, 63), sample(1, 2, None)];
        let total = drafts(&samples).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            total,
            Drafts {
                proposed: 300,
                accepted: 183
            }
        );
        assert_eq!(total.acceptance(), Some(0.61));
        assert_eq!(drafts(&[cached(0)]), None);
        assert_eq!(Drafts::default().acceptance(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn arrival_gaps_pool_every_request_and_skip_bursts() {
        let mut first = sample(1, 2, None);
        first.client.gaps = vec![ms(40), Duration::ZERO, Duration::from_micros(300), ms(44)];
        let mut second = sample(1, 2, None);
        second.client.gaps = vec![ms(36), ms(1)];
        let summary = arrival_gaps(&[first, second]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(summary.count, 4);
        assert_eq!((summary.min, summary.median), (1.0, 38.0));
        assert_eq!(arrival_gaps(&[cached(0)]), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_depth_summarises_its_repetitions_not_its_fill() {
        let mut fast = timings(8_191, 1, 1.0);
        fast.predicted_ms = 1000.0;
        let result = DepthResult {
            depth: Depth {
                tokens: 8_192,
                at_limit: false,
            },
            fill: sample(4000, 6000, Some(timings(0, 8_192, 4000.0))),
            samples: vec![
                sample(40, 2040, Some(timings(8_191, 1, 1.0))),
                sample(60, 1060, Some(fast)),
            ],
        };
        let generation = result.generation().unwrap();
        assert_eq!((generation.min, generation.max), (50.0, 100.0));
        assert_eq!(result.ttft().unwrap().mean, 50.0);
        assert_eq!(result.fill.prefill_speed(), Some(2048.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_prefill_summarises_its_speed_and_first_token() {
        let result = PrefillResult {
            samples: vec![
                sample(20_000, 20_010, Some(timings(0, 40_960, 20_480.0))),
                sample(21_000, 21_010, Some(timings(0, 40_960, 20_480.0))),
            ],
        };
        assert_eq!(result.prefill().unwrap().median, 2000.0);
        assert_eq!(result.ttft().unwrap().median, 20_500.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_session_is_summarised_turn_by_turn() {
        let result = SessionResult {
            sessions: vec![
                vec![cached(0), cached(9_000), cached(10_500)],
                vec![cached(0), cached(9_100)],
            ],
        };
        let turns = result.turns();
        assert_eq!(turns.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(turns[0].turn, 1);
        assert_eq!(turns[1].cached.unwrap().median, 9_050.0);
        assert_eq!(turns[1].evaluated.unwrap().median, 50.0);
        assert_eq!(turns[2].cached.unwrap().count, 1);
        assert_eq!(turns[2].ttft.unwrap().mean, 100.0);
        assert!(result.cache_grows());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_session_whose_cache_stalls_does_not_grow() {
        let stalled = SessionResult {
            sessions: vec![
                vec![cached(0), cached(9_000), cached(10_500)],
                vec![cached(0), cached(9_000), cached(9_000)],
            ],
        };
        assert!(!stalled.cache_grows());
        let untimed = SessionResult {
            sessions: vec![vec![cached(0), sample(1, 2, None)]],
        };
        assert!(!untimed.cache_grows());
        let single = SessionResult {
            sessions: vec![vec![cached(0)]],
        };
        assert!(!single.cache_grows());
        assert!(SessionResult { sessions: vec![] }.turns().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_overhead_is_the_difference_of_the_medians() {
        let result = OverheadResult {
            gateway: vec![sample(42, 300, None), sample(44, 304, None)],
            direct: vec![sample(41, 299, None), sample(41, 301, None)],
        };
        let added = result.added().unwrap();
        assert_eq!((added.ttft_ms, added.total_ms), (2.0, 2.0));
        let one_way = OverheadResult {
            gateway: vec![sample(42, 300, None)],
            direct: Vec::new(),
        };
        assert_eq!(one_way.added(), None);
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/build.rs">
//! Native builds: what was compiled, from which sources, with which
//! toolchain.
//!
//! A build is installed once and never modified; a project's builds sit side
//! by side, so going back to an older one needs no compilation.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fmt;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use super::machine::{HostCompiler, Version};
use super::recipe::Recipe;
use super::timestamp::Timestamp;

/// The CMake variable holding the CUDA architectures, `;`-separated.
pub const CUDA_ARCHITECTURES: &str = "CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES";
/// The architecture native builds compile for: sm_120 with its
/// architecture-specific instructions (the `a`), such as the FP4 tensor
/// cores, as device code only (`-real`).
pub const CUDA_ARCHITECTURE: &str = "120a-real";
/// Hexadecimal digits of a commit kept in a build id.
const SHORT_COMMIT: usize = 10;
const MAX_ID_LEN: usize = 64;
/// The name of a project's link to its current build.
pub const CURRENT: &str = "current";

/// A build's name, unique within its project: the release tag it was built
/// from, such as `b6512`, or the short commit otherwise. It names the
/// build's directory, so it is a safe file name.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct BuildId(String);

/// Why a text is not a build id.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error(
    "`{0}` is not a build id: 1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or a digit, and not `current`"
)]
pub struct InvalidBuildId(pub String);

impl BuildId {
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Self, InvalidBuildId> {
        let valid = text.len() <= MAX_ID_LEN
            && text
                .chars()
                .next()
                .is_some_and(|first| first.is_ascii_alphanumeric())
            && text
                .chars()
                .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(c, '.' | '_' | '-'))
            && text != CURRENT;
        if valid {
            Ok(Self(text.to_owned()))
        } else {
            Err(InvalidBuildId(text.to_owned()))
        }
    }

    /// The id of a build made from a commit that is not a release.
    pub fn from_commit(commit: &CommitId) -> Self {
        Self(commit.as_str()[..SHORT_COMMIT].to_owned())
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        &self.0
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for BuildId {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

/// A full commit hash: 40 lower-case hexadecimal digits, or 64 in a
/// SHA-256 repository.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct CommitId(String);

/// Why a text is not a full commit hash.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("`{0}` is not a full commit hash")]
pub struct InvalidCommitId(pub String);

impl CommitId {
    /// Accepts upper-case digits and stores them in lower case, as git
    /// prints them.
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Self, InvalidCommitId> {
        let valid = matches!(text.len(), 40 | 64) && text.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit());
        if valid {
            Ok(Self(text.to_ascii_lowercase()))
        } else {
            Err(InvalidCommitId(text.to_owned()))
        }
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        &self.0
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for CommitId {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

/// An installed build and how it was made.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Build {
    /// The recipe's project, such as `llama.cpp`.
    pub project: String,
    pub id: BuildId,
    /// The commit the sources were checked out at.
    pub commit: CommitId,
    /// The CUDA toolkit `nvcc` belonged to: the build loads that major
    /// version's CUDA libraries at run time.
    pub toolkit: Version,
    /// The compiler passed to CMake for C, C++ and as the CUDA host compiler.
    pub host_compiler: HostCompiler,
    /// Every `-D` option passed to CMake, by variable name.
    pub cmake_flags: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub built_at: Timestamp,
}

impl Build {
    /// The CUDA architectures compiled in, such as `120a-real`.
    pub fn cuda_architectures(&self) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.cmake_flags
            .get(CUDA_ARCHITECTURES)
            .map(|value| value.split(';').filter(|arch| !arch.is_empty()).collect())
            .unwrap_or_default()
    }
}

/// The builds `prune` removes from one project's: every build but the
/// `keep` newest and the current one.
pub fn to_prune<'a>(builds: &'a [Build], current: Option<&BuildId>, keep: usize) -> Vec<&'a Build> {
    let mut newest_first: Vec<&Build> = builds.iter().collect();
    newest_first.sort_by(|a, b| b.built_at.cmp(&a.built_at).then_with(|| b.id.cmp(&a.id)));
    newest_first
        .into_iter()
        .skip(keep)
        .filter(|build| Some(&build.id) != current)
        .collect()
}

/// The compilers a build runs, as the build gate settled them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Toolchain {
    nvcc: PathBuf,
    toolkit: Version,
    compiler: HostCompiler,
    c_compiler: PathBuf,
}

/// A toolchain path CMake cannot be given.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("{} is not a UTF-8 path, which a CMake option needs", .0.display())]
pub struct NonUtf8Path(pub PathBuf);

impl Toolchain {
    /// `compiler` compiles C++ and is `nvcc`'s host compiler; `c_compiler`
    /// is the C compiler of the same GCC.
    pub fn new(
        nvcc: PathBuf,
        toolkit: Version,
        compiler: HostCompiler,
        c_compiler: PathBuf,
    ) -> Result<Self, NonUtf8Path> {
        for path in [&nvcc, &compiler.path, &c_compiler] {
            if path.to_str().is_none() {
                return Err(NonUtf8Path(path.clone()));
            }
        }
        Ok(Self {
            nvcc,
            toolkit,
            compiler,
            c_compiler,
        })
    }

    pub fn nvcc(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.nvcc
    }

    pub fn toolkit(&self) -> Version {
        self.toolkit
    }

    pub fn compiler(&self) -> &HostCompiler {
        &self.compiler
    }

    pub fn c_compiler(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.c_compiler
    }
}

/// The C compiler installed with a GNU C++ compiler: `gcc` beside `g++`,
/// `gcc-15` beside `g++-15`.
pub fn c_compiler_beside(cxx: &Path) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let suffix = cxx.file_name()?.to_str()?.strip_prefix("g++")?;
    Some(cxx.with_file_name(format!("gcc{suffix}")))
}

/// Every `-D` option of a build: the recipe's own, then the builder's, which
/// take precedence. Every compiler is named, so CMake finds neither a
/// compiler cache's wrapper nor another toolkit on the `PATH`; ggml still
/// launches the compilers through ccache when it is installed
/// (`GGML_CCACHE`, on by default), which only speeds up a rebuild.
pub fn cmake_flags(recipe: &Recipe, toolchain: &Toolchain) -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
    // `Toolchain::new` refused paths that are not UTF-8.
    let path = |path: &Path| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    let mut flags: BTreeMap<String, String> = recipe
        .cmake_flags
        .iter()
        .map(|(name, value)| ((*name).to_owned(), (*value).to_owned()))
        .collect();
    let builder = [
        ("CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE", "Release".to_owned()),
        // llama.cpp and ggml inside the binary: nothing is loaded from the
        // build tree, which is removed after the build.
        ("BUILD_SHARED_LIBS", "OFF".to_owned()),
        ("GGML_CUDA", "ON".to_owned()),
        // CPU code tuned for this machine, the only one the build runs on.
        ("GGML_NATIVE", "ON".to_owned()),
        (CUDA_ARCHITECTURES, CUDA_ARCHITECTURE.to_owned()),
        ("CMAKE_CUDA_COMPILER", path(&toolchain.nvcc)),
        ("CMAKE_C_COMPILER", path(&toolchain.c_compiler)),
        ("CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER", path(&toolchain.compiler.path)),
        ("CMAKE_CUDA_HOST_COMPILER", path(&toolchain.compiler.path)),
    ];
    for (name, value) in builder {
        flags.insert(name.to_owned(), value);
    }
    flags
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::ffi::OsStr;
    use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt;

    use super::*;
    use crate::domain::recipe::LLAMA_CPP;

    const COMMIT: &str = "79e2e74eb11022c1ba2e438df7f0ca2d4c10f8b6";

    #[test]
    fn release_tags_and_short_commits_are_build_ids() {
        for id in [
            "b11538",
            "79e2e74eb1",
            "v1.8.2",
            "master-480-ce3e7b1",
            "a_b",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(BuildId::parse(id).unwrap().as_str(), id);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn unsafe_names_are_not_build_ids() {
        let too_long = "b".repeat(MAX_ID_LEN + 1);
        for id in [
            "",
            ".",
            "..",
            ".hidden",
            "-rf",
            "a/b",
            "a b",
            "été",
            "current",
            too_long.as_str(),
        ] {
            assert_eq!(
                BuildId::parse(id),
                Err(InvalidBuildId(id.to_owned())),
                "{id}"
            );
        }
        assert!(BuildId::parse(&"b".repeat(MAX_ID_LEN)).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_commit_build_id_keeps_ten_digits() {
        let commit = CommitId::parse(COMMIT).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(BuildId::from_commit(&commit).as_str(), "79e2e74eb1");
    }

    #[test]
    fn commit_ids_are_full_hexadecimal_hashes() {
        assert_eq!(
            CommitId::parse(&COMMIT.to_ascii_uppercase())
                .unwrap()
                .as_str(),
            COMMIT
        );
        assert!(CommitId::parse(&"a".repeat(64)).is_ok());
        for text in [
            "",
            "79e2e74",
            &COMMIT[..39],
            &format!("{COMMIT}0"),
            &COMMIT.replace('e', "g"),
        ] {
            assert!(CommitId::parse(text).is_err(), "{text}");
        }
    }

    fn gxx(path: &str, version: Version) -> HostCompiler {
        HostCompiler {
            command: Path::new(path)
                .file_name()
                .unwrap()
                .to_string_lossy()
                .into_owned(),
            path: PathBuf::from(path),
            version,
        }
    }

    fn toolchain(cxx: &str) -> Toolchain {
        Toolchain::new(
            PathBuf::from("/usr/local/cuda-13.4/bin/nvcc"),
            Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            gxx(cxx, Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
            c_compiler_beside(Path::new(cxx)).unwrap(),
        )
        .unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_c_compiler_sits_beside_the_cxx_compiler() {
        assert_eq!(
            c_compiler_beside(Path::new("/usr/bin/g++")),
            Some(PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/gcc"))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            c_compiler_beside(Path::new("/usr/bin/g++-15")),
            Some(PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/gcc-15"))
        );
        assert_eq!(c_compiler_beside(Path::new("/usr/bin/clang++")), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_toolchain_path_must_be_utf8() {
        let odd = PathBuf::from(OsStr::from_bytes(b"/opt/cuda-\xff/bin/nvcc"));
        let error = Toolchain::new(
            odd.clone(),
            Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            gxx("/usr/bin/g++", Version::new(16, 2, 1)),
            PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/gcc"),
        )
        .unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(error, NonUtf8Path(odd));
    }

    #[test]
    fn cmake_flags_name_every_compiler_and_the_blackwell_architecture() {
        let flags = cmake_flags(&LLAMA_CPP, &toolchain("/usr/bin/g++-15"));
        let expected = [
            ("BUILD_SHARED_LIBS", "OFF"),
            ("CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE", "Release"),
            ("CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES", "120a-real"),
            ("CMAKE_CUDA_COMPILER", "/usr/local/cuda-13.4/bin/nvcc"),
            ("CMAKE_CUDA_HOST_COMPILER", "/usr/bin/g++-15"),
            ("CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER", "/usr/bin/g++-15"),
            ("CMAKE_C_COMPILER", "/usr/bin/gcc-15"),
            ("GGML_CUDA", "ON"),
            ("GGML_NATIVE", "ON"),
            ("LLAMA_BUILD_APP", "OFF"),
            ("LLAMA_BUILD_EXAMPLES", "OFF"),
            ("LLAMA_BUILD_SERVER", "ON"),
            ("LLAMA_BUILD_TESTS", "OFF"),
            ("LLAMA_BUILD_TOOLS", "ON"),
        ];
        let flags: Vec<(&str, &str)> = flags
            .iter()
            .map(|(name, value)| (name.as_str(), value.as_str()))
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(flags, expected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_builder_options_win_over_the_recipe() {
        let recipe = Recipe {
            cmake_flags: &[("GGML_CUDA", "OFF"), ("WHISPER_BUILD_TESTS", "OFF")],
            ..LLAMA_CPP
        };
        let flags = cmake_flags(&recipe, &toolchain("/usr/bin/g++"));
        assert_eq!(flags["GGML_CUDA"], "ON");
        assert_eq!(flags["WHISPER_BUILD_TESTS"], "OFF");
    }

    fn built(id: &str, at: i64) -> Build {
        Build {
            project: "llama.cpp".into(),
            id: BuildId::parse(id).unwrap(),
            commit: CommitId::parse(COMMIT).unwrap(),
            toolkit: Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            host_compiler: gxx("/usr/bin/g++", Version::new(16, 2, 1)),
            cmake_flags: BTreeMap::new(),
            built_at: Timestamp::from_unix(at).unwrap(),
        }
    }

    fn ids(builds: Vec<&Build>) -> Vec<&str> {
        builds.iter().map(|build| build.id.as_str()).collect()
    }

    #[test]
    fn prune_keeps_the_newest_builds() {
        let builds = [
            built("b1", 10),
            built("b3", 30),
            built("b2", 20),
            built("b4", 40),
        ];
        assert_eq!(ids(to_prune(&builds, None, 2)), ["b2", "b1"]);
        assert_eq!(ids(to_prune(&builds, None, 0)), ["b4", "b3", "b2", "b1"]);
        assert!(to_prune(&builds, None, 4).is_empty());
        assert!(to_prune(&builds, None, 9).is_empty());
        assert!(to_prune(&[], None, 0).is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn prune_never_removes_the_current_build() {
        let builds = [built("b1", 10), built("b2", 20), built("b3", 30)];
        let current = BuildId::parse("b1").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(ids(to_prune(&builds, Some(&current), 1)), ["b2"]);
        assert!(to_prune(&builds[..1], Some(&current), 0).is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn builds_of_the_same_second_prune_in_id_order() {
        let builds = [built("aaaaaaaaaa", 10), built("bbbbbbbbbb", 10)];
        assert_eq!(ids(to_prune(&builds, None, 1)), ["aaaaaaaaaa"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn cuda_architectures_come_from_the_cmake_flags() {
        let mut build = Build {
            project: "llama.cpp".into(),
            id: BuildId::parse("b11538").unwrap(),
            commit: CommitId::parse(COMMIT).unwrap(),
            toolkit: Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            host_compiler: HostCompiler {
                command: "g++".into(),
                path: PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/g++"),
                version: Version::new(16, 2, 1),
            },
            cmake_flags: BTreeMap::from([(CUDA_ARCHITECTURES.into(), "120a-real".into())]),
            built_at: Timestamp::from_unix(0).unwrap(),
        };
        assert_eq!(build.cuda_architectures(), ["120a-real"]);
        build
            .cmake_flags
            .insert(CUDA_ARCHITECTURES.into(), "89-real;120a-real;".into());
        assert_eq!(build.cuda_architectures(), ["89-real", "120a-real"]);
        build.cmake_flags.clear();
        assert!(build.cuda_architectures().is_empty());
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/diagnostics.rs">
//! Diagnostics: the checks `doctor` reports, grouped in sections.

/// Outcome of one check.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Status {
    /// The requirement is met.
    Ok,
    /// Something works but deserves attention; it does not fail the run.
    Warn,
    /// A requirement is not met; the run fails.
    Fail,
    /// The check could not apply, because something it depends on is absent.
    Skip,
}

impl Status {
    /// Lower-case name, as printed and serialized.
    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Status::Ok => "ok",
            Status::Warn => "warn",
            Status::Fail => "fail",
            Status::Skip => "skip",
        }
    }
}

/// One diagnosed item: what was checked, what was found, and how to fix it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Check {
    pub name: String,
    pub status: Status,
    pub detail: String,
    /// Commands or instructions that fix the problem; one step per line.
    pub remedy: Option<String>,
}

impl Check {
    pub fn ok(name: impl Into<String>, detail: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self::new(name, Status::Ok, detail, None)
    }

    pub fn skip(name: impl Into<String>, detail: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self::new(name, Status::Skip, detail, None)
    }

    pub fn warn(
        name: impl Into<String>,
        detail: impl Into<String>,
        remedy: impl Into<Option<String>>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self::new(name, Status::Warn, detail, remedy.into())
    }

    pub fn fail(
        name: impl Into<String>,
        detail: impl Into<String>,
        remedy: impl Into<Option<String>>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self::new(name, Status::Fail, detail, remedy.into())
    }

    fn new(
        name: impl Into<String>,
        status: Status,
        detail: impl Into<String>,
        remedy: Option<String>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            name: name.into(),
            status,
            detail: detail.into(),
            remedy,
        }
    }
}

/// A named group of checks, such as "Toolchain".
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Section {
    pub name: String,
    pub checks: Vec<Check>,
}

impl Section {
    pub fn new(name: impl Into<String>, checks: Vec<Check>) -> Self {
        Self {
            name: name.into(),
            checks,
        }
    }
}

/// Number of checks per status.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Summary {
    pub ok: usize,
    pub warn: usize,
    pub fail: usize,
    pub skip: usize,
}

/// Every section of a diagnosis.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Report {
    pub sections: Vec<Section>,
}

impl Report {
    pub fn checks(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Check> {
        self.sections.iter().flat_map(|section| &section.checks)
    }

    pub fn summary(&self) -> Summary {
        let mut summary = Summary::default();
        for check in self.checks() {
            match check.status {
                Status::Ok => summary.ok += 1,
                Status::Warn => summary.warn += 1,
                Status::Fail => summary.fail += 1,
                Status::Skip => summary.skip += 1,
            }
        }
        summary
    }

    /// True when at least one check failed: the run exits with an error.
    pub fn has_failures(&self) -> bool {
        self.checks().any(|check| check.status == Status::Fail)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn report(statuses: &[&[Status]]) -> Report {
        Report {
            sections: statuses
                .iter()
                .enumerate()
                .map(|(i, section)| {
                    let checks = section
                        .iter()
                        .map(|&status| Check::new("check", status, "detail", None))
                        .collect();
                    Section::new(format!("section {i}"), checks)
                })
                .collect(),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_counts_every_status_across_sections() {
        let report = report(&[
            &[Status::Ok, Status::Ok, Status::Warn],
            &[Status::Fail, Status::Skip, Status::Ok],
        ]);
        assert_eq!(
            report.summary(),
            Summary {
                ok: 3,
                warn: 1,
                fail: 1,
                skip: 1
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_report_has_no_failure() {
        let report = Report::default();
        assert_eq!(report.summary(), Summary::default());
        assert!(!report.has_failures());
    }

    #[test]
    fn warnings_and_skips_do_not_fail_the_run() {
        assert!(!report(&[&[Status::Ok, Status::Warn, Status::Skip]]).has_failures());
    }

    #[test]
    fn one_failure_fails_the_run() {
        assert!(report(&[&[Status::Ok], &[Status::Warn, Status::Fail]]).has_failures());
    }

    #[test]
    fn constructors_set_status_and_remedy() {
        let check = Check::fail(
            "CMake",
            "not found",
            Some("sudo dnf install cmake".to_owned()),
        );
        assert_eq!(check.status, Status::Fail);
        assert_eq!(check.remedy.as_deref(), Some("sudo dnf install cmake"));
        assert_eq!(Check::ok("Git", "2.56.0").remedy, None);
        assert_eq!(Check::skip("CDI", "no nvidia-ctk").status, Status::Skip);
        assert_eq!(Check::warn("Podman", "missing", None).status, Status::Warn);
    }

    #[test]
    fn status_names_are_lower_case() {
        let names: Vec<_> = [Status::Ok, Status::Warn, Status::Fail, Status::Skip]
            .into_iter()
            .map(Status::as_str)
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["ok", "warn", "fail", "skip"]);
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/gateway_config.rs">
//! The gateway's configuration: what llama-swap runs, generated from the
//! inventory.
//!
//! Each model becomes one llama-swap entry whose command starts its backend
//! on the port llama-swap assigns, from a native build or in a container.
//! The file is written in the dialect
//! llama-swap reads: a command is split like a POSIX shell line once the
//! lines starting with `#` are dropped, and every `${…}` in any value is a
//! macro. Nothing escapes a `$`, so a value containing `${` is refused.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fmt::Write as _;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use super::inventory::{BackendKind, FlagValue, Flags, Inventory, Model};

/// The macro llama-swap replaces with the port it assigns to a model.
pub const PORT: &str = "${PORT}";
/// The address a backend listens on: loopback, behind the gateway.
const HOST: &str = "127.0.0.1";
/// llama-server's readiness endpoint: 503 while the model loads, then 200.
const HEALTH: &str = "/health";
/// The file a llama-server model cannot do without, put first.
const MODEL_FILE: &str = "model";
/// What a model's container is named after its ID, which the inventory
/// keeps to the characters a container name takes.
pub const CONTAINER_PREFIX: &str = "ai-ctl-";
/// The CDI device that gives a container every GPU.
pub const GPU_DEVICE: &str = "nvidia.com/gpu=all";

/// What llama-swap writes to its standard output, which `up` sends to the
/// gateway's log.
const LOG_TO_STDOUT: &str = "# The gateway's log keeps the models' output too: llama-swap holds it in\n\
                             # memory only otherwise, and loses it when it stops.\n\
                             logToStdout: \"proxy,upstream\"\n\n";

/// Why llama-server entries open a new connection for every request,
/// written beside the setting. llama-server advertises keep-alive on a
/// streamed response, then closes the connection; llama-swap reuses it for
/// the next request, which fails. Drop the setting once the builds in use
/// include a fix for llama.cpp #29680.
const KEEP_ALIVE_REASON: &[&str] = &[
    "llama-server advertises keep-alive on a streamed response, then closes the",
    "connection: a request sent right after reuses it and fails with 502",
    "\"proxy error: EOF\" (llama-swap #1205). Needed until llama.cpp fixes #29680.",
];

/// Where a backend's program comes from on this machine.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Runtime {
    /// The binary of an installed native build.
    Native(PathBuf),
    /// A container image whose entrypoint is the backend's server, run with
    /// podman.
    Image(String),
}

/// A command line, its words grouped by line: the program, then one option
/// with its value per line. The grouping only makes the file readable;
/// llama-swap joins the lines.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CommandLine(Vec<Vec<String>>);

impl CommandLine {
    pub fn lines(&self) -> &[Vec<String>] {
        &self.0
    }

    /// The words, as the program receives them once `${PORT}` is replaced.
    pub fn words(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.0.iter().flatten().map(String::as_str)
    }
}

/// One model as llama-swap serves it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ModelEntry {
    /// The ID clients request.
    pub id: String,
    /// The command that starts the backend on `${PORT}`.
    pub cmd: CommandLine,
    /// The command that stops it; llama-swap sends the command SIGTERM when
    /// absent.
    pub cmd_stop: Option<Vec<String>>,
    /// Where llama-swap forwards the model's requests.
    pub proxy: String,
    /// The path that answers 200 once the backend is ready.
    pub check_endpoint: &'static str,
    /// Seconds without a request before llama-swap stops the backend; never
    /// when absent.
    pub ttl: Option<u32>,
    /// A new connection to the backend for every request; see
    /// [`KEEP_ALIVE_REASON`].
    pub disable_keep_alives: bool,
}

/// The models llama-swap may run together, from their VRAM budgets.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Matrix {
    /// The GPU memory processes can allocate, in MiB.
    pub gpu_mib: u64,
    /// What models running together leave free of it, in MiB.
    pub margin_mib: u32,
    /// Every largest combination of models whose budgets fit together, and
    /// each model too large to share the GPU, alone; ordered by the
    /// inventory's order of their models.
    pub sets: Vec<Vec<String>>,
    /// What evicting each model costs: its VRAM budget, since loading it
    /// again reads back about as many bytes.
    pub evict_costs: Vec<(String, u32)>,
}

/// The whole configuration, ready to be written.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct GatewayConfig {
    /// One entry per model, in the inventory's order.
    pub models: Vec<ModelEntry>,
    pub matrix: Matrix,
    /// The models loaded when the gateway starts.
    pub preload: Vec<String>,
}

/// Why the inventory cannot be written as a configuration.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ConfigError {
    #[error("backend `{0}` has no program resolved for it")]
    Unresolved(String),
    #[error(
        "model `{model}`: `{text}` contains `${{`, which llama-swap reads as a macro and cannot escape"
    )]
    Macro { model: String, text: String },
    #[error("model `{model}`: {text:?} contains a control character")]
    ControlCharacter { model: String, text: String },
    #[error("model `{model}`: path {path:?} is not valid UTF-8")]
    NonUtf8Path { model: String, path: PathBuf },
    #[error(
        "model `{model}` runs in a container, which cannot mount {path:?}: a volume path cannot contain `:`"
    )]
    VolumePath { model: String, path: PathBuf },
}

/// Generates the configuration of `inventory` for a GPU of `gpu_mib` usable
/// MiB, each backend running the program `runtimes` gives for its ID. Only
/// the backends a model uses need one.
pub fn generate(
    inventory: &Inventory,
    runtimes: &BTreeMap<String, Runtime>,
    gpu_mib: u64,
) -> Result<GatewayConfig, ConfigError> {
    let models = inventory
        .models()
        .iter()
        .map(|model| entry(inventory, model, runtimes))
        .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
    let preload = inventory
        .models()
        .iter()
        .filter(|model| model.preload)
        .map(|model| model.id.clone())
        .collect();
    Ok(GatewayConfig {
        models,
        matrix: matrix(inventory, gpu_mib),
        preload,
    })
}

fn matrix(inventory: &Inventory, gpu_mib: u64) -> Matrix {
    let margin_mib = inventory.gateway().vram_margin_mib;
    let budget = gpu_mib.saturating_sub(u64::from(margin_mib));
    let models = inventory.models();
    let sizes: Vec<u64> = models.iter().map(|m| u64::from(m.vram_mib)).collect();

    let mut sets = Vec::new();
    largest_sets(&sizes, budget, 0, &mut Vec::new(), &mut sets);
    sets.extend(
        (0..sizes.len())
            .filter(|&i| sizes[i] > budget)
            .map(|i| vec![i]),
    );
    sets.sort();

    let id = |i: usize| models[i].id.clone();
    Matrix {
        gpu_mib,
        margin_mib,
        sets: sets
