            .into_iter()
            .map(|set| set.into_iter().map(id).collect())
            .collect(),
        evict_costs: models
            .iter()
            .map(|model| (model.id.clone(), model.vram_mib))
            .collect(),
    }
}

/// Adds to `sets` every largest combination of models, by index, that fits
/// in `budget` and extends `chosen` with models from `next` on.
fn largest_sets(
    sizes: &[u64],
    budget: u64,
    next: usize,
    chosen: &mut Vec<usize>,
    sets: &mut Vec<Vec<usize>>,
) {
    let used: u64 = chosen.iter().map(|&i| sizes[i]).sum();
    let rest: u64 = sizes[next..].iter().sum();
    if used + rest <= budget {
        // Every model left fits: taking them all is the only largest set.
        let set: Vec<usize> = chosen.iter().copied().chain(next..sizes.len()).collect();
        if !set.is_empty() && is_largest(sizes, budget, &set) {
            sets.push(set);
        }
        return;
    }
    for i in next..sizes.len() {
        if used + sizes[i] <= budget {
            chosen.push(i);
            largest_sets(sizes, budget, i + 1, chosen, sets);
            chosen.pop();
        }
    }
    if !chosen.is_empty() && is_largest(sizes, budget, chosen) {
        sets.push(chosen.clone());
    }
}

/// No model outside `set` fits beside it.
fn is_largest(sizes: &[u64], budget: u64, set: &[usize]) -> bool {
    let used: u64 = set.iter().map(|&i| sizes[i]).sum();
    (0..sizes.len()).all(|i| set.contains(&i) || used + sizes[i] > budget)
}

fn entry(
    inventory: &Inventory,
    model: &Model,
    runtimes: &BTreeMap<String, Runtime>,
) -> Result<ModelEntry, ConfigError> {
    let unresolved = || ConfigError::Unresolved(model.backend.clone());
    let backend = inventory.backend(&model.backend).ok_or_else(unresolved)?;
    let runtime = runtimes.get(&backend.id).ok_or_else(unresolved)?;
    let text = Text { model: &model.id };
    let id = text.checked(&model.id)?;
    let (mut lines, cmd_stop) = match runtime {
        Runtime::Native(binary) => (vec![vec![text.path(binary)?]], None),
        Runtime::Image(image) => {
            let name = format!("{CONTAINER_PREFIX}{}", model.id);
            let lines = container(inventory.models_dir(), image, &name, text)?;
            (
                lines,
                Some(vec!["podman".to_owned(), "stop".to_owned(), name]),
            )
        }
    };
    lines.extend(match backend.kind {
        BackendKind::LlamaServer => llama_server(inventory, model, text)?,
    });
    Ok(ModelEntry {
        id,
        cmd: CommandLine(lines),
        cmd_stop,
        proxy: format!("http://{HOST}:{PORT}"),
        check_endpoint: HEALTH,
        ttl: model.ttl,
        disable_keep_alives: true,
    })
}

/// `podman run` of `image`, whose entrypoint is the server, in the
/// foreground so that llama-swap holds its process.
///
/// The container is removed when it stops, and replaces one of the same name
/// a crash left behind. It never pulls: a pull of several GB would outlast
/// llama-swap's health check, so `up` pulls a missing image first. It shares
/// the host's network, so the server binds loopback as a native build does,
/// with no forwarder on the path the bench measures; the image's own health
/// check, on port 8080, would probe the gateway, and is turned off. It runs
/// outside SELinux separation, so the models directory needs no relabelling;
/// it is mounted read-only at its host path, which keeps the models' paths.
fn container(
    models_dir: &Path,
    image: &str,
    name: &str,
    text: Text,
) -> Result<Vec<Vec<String>>, ConfigError> {
    let dir = text.path(models_dir)?;
    if dir.contains(':') {
        return Err(ConfigError::VolumePath {
            model: text.model.to_owned(),
            path: models_dir.to_owned(),
        });
    }
    let words = |words: &[&str]| words.iter().map(|&word| word.to_owned()).collect();
    Ok(vec![
        words(&["podman", "run"]),
        words(&["--rm"]),
        words(&["--replace"]),
        words(&["--name", name]),
        words(&["--pull=never"]),
        words(&["--no-healthcheck"]),
        words(&["--network", "host"]),
        words(&["--security-opt", "label=disable"]),
        words(&["--device", GPU_DEVICE]),
        words(&["--volume", &format!("{dir}:{dir}:ro")]),
        vec![text.checked(image)?],
    ])
}

/// llama-server's arguments: loopback and `${PORT}`, named after the model,
/// with `/metrics`, the model's files, its context size, and the kind's
/// default flags overlaid with its own.
fn llama_server(
    inventory: &Inventory,
    model: &Model,
    text: Text,
) -> Result<Vec<Vec<String>>, ConfigError> {
    let mut lines = vec![
        vec!["--host".to_owned(), HOST.to_owned()],
        vec!["--port".to_owned(), PORT.to_owned()],
        vec!["--alias".to_owned(), text.checked(&model.id)?],
        vec!["--metrics".to_owned()],
    ];

    let dir = inventory.model_dir(model);
    let (first, others): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) = model
        .files
        .iter()
        .partition(|(flag, _)| flag.as_str() == MODEL_FILE);
    for (flag, file) in first.into_iter().chain(others) {
        lines.push(vec![text.option(flag)?, text.path(&dir.join(file))?]);
    }
    if let Some(ctx_size) = model.ctx_size {
        lines.push(vec!["--ctx-size".to_owned(), ctx_size.to_string()]);
    }

    let mut flags: Flags = inventory
        .defaults(BackendKind::LlamaServer)
        .cloned()
        .unwrap_or_default();
    flags.extend(model.flags.clone());
    for (flag, value) in &flags {
        let value = match value {
            FlagValue::Bool(false) => continue,
            FlagValue::Bool(true) => None,
            FlagValue::Int(number) => Some(number.to_string()),
            FlagValue::Float(number) => Some(number.to_string()),
            FlagValue::Text(value) => Some(text.checked(value)?),
        };
        lines.push(std::iter::once(text.option(flag)?).chain(value).collect());
    }
    Ok(lines)
}

/// Checks the text an inventory puts in the configuration, on behalf of one
/// model.
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
struct Text<'a> {
    model: &'a str,
}

impl Text<'_> {
    fn checked(self, text: &str) -> Result<String, ConfigError> {
        if text.contains("${") {
            return Err(ConfigError::Macro {
                model: self.model.to_owned(),
                text: text.to_owned(),
            });
        }
        if text.chars().any(char::is_control) {
            return Err(ConfigError::ControlCharacter {
                model: self.model.to_owned(),
                text: text.to_owned(),
            });
        }
        Ok(text.to_owned())
    }

    fn option(self, flag: &str) -> Result<String, ConfigError> {
        self.checked(&format!("--{flag}"))
    }

    fn path(self, path: &Path) -> Result<String, ConfigError> {
        let text = path.to_str().ok_or_else(|| ConfigError::NonUtf8Path {
            model: self.model.to_owned(),
            path: path.to_owned(),
        })?;
        self.checked(text)
    }
}

impl GatewayConfig {
    /// The configuration as llama-swap reads it.
    pub fn to_yaml(&self) -> String {
        let mut out = String::from(
            "# llama-swap's configuration, generated by ai-ctl from its inventory.\n\
             # `ai-ctl up` writes it again: change the inventory instead.\n\n",
        );
        out.push_str(LOG_TO_STDOUT);
        if self.models.is_empty() {
            out.push_str("models: {}\n");
        } else {
            out.push_str("models:\n");
        }
        for model in &self.models {
            write_model(&mut out, model);
        }
        if !self.matrix.sets.is_empty() {
            write_matrix(&mut out, &self.matrix);
        }
        if !self.preload.is_empty() {
            out.push_str("\nhooks:\n  on_startup:\n    preload:\n");
            for id in &self.preload {
                let _ = writeln!(out, "      - {}", quoted(id));
            }
        }
        out
    }
}

fn write_model(out: &mut String, model: &ModelEntry) {
    let _ = writeln!(out, "  {}:", quoted(&model.id));
    out.push_str("    cmd: |\n");
    for line in model.cmd.lines() {
        let words: Vec<String> = line.iter().map(|word| shell_word(word)).collect();
        let _ = writeln!(out, "      {}", words.join(" "));
    }
    if let Some(cmd_stop) = &model.cmd_stop {
        let words: Vec<String> = cmd_stop.iter().map(|word| shell_word(word)).collect();
        let _ = writeln!(out, "    cmdStop: {}", quoted(&words.join(" ")));
    }
    let _ = writeln!(out, "    proxy: {}", quoted(&model.proxy));
    let _ = writeln!(out, "    checkEndpoint: {}", quoted(model.check_endpoint));
    if let Some(ttl) = model.ttl {
        let _ = writeln!(out, "    ttl: {ttl}");
    }
    if model.disable_keep_alives {
        for line in KEEP_ALIVE_REASON {
            let _ = writeln!(out, "    # {line}");
        }
        out.push_str("    disableKeepAlives: true\n");
    }
}

/// The `matrix` swap engine, with every model's eviction cost and its sets.
fn write_matrix(out: &mut String, matrix: &Matrix) {
    let (gpu, margin) = (matrix.gpu_mib, matrix.margin_mib);
    let budget = gpu.saturating_sub(u64::from(margin));
    out.push_str("\nrouting:\n  router:\n    use: \"matrix\"\n    settings:\n");
    let _ = writeln!(
        out,
        "      # Models run side by side only when their VRAM budgets fit in {budget} MiB,"
    );
    let _ = writeln!(
        out,
        "      # the GPU's {gpu} usable MiB less the inventory's margin of {margin} MiB."
    );
    out.push_str("      # Evicting a model costs its budget, which loading it again reads back.\n");
    out.push_str("      matrix:\n        evict_costs:\n");
    for (id, cost) in &matrix.evict_costs {
        let _ = writeln!(out, "          {}: {cost}", quoted(id));
    }
    out.push_str("        sets:\n");
    for (n, set) in matrix.sets.iter().enumerate() {
        let name = format!("fit-{}", n + 1);
        let _ = writeln!(
            out,
            "          {}: {}",
            quoted(&name),
            quoted(&set.join(" & "))
        );
    }
}

/// A YAML double-quoted scalar. The text holds no control character.
fn quoted(text: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len() + 2);
    out.push('"');
    for c in text.chars() {
        match c {
            '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
            '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
            c => out.push(c),
        }
    }
    out.push('"');
    out
}

/// A word as llama-swap's POSIX splitter reads it back: bare when made of
/// characters it treats as plain, single-quoted otherwise. `#` is always
/// quoted, since a line starting with it is dropped as a comment.
fn shell_word(word: &str) -> String {
    let plain = |c: char| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || "_@%+=:,./-${}".contains(c);
    if !word.is_empty() && word.chars().all(plain) {
        word.to_owned()
    } else {
        format!("'{}'", word.replace('\'', r"'\''"))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::domain::inventory::{Backend, BackendSource, Gateway, InventoryParts};

    fn native() -> Backend {
        Backend {
            id: "llama-native".into(),
            kind: BackendKind::LlamaServer,
            source: BackendSource::Build("llama.cpp".into()),
        }
    }

    fn cuda13() -> Backend {
        Backend {
            id: "llama-cuda13".into(),
            kind: BackendKind::LlamaServer,
            source: BackendSource::Image(CUDA13.into()),
        }
    }

    const CUDA13: &str = "ghcr.io/ggml-org/llama.cpp:server-cuda13";

    fn model(id: &str) -> Model {
        Model {
            id: id.into(),
            backend: "llama-native".into(),
            repo: None,
            dir: "llm/qwen".into(),
            files: BTreeMap::from([
                ("mmproj".into(), "mmproj-F16.gguf".into()),
                ("model".into(), "Qwen-Q4_K_M.gguf".into()),
            ]),
            flags: Flags::from([
                ("temp".into(), FlagValue::Float(0.7)),
                ("no-mmproj-offload".into(), FlagValue::Bool(false)),
                ("cache-type-k".into(), FlagValue::Text("q8_0".into())),
            ]),
            ctx_size: Some(98304),
            vram_mib: 22733,
            ttl: Some(1800),
            preload: false,
        }
    }

    fn parts() -> InventoryParts {
        InventoryParts {
            models_dir: "/ai/models".into(),
            gateway: Gateway::default(),
            defaults: BTreeMap::from([(
                BackendKind::LlamaServer,
                Flags::from([
                    ("flash-attn".into(), FlagValue::Text("on".into())),
                    ("parallel".into(), FlagValue::Int(1)),
                    ("no-mmproj-offload".into(), FlagValue::Bool(true)),
                    ("temp".into(), FlagValue::Float(0.6)),
                ]),
            )]),
            backends: vec![native()],
            models: vec![model("qwen")],
        }
    }

    fn runtimes() -> BTreeMap<String, Runtime> {
        BTreeMap::from([
            (
                "llama-native".into(),
                Runtime::Native("/data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server".into()),
            ),
            ("llama-cuda13".into(), Runtime::Image(CUDA13.into())),
        ])
    }

    /// The inventory of [`parts`], its model running in the CUDA 13 image.
    fn in_a_container() -> InventoryParts {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.backends.push(cuda13());
        parts.models[0].backend = "llama-cuda13".into();
        parts
    }

    /// The usable memory of the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU: 24463 MiB less 388
    /// reserved by the driver.
    const GPU_MIB: u64 = 24075;

    fn generated(parts: InventoryParts) -> Result<GatewayConfig, ConfigError> {
        generate(&Inventory::new(parts).unwrap(), &runtimes(), GPU_MIB)
    }

    /// The inventory of [`parts`] with one model per `(id, vram_mib)`.
    fn sized(models: &[(&str, u32)]) -> InventoryParts {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models = models
            .iter()
            .map(|&(id, vram_mib)| Model {
                vram_mib,
                ..model(id)
            })
            .collect();
        parts
    }

    fn sets(parts: InventoryParts) -> Vec<String> {
        let matrix = generated(parts).unwrap().matrix;
        matrix.sets.iter().map(|set| set.join(" & ")).collect()
    }

    fn lines(entry: &ModelEntry) -> Vec<String> {
        entry
            .cmd
            .lines()
            .iter()
            .map(|line| line.join(" "))
            .collect()
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_llama_server_model_runs_the_build_on_the_assigned_port() {
        let config = generated(parts()).unwrap();
        let entry = &config.models[0];
        assert_eq!(entry.id, "qwen");
        assert_eq!(
            lines(entry),
            [
                "/data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server",
                "--host 127.0.0.1",
                "--port ${PORT}",
                "--alias qwen",
                "--metrics",
                "--model /ai/models/llm/qwen/Qwen-Q4_K_M.gguf",
                "--mmproj /ai/models/llm/qwen/mmproj-F16.gguf",
                "--ctx-size 98304",
                "--cache-type-k q8_0",
                "--flash-attn on",
                "--parallel 1",
                "--temp 0.7",
            ]
        );
        assert_eq!(entry.cmd_stop, None);
        assert_eq!(entry.proxy, "http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}");
        assert_eq!(entry.check_endpoint, "/health");
        assert_eq!(entry.ttl, Some(1800));
        assert!(entry.disable_keep_alives);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_image_model_runs_the_same_server_in_a_named_container() {
        let config = generated(in_a_container()).unwrap();
        let entry = &config.models[0];
        assert_eq!(
            lines(entry),
            [
                "podman run",
                "--rm",
                "--replace",
                "--name ai-ctl-qwen",
                "--pull=never",
                "--no-healthcheck",
                "--network host",
                "--security-opt label=disable",
                "--device nvidia.com/gpu=all",
                "--volume /ai/models:/ai/models:ro",
                "ghcr.io/ggml-org/llama.cpp:server-cuda13",
                "--host 127.0.0.1",
                "--port ${PORT}",
                "--alias qwen",
                "--metrics",
                "--model /ai/models/llm/qwen/Qwen-Q4_K_M.gguf",
                "--mmproj /ai/models/llm/qwen/mmproj-F16.gguf",
                "--ctx-size 98304",
                "--cache-type-k q8_0",
                "--flash-attn on",
                "--parallel 1",
                "--temp 0.7",
            ]
        );
        assert_eq!(
            entry.cmd_stop.as_deref(),
            Some(
                ["podman", "stop", "ai-ctl-qwen"]
                    .map(String::from)
                    .as_slice()
            )
        );
        assert_eq!(entry.proxy, "http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}");
        assert_eq!(entry.check_endpoint, "/health");
        assert!(entry.disable_keep_alives);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_container_needs_a_directory_a_volume_can_mount() {
        let mut colon = in_a_container();
        colon.models_dir = "/ai/a:b".into();
        let message = generated(colon).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert_eq!(
            message,
            "model `qwen` runs in a container, which cannot mount \"/ai/a:b\": a volume path cannot contain `:`"
        );

        let mut native = parts();
        native.models_dir = "/ai/a:b".into();
        assert!(generated(native).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_model_flag_overrides_the_default_and_false_leaves_it_out() {
        let config = generated(parts()).unwrap();
        let words: Vec<&str> = config.models[0].cmd.words().collect();
        assert!(!words.contains(&"--no-mmproj-offload"));
        assert!(!words.contains(&"0.6"));

        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].flags.clear();
        let config = generated(parts).unwrap();
        let lines = lines(&config.models[0]);
        assert!(
            lines.contains(&"--no-mmproj-offload".to_owned()),
            "{lines:?}"
        );
        assert!(lines.contains(&"--temp 0.6".to_owned()), "{lines:?}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn models_keep_their_order_and_preloaded_ones_are_listed() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].preload = true;
        let mut second = model("fast");
        second.ttl = None;
        parts.models.push(second);
        let config = generated(parts).unwrap();
        let ids: Vec<&str> = config.models.iter().map(|m| m.id.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(ids, ["qwen", "fast"]);
        assert_eq!(config.preload, ["qwen"]);
        assert_eq!(config.models[1].ttl, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_value_llama_swap_would_read_as_a_macro_is_refused() {
        let mut in_a_flag = parts();
        in_a_flag.models[0].flags.insert(
            "chat-template-file".into(),
            FlagValue::Text("${HOME}/t.jinja".into()),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            generated(in_a_flag),
            Err(ConfigError::Macro {
                model: "qwen".into(),
                text: "${HOME}/t.jinja".into()
            })
        );

        let mut in_a_path = parts();
        in_a_path.models_dir = "/ai/${env.USER}".into();
        let message = generated(in_a_path).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("/ai/${env.USER}/llm/qwen"), "{message}");
        assert!(message.contains("cannot escape"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_control_character_is_refused() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0]
            .flags
            .insert("system-prompt".into(), FlagValue::Text("one\ntwo".into()));
        assert!(matches!(
            generated(parts),
            Err(ConfigError::ControlCharacter { .. })
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_backend_without_a_runtime_is_an_error() {
        let inventory = Inventory::new(parts()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            generate(&inventory, &BTreeMap::new(), GPU_MIB),
            Err(ConfigError::Unresolved("llama-native".into()))
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_yaml_writes_each_option_on_its_own_line() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].preload = true;
        parts.defaults.clear();
        parts.models[0].flags = Flags::from([
            (
                "chat-template-kwargs".into(),
                FlagValue::Text(r#"{"a":"b c"}"#.into()),
            ),
            ("jinja".into(), FlagValue::Bool(true)),
        ]);
        let mut second = model("fast-1.0");
        second.vram_mib = 21094;
        second.ttl = None;
        second.flags.clear();
        second.files = BTreeMap::from([("model".into(), "My Model #1.gguf".into())]);
        second.ctx_size = Some(4096);
        parts.models.push(second);
        let yaml = generated(parts).unwrap().to_yaml();
        assert_eq!(
            yaml,
            r#"# llama-swap's configuration, generated by ai-ctl from its inventory.
# `ai-ctl up` writes it again: change the inventory instead.

# The gateway's log keeps the models' output too: llama-swap holds it in
# memory only otherwise, and loses it when it stops.
logToStdout: "proxy,upstream"

models:
  "qwen":
    cmd: |
      /data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server
      --host 127.0.0.1
      --port ${PORT}
      --alias qwen
      --metrics
      --model /ai/models/llm/qwen/Qwen-Q4_K_M.gguf
      --mmproj /ai/models/llm/qwen/mmproj-F16.gguf
      --ctx-size 98304
      --chat-template-kwargs '{"a":"b c"}'
      --jinja
    proxy: "http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}"
    checkEndpoint: "/health"
    ttl: 1800
    # llama-server advertises keep-alive on a streamed response, then closes the
    # connection: a request sent right after reuses it and fails with 502
    # "proxy error: EOF" (llama-swap #1205). Needed until llama.cpp fixes #29680.
    disableKeepAlives: true
  "fast-1.0":
    cmd: |
      /data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server
      --host 127.0.0.1
      --port ${PORT}
      --alias fast-1.0
      --metrics
      --model '/ai/models/llm/qwen/My Model #1.gguf'
      --ctx-size 4096
    proxy: "http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}"
    checkEndpoint: "/health"
    # llama-server advertises keep-alive on a streamed response, then closes the
    # connection: a request sent right after reuses it and fails with 502
    # "proxy error: EOF" (llama-swap #1205). Needed until llama.cpp fixes #29680.
    disableKeepAlives: true

routing:
  router:
    use: "matrix"
    settings:
      # Models run side by side only when their VRAM budgets fit in 23051 MiB,
      # the GPU's 24075 usable MiB less the inventory's margin of 1024 MiB.
      # Evicting a model costs its budget, which loading it again reads back.
      matrix:
        evict_costs:
          "qwen": 22733
          "fast-1.0": 21094
        sets:
          "fit-1": "qwen"
          "fit-2": "fast-1.0"

hooks:
  on_startup:
    preload:
      - "qwen"
"#
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_yaml_stops_a_container_by_its_name() {
        let yaml = generated(in_a_container()).unwrap().to_yaml();
        assert!(
            yaml.contains(
                "      ghcr.io/ggml-org/llama.cpp:server-cuda13\n      --host 127.0.0.1\n"
            ),
            "{yaml}"
        );
        assert!(
            yaml.contains(
                "      --temp 0.7\n    cmdStop: \"podman stop ai-ctl-qwen\"\n    proxy: "
            ),
            "{yaml}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_matrix_lists_the_largest_combinations_that_fit() {
        // 23051 MiB to share: the fast model and whisper miss by 43 MiB.
        let models = [
            ("qwen", 22733),
            ("fast", 21094),
            ("whisper", 2000),
            ("tts", 1000),
        ];
        assert_eq!(
            sets(sized(&models)),
            ["qwen", "fast & tts", "whisper & tts"]
        );

        let mut no_margin = sized(&models);
        no_margin.gateway.vram_margin_mib = 0;
        assert_eq!(
            sets(no_margin),
            [
                "qwen & tts",
                "fast & whisper",
                "fast & tts",
                "whisper & tts"
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn models_that_all_fit_make_one_set() {
        let models = [("a", 4000), ("b", 3000), ("c", 2000)];
        assert_eq!(sets(sized(&models)), ["a & b & c"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_model_too_large_for_the_gpu_runs_alone() {
        let models = [("small", 1000), ("huge", 30000), ("tiny", 500)];
        assert_eq!(sets(sized(&models)), ["small & tiny", "huge"]);

        let mut no_room = sized(&models);
        no_room.gateway.vram_margin_mib = 30000;
        assert_eq!(sets(no_room), ["small", "huge", "tiny"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn evicting_a_model_costs_its_vram_budget() {
        let matrix = generated(sized(&[("a", 4000), ("b", 3000)]))
            .unwrap()
            .matrix;
        assert_eq!(
            matrix.evict_costs,
            [("a".to_owned(), 4000), ("b".to_owned(), 3000)]
        );
        assert_eq!((matrix.gpu_mib, matrix.margin_mib), (GPU_MIB, 1024));
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_inventory_without_models_gives_an_empty_map() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models.clear();
        assert_eq!(
            generated(parts).unwrap().to_yaml().lines().last(),
            Some("models: {}")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn words_are_quoted_only_when_the_splitter_needs_it() {
        assert_eq!(shell_word("--port"), "--port");
        assert_eq!(shell_word("${PORT}"), "${PORT}");
        assert_eq!(
            shell_word("127.0.0.1:${PORT}:8080"),
            "127.0.0.1:${PORT}:8080"
        );
        assert_eq!(shell_word("nvidia.com/gpu=all"), "nvidia.com/gpu=all");
        assert_eq!(shell_word(""), "''");
        assert_eq!(shell_word("a b"), "'a b'");
        assert_eq!(shell_word("#1"), "'#1'");
        assert_eq!(shell_word("it's"), r"'it'\''s'");
        assert_eq!(shell_word(r"C:\x"), r"'C:\x'");
        assert_eq!(shell_word("é"), "'é'");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_double_quoted_scalar_escapes_quotes_and_backslashes() {
        assert_eq!(quoted("qwen"), r#""qwen""#);
        assert_eq!(quoted(r#"a"b\c"#), r#""a\"b\\c""#);
    }
}

<file path="src/domain/gateway.rs">
//! The gateway, llama-swap: the releases ai-ctl installs.
//!
//! ai-ctl ships with one pinned release, whose archive's SHA-256 is written
//! here; any other release is checked against the checksums file published
//! with it.

use std::fmt;

/// llama-swap's repository, which publishes the release archives.
pub const REPOSITORY: &str = "https://github.com/mostlygeek/llama-swap";
/// The executable in a release archive, and once installed.
pub const BINARY: &str = "llama-swap";
/// The platform of the archives ai-ctl installs.
const PLATFORM: &str = "linux_amd64";

/// The release `gateway install` installs unless told otherwise, with the
/// SHA-256 of its archive for [`PLATFORM`]. v263 brings `disableKeepAlives`
/// against the 502 errors of a reused connection that llama-server closed
/// (llama-swap #1211).
pub const PINNED: Pinned = Pinned {
    release: Release(263),
    sha256: "9f561442a43ed71043105891a10a41019efdc8c023f5a3c807bcf12fbd2a048d",
};

/// A release ai-ctl knows the archive's SHA-256 of.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Pinned {
    pub release: Release,
    pub sha256: &'static str,
}

/// A llama-swap release, numbered: its tag is `v263`, and its directory once
/// installed too.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Release(u32);

/// Why a text is not a release.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("`{0}` is not a llama-swap release: a number such as 263, or a tag such as v263")]
pub struct InvalidRelease(pub String);

impl Release {
    /// Reads a release number, with or without the tag's `v`.
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Self, InvalidRelease> {
        let digits = text.strip_prefix('v').unwrap_or(text);
        match digits.parse() {
            Ok(number) if number > 0 && digits.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()) => {
                Ok(Self(number))
            }
            _ => Err(InvalidRelease(text.to_owned())),
        }
    }

    /// The archive of this release for the platform ai-ctl runs on.
    pub fn archive(self) -> String {
        format!("{BINARY}_{}_{PLATFORM}.tar.gz", self.0)
    }

    /// The checksums file published with the release.
    pub fn checksums(self) -> String {
        format!("{BINARY}_{}_checksums.txt", self.0)
    }

    /// Where a file of the release is downloaded from.
    pub fn url(self, file: &str) -> String {
        format!("{REPOSITORY}/releases/download/{self}/{file}")
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Release {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "v{}", self.0)
    }
}

/// A SHA-256 digest: 64 lower-case hexadecimal digits.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct Sha256(String);

/// Why a text is not a SHA-256 digest.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("`{0}` is not a SHA-256 digest: 64 lower-case hexadecimal digits")]
pub struct InvalidSha256(pub String);

impl Sha256 {
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Self, InvalidSha256> {
        if text.len() == 64
            && text
                .bytes()
                .all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit() || (b'a'..=b'f').contains(&b))
        {
            Ok(Self(text.to_owned()))
        } else {
            Err(InvalidSha256(text.to_owned()))
        }
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        &self.0
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Sha256 {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

/// The digest a checksums file gives `file`, in the format `sha256sum`
/// writes (`<digest>  <file>`, or `<digest> *<file>`); `None` when the file
/// is not listed or its digest is malformed.
pub fn checksum(checksums: &str, file: &str) -> Option<Sha256> {
    checksums.lines().find_map(|line| {
        let (digest, name) = line.trim().split_once(char::is_whitespace)?;
        let name = name.trim_start();
        let name = name.strip_prefix('*').unwrap_or(name);
        if name == file {
            Sha256::parse(digest).ok()
        } else {
            None
        }
    })
}

/// Whether the output of `llama-swap -version` names `release`, as in
/// `version: v263 (e33e97b), built at 2026-10-10T06:17:54Z`.
pub fn reports(version_output: &str, release: Release) -> bool {
    version_output
        .trim_start()
        .strip_prefix("version:")
        .and_then(|rest| rest.split_whitespace().next())
        .is_some_and(|tag| tag == release.to_string())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// The checksums file of v263.
    const CHECKSUMS: &str = include_str!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-checksums.txt");

    #[test]
    fn a_release_is_a_number_with_or_without_its_v() {
        assert_eq!(Release::parse("263"), Ok(Release(263)));
        assert_eq!(Release::parse("v263"), Ok(Release(263)));
        assert_eq!(Release(263).to_string(), "v263");
        for text in [
            "", "v", "0", "v0", "+263", "26 3", "vv263", "263.1", "latest", "V263",
        ] {
            assert!(Release::parse(text).is_err(), "{text}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_release_names_its_archive_checksums_and_urls() {
        let release = Release(263);
        assert_eq!(release.archive(), "llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz");
        assert_eq!(release.checksums(), "llama-swap_263_checksums.txt");
        assert_eq!(
            release.url(&release.archive()),
            "https://github.com/mostlygeek/llama-swap/releases/download/v263/llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_pinned_digest_is_the_one_its_release_publishes() {
        let published = checksum(CHECKSUMS, &PINNED.release.archive()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(Sha256::parse(PINNED.sha256), Ok(published));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_checksums_file_gives_the_digest_of_one_file() {
        assert_eq!(
            checksum(CHECKSUMS, "llama-swap_263_linux_arm64.tar.gz")
                .unwrap()
                .as_str(),
            "eb2a67d5212b688e32038e3d9c4b96dc2c64d5a16208ba008e30b4af4a7becb0"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            checksum(CHECKSUMS, "llama-swap_264_linux_amd64.tar.gz"),
            None
        );
        assert_eq!(checksum(CHECKSUMS, "linux_amd64.tar.gz"), None);
        let digest = "9f561442a43ed71043105891a10a41019efdc8c023f5a3c807bcf12fbd2a048d";
        let binary_mode = format!("{digest} *llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz\n");
        assert!(checksum(&binary_mode, "llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz").is_some());
        let malformed = "9F56  llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz\n";
        assert_eq!(
            checksum(malformed, "llama-swap_263_linux_amd64.tar.gz"),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_digest_is_64_lower_case_hexadecimal_digits() {
        let digest = "9f561442a43ed71043105891a10a41019efdc8c023f5a3c807bcf12fbd2a048d";
        assert!(Sha256::parse(digest).is_ok());
        assert!(Sha256::parse(&digest.to_uppercase()).is_err());
        assert!(Sha256::parse(&digest[1..]).is_err());
        assert!(Sha256::parse(&format!("{}g", &digest[1..])).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_version_output_must_name_the_release() {
        let output = include_str!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-version.txt");
        assert!(reports(output, Release(263)));
        assert!(!reports(output, Release(26)));
        assert!(!reports(output, Release(2630)));
        assert!(!reports("", Release(263)));
        assert!(!reports("v263\n", Release(263)));
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/inventory.rs">
//! The inventory: the backends and models the gateway serves.
//!
//! A model names a backend; the backend's kind decides which of the model's
//! fields apply, so audio and image kinds can join `llama-server` without a
//! breaking change to the format. An [`Inventory`] value is always valid:
//! [`Inventory::new`] checks every rule and reports every violation at once.

use std::collections::{BTreeMap, HashSet};
use std::fmt;
use std::net::SocketAddr;
use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};

/// The program a backend runs.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub enum BackendKind {
    LlamaServer,
}

impl BackendKind {
    pub const ALL: [BackendKind; 1] = [BackendKind::LlamaServer];

    /// The name used in the inventory file.
    pub fn name(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            BackendKind::LlamaServer => "llama-server",
        }
    }

    pub fn from_name(name: &str) -> Option<Self> {
        Self::ALL.into_iter().find(|kind| kind.name() == name)
    }

    /// Flags ai-ctl or the gateway sets itself, which no flag table may set.
    fn reserved_flags(self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
        match self {
            BackendKind::LlamaServer => &["host", "port", "alias", "metrics", "ctx-size", "model"],
        }
    }

    /// The file every model of this kind must name.
    fn required_file(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            BackendKind::LlamaServer => "model",
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for BackendKind {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.name())
    }
}

/// Where a backend's program comes from.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BackendSource {
    /// The current native build of a project, such as `llama.cpp`.
    Build(String),
    /// An official container image reference.
    Image(String),
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Backend {
    pub id: String,
    pub kind: BackendKind,
    pub source: BackendSource,
}

/// The value of a server flag. `Bool(true)` passes the flag alone,
/// `Bool(false)` leaves it out.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum FlagValue {
    Bool(bool),
    Int(i64),
    Float(f64),
    Text(String),
}

/// Server flags by long name, without the leading dashes.
pub type Flags = BTreeMap<String, FlagValue>;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Model {
    /// The ID clients put in a request's `model` field.
    pub id: String,
    /// The ID of the backend that serves it.
    pub backend: String,
    /// The Hugging Face repository its files come from.
    pub repo: Option<String>,
    /// Its directory, relative to the models directory.
    pub dir: PathBuf,
    /// Its files by server flag (`model`, `mmproj`…), relative to `dir`.
    pub files: BTreeMap<String, PathBuf>,
    /// Flags added to, or overriding, the kind's defaults.
    pub flags: Flags,
    /// Context size in tokens; required by `llama-server`.
    pub ctx_size: Option<u32>,
    /// VRAM the model needs once loaded, in MiB.
    pub vram_mib: u32,
    /// Seconds of inactivity before the gateway stops it; never when absent.
    pub ttl: Option<u32>,
    /// Loaded when the gateway starts.
    pub preload: bool,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Gateway {
    pub listen: SocketAddr,
    /// GPU memory, in MiB, that the models running together leave free.
    pub vram_margin_mib: u32,
}

impl Gateway {
    /// llama-server's `fit-target` in the shipped defaults: a model started
    /// beside another finds the memory its `--fit` keeps free, and moves no
    /// layer to the CPU.
    pub const DEFAULT_VRAM_MARGIN_MIB: u32 = 1024;
}

impl Default for Gateway {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            listen: SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 8080)),
            vram_margin_mib: Self::DEFAULT_VRAM_MARGIN_MIB,
        }
    }
}

/// The raw content of an inventory, before validation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct InventoryParts {
    pub models_dir: PathBuf,
    pub gateway: Gateway,
    pub defaults: BTreeMap<BackendKind, Flags>,
    pub backends: Vec<Backend>,
    pub models: Vec<Model>,
}

/// A validated inventory.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Inventory {
    parts: InventoryParts,
}

/// A rule an inventory breaks.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum InventoryError {
    #[error(
        "models_dir `{0}` is not an absolute path (install the inventory with `ai-ctl init --models-dir <dir>`)"
    )]
    RelativeModelsDir(PathBuf),
    #[error(
        "{what} id `{id}` must start with a letter or a digit and hold only ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`"
    )]
    InvalidId { what: &'static str, id: String },
    #[error("backend `{0}` is declared more than once")]
    DuplicateBackend(String),
    #[error("model `{0}` is declared more than once")]
    DuplicateModel(String),
    #[error("model `{model}` names backend `{backend}`, which is not declared")]
    UnknownBackend { model: String, backend: String },
    #[error("model `{0}` needs a VRAM budget above zero (vram_mib)")]
    ZeroVram(String),
    #[error("model `{0}` needs a context size above zero (ctx_size)")]
    MissingContextSize(String),
    #[error("model `{0}`: a ttl of zero is not allowed; leave ttl out to keep the model loaded")]
    ZeroTtl(String),
    #[error("model `{model}` has no `{file}` file, which a {kind} model needs")]
    MissingFile {
        model: String,
        kind: BackendKind,
        file: String,
    },
    #[error("model `{model}`: path `{path}` must be relative and stay inside its directory")]
    UnsafePath { model: String, path: PathBuf },
    #[error("{scope}: flag `{flag}` is set by ai-ctl and cannot be overridden")]
    ReservedFlag { scope: String, flag: String },
    #[error("{scope}: flag `{flag}` must be written without leading dashes")]
    DashedFlag { scope: String, flag: String },
}

/// Every rule an inventory breaks.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("{}", list(.0))]
pub struct InvalidInventory(pub Vec<InventoryError>);

fn list(errors: &[InventoryError]) -> String {
    errors
        .iter()
        .map(ToString::to_string)
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join("; ")
}

impl Inventory {
    /// Validates `parts`, reporting every rule broken.
    pub fn new(parts: InventoryParts) -> Result<Self, InvalidInventory> {
        let errors = validate(&parts);
        if errors.is_empty() {
            Ok(Self { parts })
        } else {
            Err(InvalidInventory(errors))
        }
    }

    pub fn models_dir(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.parts.models_dir
    }

    pub fn gateway(&self) -> Gateway {
        self.parts.gateway
    }

    /// Flags shared by every model of `kind`.
    pub fn defaults(&self, kind: BackendKind) -> Option<&Flags> {
        self.parts.defaults.get(&kind)
    }

    pub fn backends(&self) -> &[Backend] {
        &self.parts.backends
    }

    pub fn models(&self) -> &[Model] {
        &self.parts.models
    }

    pub fn backend(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Backend> {
        self.parts.backends.iter().find(|backend| backend.id == id)
    }

    /// The absolute directory holding `model`'s files.
    pub fn model_dir(&self, model: &Model) -> PathBuf {
        self.parts.models_dir.join(&model.dir)
    }

    /// The absolute path of every file `model` references, in flag order.
    pub fn model_files(&self, model: &Model) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
        let dir = self.model_dir(model);
        model.files.values().map(|file| dir.join(file)).collect()
    }
}

fn validate(parts: &InventoryParts) -> Vec<InventoryError> {
    let mut errors = Vec::new();

    if !parts.models_dir.is_absolute() {
        errors.push(InventoryError::RelativeModelsDir(parts.models_dir.clone()));
    }

    for (kind, flags) in &parts.defaults {
        check_flags(&format!("defaults.{kind}"), *kind, flags, &[], &mut errors);
    }

    let mut backend_ids = HashSet::new();
    for backend in &parts.backends {
        check_id("backend", &backend.id, &mut errors);
        if !backend_ids.insert(backend.id.as_str()) {
            errors.push(InventoryError::DuplicateBackend(backend.id.clone()));
        }
    }

    let mut model_ids = HashSet::new();
    for model in &parts.models {
        check_id("model", &model.id, &mut errors);
        if !model_ids.insert(model.id.as_str()) {
            errors.push(InventoryError::DuplicateModel(model.id.clone()));
        }
        check_model(parts, model, &mut errors);
    }

    errors
}

/// Whether `id` can name a model or a backend: an ID names a container and a
/// term of llama-swap's `matrix`, which take these characters only.
pub fn is_valid_id(id: &str) -> bool {
    let valid = |c: char| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || "._-".contains(c);
    id.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric()) && id.chars().all(valid)
}

fn check_id(what: &'static str, id: &str, errors: &mut Vec<InventoryError>) {
    if !is_valid_id(id) {
        errors.push(InventoryError::InvalidId {
            what,
            id: id.to_owned(),
        });
    }
}

fn check_model(parts: &InventoryParts, model: &Model, errors: &mut Vec<InventoryError>) {
    let id = &model.id;
    if model.vram_mib == 0 {
        errors.push(InventoryError::ZeroVram(id.clone()));
    }
    if model.ttl == Some(0) {
        errors.push(InventoryError::ZeroTtl(id.clone()));
    }
    for path in std::iter::once(&model.dir).chain(model.files.values()) {
        if !is_contained(path) {
            errors.push(InventoryError::UnsafePath {
                model: id.clone(),
                path: path.clone(),
            });
        }
    }

    let Some(backend) = parts.backends.iter().find(|b| b.id == model.backend) else {
        errors.push(InventoryError::UnknownBackend {
            model: id.clone(),
            backend: model.backend.clone(),
        });
        return;
    };

    let kind = backend.kind;
    match kind {
        BackendKind::LlamaServer => {
            if model.ctx_size.unwrap_or(0) == 0 {
                errors.push(InventoryError::MissingContextSize(id.clone()));
            }
        }
    }
    let required = kind.required_file();
    if !model.files.contains_key(required) {
        errors.push(InventoryError::MissingFile {
            model: id.clone(),
            kind,
            file: required.to_owned(),
        });
    }
    for flag in model.files.keys() {
        check_flag_name(&format!("model `{id}` files"), flag, errors);
    }
    let file_flags: Vec<&str> = model.files.keys().map(String::as_str).collect();
    check_flags(
        &format!("model `{id}`"),
        kind,
        &model.flags,
        &file_flags,
        errors,
    );
}

fn check_flags(
    scope: &str,
    kind: BackendKind,
    flags: &Flags,
    also_reserved: &[&str],
    errors: &mut Vec<InventoryError>,
) {
    for flag in flags.keys() {
        check_flag_name(scope, flag, errors);
        if kind.reserved_flags().contains(&flag.as_str()) || also_reserved.contains(&flag.as_str())
        {
            errors.push(InventoryError::ReservedFlag {
                scope: scope.to_owned(),
                flag: flag.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
}

fn check_flag_name(scope: &str, flag: &str, errors: &mut Vec<InventoryError>) {
    if flag.starts_with('-') {
        errors.push(InventoryError::DashedFlag {
            scope: scope.to_owned(),
            flag: flag.to_owned(),
        });
    }
}

/// True for a non-empty relative path made of plain components only.
fn is_contained(path: &Path) -> bool {
    path.components().next().is_some()
        && path
            .components()
            .all(|component| matches!(component, Component::Normal(_)))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn native() -> Backend {
        Backend {
            id: "llama-native".into(),
            kind: BackendKind::LlamaServer,
            source: BackendSource::Build("llama.cpp".into()),
        }
    }

    fn model(id: &str) -> Model {
        Model {
            id: id.into(),
            backend: "llama-native".into(),
            repo: Some("unsloth/Qwen3.8-27B-GGUF".into()),
            dir: "qwen3.8-27b".into(),
            files: BTreeMap::from([
                ("model".into(), "Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q4_K_M.gguf".into()),
                ("mmproj".into(), "mmproj-F16.gguf".into()),
            ]),
            flags: Flags::from([("cache-type-k".into(), FlagValue::Text("q8_0".into()))]),
            ctx_size: Some(98304),
            vram_mib: 22733,
            ttl: Some(1800),
            preload: true,
        }
    }

    fn parts() -> InventoryParts {
        InventoryParts {
            models_dir: "/models".into(),
            gateway: Gateway::default(),
            defaults: BTreeMap::from([(
                BackendKind::LlamaServer,
                Flags::from([("flash-attn".into(), FlagValue::Text("on".into()))]),
            )]),
            backends: vec![native()],
            models: vec![model("qwen3.8-27b")],
        }
    }

    fn errors(parts: InventoryParts) -> Vec<InventoryError> {
        Inventory::new(parts)
            .expect_err("the inventory is invalid")
            .0
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_valid_inventory_is_accepted() {
        let inventory = Inventory::new(parts()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(inventory.models().len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(inventory.backend("llama-native"), Some(&native()));
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.gateway().listen,
            "127.0.0.1:8080".parse().unwrap()
        );
        assert!(inventory.defaults(BackendKind::LlamaServer).is_some());
    }

    #[test]
    fn model_files_are_resolved_under_the_models_directory() {
        let inventory = Inventory::new(parts()).unwrap();
        let model = &inventory.models()[0];
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.model_dir(model),
            PathBuf::from("/models/qwen3.8-27b")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.model_files(model),
            [
                PathBuf::from("/models/qwen3.8-27b/mmproj-F16.gguf"),
                PathBuf::from("/models/qwen3.8-27b/Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q4_K_M.gguf"),
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn model_ids_must_be_unique() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models.push(model("qwen3.8-27b"));
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::DuplicateModel("qwen3.8-27b".into())]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn backend_ids_must_be_unique() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.backends.push(native());
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::DuplicateBackend("llama-native".into())]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn ids_hold_letters_digits_dots_underscores_and_dashes() {
        let mut valid = parts();
        valid.models[0].id = "Qwen_3.8-27b".into();
        assert!(Inventory::new(valid).is_ok());

        for id in [
            "", "qwen 27b", "qwen/27b", "qwen:27b", "-qwen", ".qwen", "qwén",
        ] {
            let mut parts = parts();
            parts.models[0].id = id.into();
            parts.backends.push(Backend {
                id: id.into(),
                ..native()
            });
            assert_eq!(
                errors(parts),
                [
                    InventoryError::InvalidId {
                        what: "backend",
                        id: id.into()
                    },
                    InventoryError::InvalidId {
                        what: "model",
                        id: id.into()
                    },
                ],
                "{id:?}"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_model_must_name_a_declared_backend() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].backend = "llama-cuda13".into();
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::UnknownBackend {
                model: "qwen3.8-27b".into(),
                backend: "llama-cuda13".into()
            }]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn budgets_and_context_sizes_must_be_positive() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].vram_mib = 0;
        parts.models[0].ctx_size = Some(0);
        parts.models[0].ttl = Some(0);
        let mut second = model("qwen3.6-35b-a3b");
        second.ctx_size = None;
        parts.models.push(second);
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [
                InventoryError::ZeroVram("qwen3.8-27b".into()),
                InventoryError::ZeroTtl("qwen3.8-27b".into()),
                InventoryError::MissingContextSize("qwen3.8-27b".into()),
                InventoryError::MissingContextSize("qwen3.6-35b-a3b".into()),
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_llama_server_model_needs_a_model_file() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].files.remove("model");
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::MissingFile {
                model: "qwen3.8-27b".into(),
                kind: BackendKind::LlamaServer,
                file: "model".into()
            }]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn paths_must_stay_inside_the_models_directory() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0].dir = "../elsewhere".into();
        parts.models[0]
            .files
            .insert("model-draft".into(), "/etc/passwd".into());
        parts.models[0].files.insert("lora".into(), "".into());
        let found = errors(parts);
        assert_eq!(found.len(), 3, "{found:?}");
        assert!(
            found
                .iter()
                .all(|e| matches!(e, InventoryError::UnsafePath { .. }))
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn models_dir_must_be_absolute() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models_dir = "${MODELS_DIR}".into();
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::RelativeModelsDir("${MODELS_DIR}".into())]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn flags_cannot_override_what_ai_ctl_sets() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        let flags = parts.defaults.get_mut(&BackendKind::LlamaServer).unwrap();
        flags.insert("port".into(), FlagValue::Int(9000));
        parts.models[0]
            .flags
            .insert("ctx-size".into(), FlagValue::Int(4096));
        parts.models[0]
            .flags
            .insert("mmproj".into(), FlagValue::Text("x.gguf".into()));
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [
                InventoryError::ReservedFlag {
                    scope: "defaults.llama-server".into(),
                    flag: "port".into()
                },
                InventoryError::ReservedFlag {
                    scope: "model `qwen3.8-27b`".into(),
                    flag: "ctx-size".into()
                },
                InventoryError::ReservedFlag {
                    scope: "model `qwen3.8-27b`".into(),
                    flag: "mmproj".into()
                },
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn flags_are_written_without_dashes() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models[0]
            .flags
            .insert("--temp".into(), FlagValue::Float(0.6));
        assert_eq!(
            errors(parts),
            [InventoryError::DashedFlag {
                scope: "model `qwen3.8-27b`".into(),
                flag: "--temp".into()
            }]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_error_is_reported_at_once() {
        let mut parts = parts();
        parts.models_dir = "models".into();
        parts.models[0].vram_mib = 0;
        parts.models.push(model("qwen3.8-27b"));
        let message = Inventory::new(parts).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("models_dir `models`"), "{message}");
        assert!(message.contains("VRAM budget"), "{message}");
        assert!(message.contains("more than once"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn backend_kinds_round_trip_through_their_names() {
        for kind in BackendKind::ALL {
            assert_eq!(BackendKind::from_name(kind.name()), Some(kind));
        }
        assert_eq!(BackendKind::from_name("whisper-server"), None);
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/machine.rs">
//! Facts about the machine, as the probes report them.

use std::fmt;
use std::path::PathBuf;

/// A dotted version number such as `13.2.86`; missing components are zero.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Version {
    pub major: u32,
    pub minor: u32,
    pub patch: u32,
}

impl Version {
    pub const fn new(major: u32, minor: u32, patch: u32) -> Self {
        Self {
            major,
            minor,
            patch,
        }
    }

    /// Parses `13`, `13.2` or `13.2.86`, ignoring a non-numeric suffix on the
    /// last component (`2.45.0-rc1`). Returns `None` without a numeric major.
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Option<Self> {
        let mut parts = text.trim().splitn(4, '.');
        let major = leading_number(parts.next()?)?;
        let minor = parts.next().and_then(leading_number).unwrap_or(0);
        let patch = parts.next().and_then(leading_number).unwrap_or(0);
        Some(Self::new(major, minor, patch))
    }

    /// The `major.minor` pair, for comparisons that ignore the patch level.
    pub fn release(self) -> (u32, u32) {
        (self.major, self.minor)
    }
}

fn leading_number(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    let end = text
        .find(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit())
        .unwrap_or(text.len());
    text[..end].parse().ok()
}

impl fmt::Display for Version {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}.{}.{}", self.major, self.minor, self.patch)
    }
}

/// What a probe found.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Probed<T> {
    Found(T),
    /// The program, library or service is not installed.
    Missing,
    /// It is installed but could not be read; the message says why.
    Failed(String),
}

impl<T> Probed<T> {
    pub fn found(&self) -> Option<&T> {
        match self {
            Probed::Found(value) => Some(value),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// The NVIDIA GPU and its driver. Memory is in bytes.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Gpu {
    pub name: String,
    /// Compute capability, `(12, 0)` for Blackwell consumer GPUs.
    pub compute_capability: (u32, u32),
    pub memory_total: u64,
    /// Set aside by the driver, out of `memory_total`; no process can
    /// allocate it.
    pub memory_reserved: u64,
    pub memory_used: u64,
    pub driver_version: String,
    /// Newest CUDA version the driver supports.
    pub cuda_version: Version,
    /// Processes holding GPU memory.
    pub processes: Vec<GpuProcess>,
}

impl Gpu {
    /// The memory processes can allocate, in MiB.
    pub fn usable_mib(&self) -> u64 {
        self.memory_total.saturating_sub(self.memory_reserved) >> 20
    }
}

/// A process holding GPU memory.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct GpuProcess {
    pub pid: u32,
    pub name: String,
    /// Bytes, when the driver reports them.
    pub memory: Option<u64>,
}

/// The CUDA toolkit `nvcc` belongs to.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CudaToolkit {
    pub version: Version,
    pub nvcc: PathBuf,
    /// Newest GCC major version `nvcc` accepts, read from the toolkit's
    /// `crt/host_config.h`.
    pub max_gcc_major: Probed<u32>,
}

/// CUDA 13.2 is reported to produce wrong output in llama.cpp builds
/// (llama.cpp issue #21255); see `docs/stack.md` §8.
pub const BROKEN_TOOLKIT: (u32, u32) = (13, 2);
/// ggml adds the `120a-real` architecture from CUDA 12.8 on.
pub const MIN_TOOLKIT: (u32, u32) = (12, 8);

/// Why a CUDA toolkit cannot build the backends on this machine.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ToolkitProblem {
    #[error(
        "CUDA {}.{} is reported to produce wrong output in llama.cpp builds (llama.cpp issue #21255)",
        BROKEN_TOOLKIT.0,
        BROKEN_TOOLKIT.1
    )]
    Broken,
    #[error(
        "building for sm_120a needs CUDA {}.{} or newer",
        MIN_TOOLKIT.0,
        MIN_TOOLKIT.1
    )]
    TooOld,
    #[error(
        "newer than CUDA {}.{}, the newest the driver supports",
        driver.major,
        driver.minor
    )]
    NewerThanDriver { driver: Version },
}

/// The toolkit rule `doctor` and the build gate share: CUDA 13.2 is
/// refused, 12.8 is the minimum, and the toolkit cannot be newer than the
/// driver's CUDA version. An unknown driver is not compared.
pub fn toolkit_problem(toolkit: Version, driver_cuda: Option<Version>) -> Option<ToolkitProblem> {
    let release = toolkit.release();
    if release == BROKEN_TOOLKIT {
        return Some(ToolkitProblem::Broken);
    }
    if release < MIN_TOOLKIT {
        return Some(ToolkitProblem::TooOld);
    }
    match driver_cuda {
        Some(driver) if release > driver.release() => {
            Some(ToolkitProblem::NewerThanDriver { driver })
        }
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The packages of NVIDIA's CUDA repository that make up a driver, which
/// comes from RPM Fusion only; `docs/toolchain.md` §2 sets this exclusion.
pub const DRIVER_PACKAGES: [&str; 5] = [
    "nvidia-*",
    "kmod-nvidia*",
    "xorg-x11-*nvidia*",
    "libnvidia-*",
    "dnf-plugin-nvidia",
];

/// An enabled NVIDIA CUDA repository, such as `cuda-fedora44-x86_64`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CudaRepository {
    pub id: String,
    /// The driver packages it offers despite its exclusions.
    pub driver_packages: Vec<String>,
}

/// The id dnf gives NVIDIA's CUDA repository for a Fedora release.
pub fn cuda_repository_id(fedora: u32) -> String {
    format!("cuda-fedora{fedora}-x86_64")
}

/// The repository file of NVIDIA's CUDA repository for a Fedora release.
pub fn cuda_repository_url(fedora: u32) -> String {
    format!(
        "https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/repos/fedora{fedora}/x86_64/cuda-fedora{fedora}.repo"
    )
}

/// True for the toolkit meta-packages that follow NVIDIA's releases:
/// `cuda-toolkit` follows the newest, `cuda-toolkit-13` the newest 13.x.
/// Only `cuda-toolkit-<major>-<minor>` stays on its version.
pub fn is_unversioned_toolkit(package: &str) -> bool {
    package == "cuda-toolkit"
        || package
            .strip_prefix("cuda-toolkit-")
            .is_some_and(|major| !major.is_empty() && major.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()))
}

/// The `(major, minor)` of a versioned toolkit meta-package such as
/// `cuda-toolkit-13-4`, which stays on its version.
pub fn versioned_toolkit(package: &str) -> Option<(u32, u32)> {
    let (major, minor) = package.strip_prefix("cuda-toolkit-")?.split_once('-')?;
    let number = |text: &str| {
        (!text.is_empty() && text.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()))
            .then(|| text.parse().ok())
            .flatten()
    };
    Some((number(major)?, number(minor)?))
}

/// A GNU C++ compiler found on the `PATH`, such as `g++` or `g++-15`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct HostCompiler {
    pub command: String,
    pub path: PathBuf,
    pub version: Version,
}

/// The compiler a CUDA build should use: the newest one whose major version
/// `nvcc` accepts. On equal versions, the earlier one in `compilers` wins, so
/// the default `g++` is kept over an identical `g++-N`.
pub fn pick_host_compiler(compilers: &[HostCompiler], max_major: u32) -> Option<&HostCompiler> {
    compilers
        .iter()
        .filter(|compiler| compiler.version.major <= max_major)
        .fold(None, |best: Option<&HostCompiler>, compiler| match best {
            Some(best) if best.version >= compiler.version => Some(best),
            _ => Some(compiler),
        })
}

/// A command-line tool whose version `doctor` checks.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Tool {
    Cmake,
    Git,
    Podman,
    NvidiaCtk,
}

impl Tool {
    /// The command that runs the tool.
    pub fn command(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Tool::Cmake => "cmake",
            Tool::Git => "git",
            Tool::Podman => "podman",
            Tool::NvidiaCtk => "nvidia-ctk",
        }
    }

    /// The oldest version a native build accepts, for the build tools.
    pub fn minimum(self) -> Option<Version> {
        match self {
            // ggml's CUDA backend: `cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.18)`.
            Tool::Cmake => Some(Version::new(3, 18, 0)),
            // `git ls-remote --sort`, which resolves release tags, arrived in 2.18.
            Tool::Git => Some(Version::new(2, 18, 0)),
            Tool::Podman | Tool::NvidiaCtk => None,
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn version_parses_one_to_three_components() {
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("13"), Some(Version::new(13, 0, 0)));
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("13.2"), Some(Version::new(13, 2, 0)));
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("13.2.86"), Some(Version::new(13, 2, 86)));
        assert_eq!(Version::parse(" 4.3.0\n"), Some(Version::new(4, 3, 0)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn version_ignores_suffixes_and_extra_components() {
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("2.45.0-rc1"), Some(Version::new(2, 45, 0)));
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("1.2.3.4"), Some(Version::new(1, 2, 3)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn version_needs_a_numeric_major() {
        assert_eq!(Version::parse(""), None);
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("V13.2"), None);
        assert_eq!(Version::parse("unknown"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn versions_order_numerically() {
        assert!(Version::new(13, 10, 0) > Version::new(13, 9, 99));
        assert_eq!(Version::new(13, 2, 86).release(), (13, 2));
        assert_eq!(Version::new(13, 2, 86).to_string(), "13.2.86");
    }

    fn gxx(command: &str, version: Version) -> HostCompiler {
        HostCompiler {
            command: command.to_owned(),
            path: PathBuf::from("/usr/bin").join(command),
            version,
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn host_compiler_skips_versions_nvcc_rejects() {
        let compilers = [
            gxx("g++", Version::new(16, 2, 1)),
            gxx("g++-15", Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
            gxx("g++-14", Version::new(14, 3, 0)),
        ];
        let picked = pick_host_compiler(&compilers, 15).map(|c| c.command.as_str());
        assert_eq!(picked, Some("g++-15"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn host_compiler_keeps_the_default_when_accepted() {
        let compilers = [
            gxx("g++", Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
            gxx("g++-15", Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
            gxx("g++-14", Version::new(14, 3, 0)),
        ];
        let picked = pick_host_compiler(&compilers, 15).map(|c| c.command.as_str());
        assert_eq!(picked, Some("g++"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn host_compiler_is_none_when_every_version_is_too_new() {
        let compilers = [gxx("g++", Version::new(16, 2, 1))];
        assert_eq!(pick_host_compiler(&compilers, 15), None);
        assert_eq!(pick_host_compiler(&[], 15), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn cuda_13_2_is_refused_whatever_the_driver() {
        let driver = Some(Version::new(13, 4, 0));
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 2, 86), driver),
            Some(ToolkitProblem::Broken)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 2, 0), None),
            Some(ToolkitProblem::Broken)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_toolkit_older_than_12_8_is_refused() {
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_problem(Version::new(12, 6, 85), Some(Version::new(13, 4, 0))),
            Some(ToolkitProblem::TooOld)
        );
        assert_eq!(toolkit_problem(Version::new(12, 8, 0), None), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_toolkit_newer_than_the_driver_is_refused() {
        let driver = Version::new(13, 4, 0);
        assert_eq!(
            toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 5, 10), Some(driver)),
            Some(ToolkitProblem::NewerThanDriver { driver })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            ToolkitProblem::NewerThanDriver { driver }.to_string(),
            "newer than CUDA 13.4, the newest the driver supports"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_toolkit_up_to_the_driver_is_accepted() {
        let driver = Some(Version::new(13, 4, 0));
        assert_eq!(toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 4, 92), driver), None);
        assert_eq!(toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 3, 0), driver), None);
        // An unknown driver is not compared.
        assert_eq!(toolkit_problem(Version::new(13, 9, 0), None), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_meta_packages_without_a_minor_version_are_unversioned() {
        assert!(is_unversioned_toolkit("cuda-toolkit"));
        assert!(is_unversioned_toolkit("cuda-toolkit-13"));
        for package in [
            "cuda-toolkit-13-4",
            "cuda-toolkit-13-4-config-common",
            "cuda-toolkit-13-config-common",
            "cuda-toolkit-config-common",
            "cuda-toolkit-",
        ] {
            assert!(!is_unversioned_toolkit(package), "{package}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn versioned_meta_packages_name_their_release() {
        assert_eq!(versioned_toolkit("cuda-toolkit-13-4"), Some((13, 4)));
        for package in [
            "cuda-toolkit",
            "cuda-toolkit-13",
            "cuda-toolkit-13-4-config-common",
            "cuda-toolkit-13-config-common",
            "cuda-toolkit-config-common",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(versioned_toolkit(package), None, "{package}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_cuda_repository_follows_the_fedora_release() {
        assert_eq!(cuda_repository_id(44), "cuda-fedora44-x86_64");
        assert_eq!(
            cuda_repository_url(45),
            "https://developer.download.nvidia.com/compute/cuda/repos/fedora45/x86_64/cuda-fedora45.repo"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_build_tools_have_a_minimum() {
        assert_eq!(Tool::Cmake.minimum(), Some(Version::new(3, 18, 0)));
        assert_eq!(Tool::Git.minimum(), Some(Version::new(2, 18, 0)));
        assert_eq!(Tool::Podman.minimum(), None);
        assert_eq!(Tool::NvidiaCtk.minimum(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn probed_exposes_only_found_values() {
        assert_eq!(Probed::Found(3).found(), Some(&3));
        assert_eq!(Probed::<u32>::Missing.found(), None);
        assert_eq!(Probed::<u32>::Failed("boom".into()).found(), None);
    }
}

<file path="src/domain/recipe.rs">
//! Build recipes: where a ggml project's sources live, how its releases are
//! tagged, and which CMake target makes its server.
//!
//! The builder is the same for every project; everything specific to one
//! project is in its recipe. llama.cpp is the only recipe shipped so far.

use std::path::Path;

/// How to fetch and compile one project.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Recipe {
    /// The project's name, as the inventory's `source = { build = "…" }`
    /// gives it.
    pub project: &'static str,
    /// The git repository the sources are cloned from.
    pub repository: &'static str,
    pub tags: TagPattern,
    /// The project's own CMake options; the builder adds the CUDA and
    /// toolchain ones.
    pub cmake_flags: &'static [(&'static str, &'static str)],
    /// The CMake target to build, and nothing else.
    pub target: &'static str,
    /// Where the target's binary lands, relative to the CMake build tree.
    pub artifact: &'static str,
    /// How the binary lists the devices it sees, when it can.
    pub device_listing: Option<DeviceListing>,
}

impl Recipe {
    /// The installed binary's file name, such as `llama-server`.
    pub fn binary(&self) -> &'static str {
        Path::new(self.artifact)
            .file_name()
            .and_then(|name| name.to_str())
            .unwrap_or(self.artifact)
    }
}

/// How a project names its release tags.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum TagPattern {
    /// A prefix and a release number, such as llama.cpp's `b6512`.
    Numbered { prefix: &'static str },
}

impl TagPattern {
    /// The tag's release number, or `None` when the tag is not a release.
    pub fn release(&self, tag: &str) -> Option<u64> {
        match self {
            TagPattern::Numbered { prefix } => {
                let number = tag.strip_prefix(prefix)?;
                let digits = !number.is_empty() && number.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit());
                if digits { number.parse().ok() } else { None }
            }
        }
    }

    /// The newest release among `tags`; other tags are ignored.
    pub fn newest<'a>(&self, tags: impl IntoIterator<Item = &'a str>) -> Option<&'a str> {
        tags.into_iter()
            .filter_map(|tag| self.release(tag).map(|number| (number, tag)))
            .max_by_key(|(number, _)| *number)
            .map(|(_, tag)| tag)
    }

    /// The pattern `git ls-remote` narrows the tags with. It lets through
    /// tags that are not releases, such as `b10964-b10964-b29c606`:
    /// [`TagPattern::release`] has the last word.
    pub fn ls_remote_pattern(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            TagPattern::Numbered { prefix } => format!("refs/tags/{prefix}[0-9]*"),
        }
    }
}

/// Arguments that make a binary print the devices it sees, one per line,
/// such as `  CUDA0: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090 Laptop GPU (24463 MiB, …)`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct DeviceListing {
    pub args: &'static [&'static str],
}

/// The CUDA devices in a device listing: the lines naming `CUDA<n>`.
pub fn cuda_devices(listing: &str) -> Vec<&str> {
    listing
        .lines()
        .map(str::trim)
        .filter(|line| {
            line.split_once(':').is_some_and(|(name, _)| {
                name.strip_prefix("CUDA").is_some_and(|index| {
                    !index.is_empty() && index.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit())
                })
            })
        })
        .collect()
}

/// llama.cpp: releases are tagged `b<number>` several times a day; the
/// server is the `llama-server` target, in `tools/server`.
pub const LLAMA_CPP: Recipe = Recipe {
    project: "llama.cpp",
    repository: "https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp.git",
    tags: TagPattern::Numbered { prefix: "b" },
    cmake_flags: &[
        // `tools/` holds the server; only the target below is compiled.
        ("LLAMA_BUILD_TOOLS", "ON"),
        ("LLAMA_BUILD_SERVER", "ON"),
        ("LLAMA_BUILD_TESTS", "OFF"),
        ("LLAMA_BUILD_EXAMPLES", "OFF"),
        // The unified `llama` binary, which is not needed.
        ("LLAMA_BUILD_APP", "OFF"),
    ],
    target: "llama-server",
    artifact: "bin/llama-server",
    device_listing: Some(DeviceListing {
        args: &["--list-devices"],
    }),
};

/// Every recipe ai-ctl ships.
pub const RECIPES: &[Recipe] = &[LLAMA_CPP];

/// The recipe of `project`.
pub fn recipe(project: &str) -> Option<&'static Recipe> {
    RECIPES.iter().find(|recipe| recipe.project == project)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const LLAMA_TAGS: TagPattern = TagPattern::Numbered { prefix: "b" };

    #[test]
    fn llama_cpp_is_the_shipped_recipe() {
        let recipe = recipe("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(recipe.target, "llama-server");
        assert_eq!(recipe.binary(), "llama-server");
        assert!(recipe.repository.starts_with("https://"));
        assert_eq!(super::recipe("whisper.cpp"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn recipe_projects_are_unique() {
        for (i, recipe) in RECIPES.iter().enumerate() {
            assert!(
                RECIPES[i + 1..]
                    .iter()
                    .all(|other| other.project != recipe.project),
                "{} is shipped twice",
                recipe.project
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn recipes_leave_toolchain_and_cuda_options_to_the_builder() {
        for recipe in RECIPES {
            for (name, _) in recipe.cmake_flags {
                assert!(
                    !name.starts_with("CMAKE_") && !name.starts_with("GGML_CUDA"),
                    "{}: {name}",
                    recipe.project
                );
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn llama_cpp_release_tags_are_numbered() {
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.release("b11538"), Some(11538));
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.release("b1"), Some(1));
        for tag in [
            "b",
            "b10964-b10964-b29c606",
            "backup/allozaur-ui",
            "11538",
            "B11538",
            "b+1",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.release(tag), None, "{tag}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_newest_release_is_compared_numerically() {
        let tags = [
            "b9999",
            "b10964-b10964-b29c606",
            "b11538",
            "b10000",
            "gguf-v0.17.1",
        ];
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.newest(tags), Some("b11538"));
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.newest(["b9", "b10"]), Some("b10"));
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.newest(["master"]), None);
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.newest([]), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn ls_remote_narrows_to_tags_starting_like_releases() {
        assert_eq!(LLAMA_TAGS.ls_remote_pattern(), "refs/tags/b[0-9]*");
    }

    #[test]
    fn cuda_devices_are_read_from_the_listing() {
        // `llama-server --list-devices` of b11538 on this machine.
        let listing = include_str!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-server-list-devices.txt");
        assert_eq!(
            cuda_devices(listing),
            ["CUDA0: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090 Laptop GPU (24075 MiB, 23798 MiB free)"]
        );
        assert!(cuda_devices("Available devices:\n").is_empty());
        assert!(cuda_devices("CUDA: no device\nCUDAx: y\n  Vulkan0: other\n").is_empty());
    }
}
