<file path="src/domain/stats.rs">
//! Statistics over the repeated measures of a bench run.
//!
//! Ported from llm-ctl v1 (`PromptResult::from_metrics` and `percentile` in
//! its `src/domain/benchmark.rs`), with three changes: an empty series has no
//! summary rather than zeros, the standard deviation is a sample's, and the
//! 99th percentile is given only over enough values.

/// Below this many values, a 99th percentile is the largest value or an
/// interpolation toward it, and is not reported.
pub const P99_MIN_COUNT: usize = 100;

/// The summary of a series of measures.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Summary {
    pub count: usize,
    pub mean: f64,
    pub median: f64,
    pub min: f64,
    pub max: f64,
    /// The standard deviation of a sample (divided by n − 1), since the
    /// repetitions of a run sample a setting's behaviour; `None` for a single
    /// value.
    pub stddev: Option<f64>,
    /// `None` below [`P99_MIN_COUNT`] values.
    pub p99: Option<f64>,
}

impl Summary {
    /// The summary of `values`, non-finite ones left out; `None` when no
    /// value is left.
    pub fn of(values: impl IntoIterator<Item = f64>) -> Option<Self> {
        let mut sorted: Vec<f64> = values.into_iter().filter(|v| v.is_finite()).collect();
        sorted.sort_by(f64::total_cmp);
        let (&min, &max) = (sorted.first()?, sorted.last()?);
        let count = sorted.len();
        let n = count as f64;
        let mean = sorted.iter().sum::<f64>() / n;
        let stddev = (count > 1).then(|| {
            let squares: f64 = sorted.iter().map(|v| (v - mean).powi(2)).sum();
            (squares / (n - 1.0)).sqrt()
        });
        Some(Self {
            count,
            mean,
            median: percentile(&sorted, 50.0),
            min,
            max,
            stddev,
            p99: (count >= P99_MIN_COUNT).then(|| percentile(&sorted, 99.0)),
        })
    }

    /// The standard deviation relative to the mean: the spread that more
    /// repetitions narrow. `None` for a single value or a zero mean.
    pub fn relative_spread(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        self.stddev
            .filter(|_| self.mean != 0.0)
            .map(|stddev| stddev / self.mean.abs())
    }
}

/// The `pct` percentile of `sorted`, which is not empty, interpolated
/// linearly between the two nearest ranks.
fn percentile(sorted: &[f64], pct: f64) -> f64 {
    let rank = pct / 100.0 * (sorted.len() - 1) as f64;
    let (lower, upper) = (rank.floor() as usize, rank.ceil() as usize);
    let fraction = rank - lower as f64;
    sorted[lower] * (1.0 - fraction) + sorted[upper] * fraction
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn close(a: f64, b: f64) -> bool {
        (a - b).abs() < 1e-9
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_series_is_summarised_whatever_its_order() {
        let summary = Summary::of([50.0, 40.0, 45.0]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(summary.count, 3);
        assert!(close(summary.mean, 45.0));
        assert_eq!(summary.median, 45.0);
        assert_eq!((summary.min, summary.max), (40.0, 50.0));
        // Sample deviation: squares 25 + 25 + 0 over n - 1 = 2.
        assert!(close(summary.stddev.unwrap(), 5.0));
        assert_eq!(summary.p99, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_series_has_no_summary() {
        assert_eq!(Summary::of([]), None);
        assert_eq!(Summary::of([f64::NAN, f64::INFINITY]), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn non_finite_values_are_left_out() {
        let summary = Summary::of([f64::NAN, 2.0, f64::NEG_INFINITY, 4.0]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(summary.count, 2);
        assert!(close(summary.mean, 3.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_single_value_has_no_deviation() {
        let summary = Summary::of([42.0]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((summary.mean, summary.median), (42.0, 42.0));
        assert_eq!(summary.stddev, None);
        assert_eq!(summary.relative_spread(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_median_of_an_even_series_is_interpolated() {
        assert_eq!(Summary::of([10.0, 20.0, 30.0, 40.0]).unwrap().median, 25.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn percentiles_interpolate_between_ranks() {
        let sorted = [10.0, 20.0, 30.0, 40.0, 50.0];
        assert_eq!(percentile(&sorted, 0.0), 10.0);
        assert_eq!(percentile(&sorted, 50.0), 30.0);
        assert_eq!(percentile(&sorted, 100.0), 50.0);
        assert!(close(percentile(&sorted, 99.0), 49.6));
        assert_eq!(percentile(&[42.0], 99.0), 42.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_99th_percentile_needs_enough_values() {
        let short: Vec<f64> = (1..P99_MIN_COUNT).map(|v| v as f64).collect();
        assert_eq!(Summary::of(short).unwrap().p99, None);
        let long: Vec<f64> = (1..=P99_MIN_COUNT).map(|v| v as f64).collect();
        // Rank 0.99 × 99 = 98.01, between 99 and 100.
        assert!(close(Summary::of(long).unwrap().p99.unwrap(), 99.01));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_relative_spread_is_the_deviation_over_the_mean() {
        let summary = Summary::of([40.0, 50.0, 45.0]).unwrap();
        assert!(close(summary.relative_spread().unwrap(), 5.0 / 45.0));
        assert_eq!(Summary::of([-1.0, 1.0]).unwrap().relative_spread(), None);
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/domain/timestamp.rs">
//! Points in time as ai-ctl records them: UTC, to the second.

use std::fmt;

/// Seconds since the Unix epoch, in UTC, written in RFC 3339 form:
/// `2026-10-09T18:02:11Z`. Years run from 0 to 9999, so the text always has
/// the same width and sorts in time order.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Timestamp(i64);

const SECONDS_PER_DAY: i64 = 86_400;
/// `0000-01-01T00:00:00Z` and `9999-12-31T23:59:59Z`.
const MIN: i64 = -62_167_219_200;
const MAX: i64 = 253_402_300_799;

impl Timestamp {
    /// 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z.
    pub const EPOCH: Self = Self(0);

    /// The timestamp `seconds` after the Unix epoch, or `None` outside years
    /// 0 to 9999.
    pub fn from_unix(seconds: i64) -> Option<Self> {
        (MIN..=MAX).contains(&seconds).then_some(Self(seconds))
    }

    pub fn unix(self) -> i64 {
        self.0
    }

    /// Parses exactly `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, the form [`Timestamp`] writes.
    /// Out-of-range fields, such as February 30, are rejected.
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Option<Self> {
        let bytes = text.as_bytes();
        let shape = bytes.len() == 20
            && bytes.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, &b)| match i {
                4 | 7 => b == b'-',
                10 => b == b'T',
                13 | 16 => b == b':',
                19 => b == b'Z',
                _ => b.is_ascii_digit(),
            });
        if !shape {
            return None;
        }
        let field = |range: std::ops::Range<usize>| text[range].parse::<i64>().ok();
        let (year, month, day) = (field(0..4)?, field(5..7)?, field(8..10)?);
        let (hour, minute, second) = (field(11..13)?, field(14..16)?, field(17..19)?);
        if !(1..=12).contains(&month)
            || !(1..=31).contains(&day)
            || hour > 23
            || minute > 59
            || second > 59
        {
            return None;
        }
        let days = days_from_civil(year, month, day);
        let timestamp = Self(days * SECONDS_PER_DAY + hour * 3600 + minute * 60 + second);
        // A day past the end of its month moves into the next one: writing
        // the value back exposes it.
        (timestamp.to_string() == text).then_some(timestamp)
    }

    /// `2026-10-09 18:02 UTC`, for listings.
    pub fn minutes(self) -> String {
        let text = self.to_string();
        format!("{} {} UTC", &text[..10], &text[11..16])
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Timestamp {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        let days = self.0.div_euclid(SECONDS_PER_DAY);
        let seconds = self.0.rem_euclid(SECONDS_PER_DAY);
        let (year, month, day) = civil_from_days(days);
        write!(
            f,
            "{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z",
            seconds / 3600,
            seconds % 3600 / 60,
            seconds % 60
        )
    }
}

/// Days since 1970-01-01 of a proleptic Gregorian date (Howard Hinnant's
/// `days_from_civil`).
fn days_from_civil(year: i64, month: i64, day: i64) -> i64 {
    let year = if month <= 2 { year - 1 } else { year };
    let era = year.div_euclid(400);
    let year_of_era = year - era * 400;
    let day_of_year = (153 * ((month + 9) % 12) + 2) / 5 + day - 1;
    let day_of_era = year_of_era * 365 + year_of_era / 4 - year_of_era / 100 + day_of_year;
    era * 146_097 + day_of_era - 719_468
}

/// The date `days` after 1970-01-01 (Howard Hinnant's `civil_from_days`).
fn civil_from_days(days: i64) -> (i64, i64, i64) {
    let days = days + 719_468;
    let era = days.div_euclid(146_097);
    let day_of_era = days - era * 146_097;
    let year_of_era =
        (day_of_era - day_of_era / 1460 + day_of_era / 36_524 - day_of_era / 146_096) / 365;
    let day_of_year = day_of_era - (365 * year_of_era + year_of_era / 4 - year_of_era / 100);
    let shifted_month = (5 * day_of_year + 2) / 153;
    let day = day_of_year - (153 * shifted_month + 2) / 5 + 1;
    let month = if shifted_month < 10 {
        shifted_month + 3
    } else {
        shifted_month - 9
    };
    let year = year_of_era + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    (year, month, day)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn at(text: &str) -> Timestamp {
        Timestamp::parse(text).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{text} does not parse"))
    }

    #[test]
    fn known_instants_format_in_rfc_3339() {
        let cases = [
            (0, "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z"),
            (951_782_400, "2000-02-29T00:00:00Z"),
            (1_791_568_931, "2026-10-09T18:02:11Z"),
            (-1, "1969-12-31T23:59:59Z"),
            (MIN, "0000-01-01T00:00:00Z"),
            (MAX, "9999-12-31T23:59:59Z"),
        ];
        for (seconds, text) in cases {
            let timestamp = Timestamp::from_unix(seconds).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(timestamp.to_string(), text);
            assert_eq!(at(text), timestamp, "{text}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_day_of_a_leap_cycle_round_trips() {
        let start = at("2000-01-01T00:00:00Z").unix();
        for day in 0..(4 * 366) {
            let timestamp = Timestamp::from_unix(start + day * SECONDS_PER_DAY + 43_199).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(Timestamp::parse(&timestamp.to_string()), Some(timestamp));
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn impossible_dates_are_rejected() {
        for text in [
            "2026-02-29T00:00:00Z",
            "2100-02-29T00:00:00Z",
            "2026-04-31T00:00:00Z",
            "2026-13-01T00:00:00Z",
            "2026-00-10T00:00:00Z",
            "2026-10-09T24:00:00Z",
            "2026-10-09T18:60:00Z",
            "2026-10-09T18:02:60Z",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(Timestamp::parse(text), None, "{text}");
        }
        assert!(Timestamp::parse("2024-02-29T00:00:00Z").is_some());
    }

    #[test]
    fn other_shapes_are_rejected() {
        for text in [
            "",
            "2026-10-09",
            "2026-10-09T18:02:11",
            "2026-10-09T18:02:11+00:00",
            "2026-10-09 18:02:11Z",
            "2026-10-09T18:02:11.5Z",
            "+026-10-09T18:02:11Z",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(Timestamp::parse(text), None, "{text:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn years_beyond_four_digits_are_out_of_range() {
        assert_eq!(Timestamp::from_unix(MAX + 1), None);
        assert_eq!(Timestamp::from_unix(MIN - 1), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn timestamps_order_in_time_and_list_to_the_minute() {
        assert!(at("2026-10-09T18:02:11Z") < at("2026-10-10T00:00:00Z"));
        assert_eq!(at("2026-10-09T18:02:11Z").minutes(), "2026-10-09 18:02 UTC");
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/infrastructure/build_store.rs">
//! Installed builds on disk.
//!
//! ```text
//! builds/
//!   .lock                     held by every change to the builds
//!   llama.cpp/
//!     b11538/                 llama-server, build.json, build.log
//!     current -> b11538
//!     .staging-b11540-4242/   a build being made, out of sight
//! ```
//!
//! A build appears under its id by a rename, so an installed build is
//! always complete; the `current` link is replaced by a rename too. Hidden
//! directories left by an interrupted run are removed by the next build.

use std::any::Any;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fs;
use std::io;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail, ensure};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

use crate::application::ports::{BuildStore, Staged, Unreadable};
use crate::domain::build::{Build, BuildId, CURRENT, CommitId};
use crate::domain::machine::{HostCompiler, Version};
use crate::domain::timestamp::Timestamp;

use super::files::{lock, remove_dir_if_present, remove_hidden, replace_link};

/// The metadata file in each build's directory.
pub const METADATA: &str = "build.json";
/// The build log, copied beside the binary.
const LOG: &str = "build.log";
/// The metadata format this ai-ctl writes and reads.
const FORMAT: u32 = 1;

/// `build.json`, as written to disk.
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Metadata {
    format: u32,
    project: String,
    id: String,
    commit: String,
    toolkit: String,
    host_compiler: Compiler,
    cmake_flags: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    built_at: String,
}

#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Compiler {
    command: String,
    path: PathBuf,
    version: String,
}

/// Serializes `build` as the content of its `build.json`. Fails only on a
/// compiler path that is not valid UTF-8.
pub fn to_json(build: &Build) -> Result<String> {
    let metadata = Metadata {
        format: FORMAT,
        project: build.project.clone(),
        id: build.id.to_string(),
        commit: build.commit.to_string(),
        toolkit: build.toolkit.to_string(),
        host_compiler: Compiler {
            command: build.host_compiler.command.clone(),
            path: build.host_compiler.path.clone(),
            version: build.host_compiler.version.to_string(),
        },
        cmake_flags: build.cmake_flags.clone(),
        built_at: build.built_at.to_string(),
    };
    let mut text =
        serde_json::to_string_pretty(&metadata).context("cannot serialize the build metadata")?;
    text.push('\n');
    Ok(text)
}

/// Reads the content of a `build.json`.
pub fn from_json(text: &str) -> Result<Build> {
    let metadata: Metadata = serde_json::from_str(text).context("malformed build metadata")?;
    ensure!(
        metadata.format == FORMAT,
        "build metadata format {} is not supported (this ai-ctl reads format {FORMAT})",
        metadata.format
    );
    let version = |what: &str, text: &str| {
        Version::parse(text).with_context(|| format!("{what} `{text}` is not a version"))
    };
    Ok(Build {
        project: metadata.project,
        id: BuildId::parse(&metadata.id)?,
        commit: CommitId::parse(&metadata.commit)?,
        toolkit: version("toolkit", &metadata.toolkit)?,
        host_compiler: HostCompiler {
            command: metadata.host_compiler.command,
            path: metadata.host_compiler.path,
            version: version("host compiler version", &metadata.host_compiler.version)?,
        },
        cmake_flags: metadata.cmake_flags,
        built_at: Timestamp::parse(&metadata.built_at).with_context(|| {
            format!(
                "built_at `{}` is not a UTC timestamp such as 2026-10-09T18:02:11Z",
                metadata.built_at
            )
        })?,
    })
}

/// The builds under `root`, one directory per project.
pub struct FsBuildStore {
    root: PathBuf,
}

impl FsBuildStore {
    pub fn new(root: PathBuf) -> Self {
        Self { root }
    }

    fn project_dir(&self, project: &str) -> PathBuf {
        self.root.join(project)
    }

    /// A hidden name in the project's directory, unique to this process.
    fn hidden(&self, project: &str, kind: &str, id: &BuildId) -> PathBuf {
        self.project_dir(project)
            .join(format!(".{kind}-{id}-{}", std::process::id()))
    }

    /// Removes what an interrupted run left: staged, replaced or half-removed
    /// builds, and a link not yet renamed into place.
    fn remove_leftovers(&self, project: &str) -> Result<()> {
        remove_hidden(&self.project_dir(project))
    }
}

impl BuildStore for FsBuildStore {
    fn lock(&self) -> Result<Box<dyn Any>> {
        lock(&self.root, "building or changing the builds")
    }

    fn builds(&self, project: &str) -> Result<(Vec<Build>, Vec<Unreadable>)> {
        let dir = self.project_dir(project);
        let entries = match fs::read_dir(&dir) {
            Ok(entries) => entries,
            Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((vec![], vec![])),
            Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", dir.display())),
        };
        let mut builds = Vec::new();
        let mut unreadable = Vec::new();
        for entry in entries {
            let entry = entry.with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", dir.display()))?;
            let name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
            let path = entry.path();
            if name.starts_with('.') || name == CURRENT || !path.is_dir() {
                continue;
            }
            match read_build(&path, project, &name) {
                Ok(build) => builds.push(build),
                Err(err) => unreadable.push(Unreadable {
                    dir: path,
                    reason: format!("{err:#}"),
                }),
            }
        }
        Ok((builds, unreadable))
    }

    fn current(&self, project: &str) -> Result<Option<BuildId>> {
        let link = self.project_dir(project).join(CURRENT);
        let target = match fs::read_link(&link) {
            Ok(target) => target,
            Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(None),
            Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", link.display())),
        };
        let name = target
            .to_str()
            .filter(|name| !name.contains('/'))
            .with_context(|| {
                format!(
                    "{} points at {}, not at a build of its directory",
                    link.display(),
                    target.display()
                )
            })?;
        Ok(Some(BuildId::parse(name)?))
    }

    fn binary(&self, project: &str, id: &BuildId, name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        self.project_dir(project).join(id.as_str()).join(name)
    }

    fn stage(&self, build: &Build, artifact: &Path, log: &Path) -> Result<Staged> {
        self.remove_leftovers(&build.project)?;
        let dir = self.hidden(&build.project, "staging", &build.id);
        let staged = (|| {
            fs::create_dir_all(&dir).with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", dir.display()))?;
            let name = artifact
                .file_name()
                .with_context(|| format!("{} has no file name", artifact.display()))?;
            let binary = dir.join(name);
            fs::copy(artifact, &binary).with_context(|| {
                format!("cannot copy {} to {}", artifact.display(), binary.display())
            })?;
            fs::copy(log, dir.join(LOG))
                .with_context(|| format!("cannot copy {}", log.display()))?;
            let metadata = dir.join(METADATA);
            fs::write(&metadata, to_json(build)?)
                .with_context(|| format!("cannot write {}", metadata.display()))?;
            Ok(binary)
        })();
        match staged {
            Ok(binary) => Ok(Staged {
                dir,
                binary,
                build: build.clone(),
            }),
            Err(err) => {
                // Best effort: the error above is what matters.
                let _ = remove_dir_if_present(&dir);
                Err(err)
            }
        }
    }

    fn publish(&self, staged: &Staged, replace: bool) -> Result<()> {
        let project = &staged.build.project;
        let target = self.project_dir(project).join(staged.build.id.as_str());
        if target.symlink_metadata().is_err() {
            return fs::rename(&staged.dir, &target)
                .with_context(|| format!("cannot install {}", target.display()));
        }
        ensure!(replace, "{} already exists", target.display());
        let old = self.hidden(project, "replaced", &staged.build.id);
        fs::rename(&target, &old)
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot move {} aside", target.display()))?;
        if let Err(err) = fs::rename(&staged.dir, &target) {
            // Put the old build back; failing that, the next build removes it.
            let _ = fs::rename(&old, &target);
            return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot install {}", target.display()));
        }
        // The new build is in place; a leftover is removed by the next build.
        let _ = remove_dir_if_present(&old);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn discard(&self, staged: &Staged) -> Result<()> {
        remove_dir_if_present(&staged.dir)
    }

    fn set_current(&self, project: &str, id: &BuildId) -> Result<()> {
        let dir = self.project_dir(project);
        ensure!(
            dir.join(id.as_str()).is_dir(),
            "{project} has no build {id} in {}",
            dir.display()
        );
        replace_link(
            &dir.join(CURRENT),
            id.as_str(),
            &self.hidden(project, "current", id),
        )
    }

    fn remove(&self, project: &str, id: &BuildId) -> Result<()> {
        if self.current(project)?.as_ref() == Some(id) {
            bail!("{project} {id} is the current build");
        }
        let target = self.project_dir(project).join(id.as_str());
        let doomed = self.hidden(project, "removing", id);
        // Out of sight first: an interrupted removal leaves no half build.
        fs::rename(&target, &doomed)
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot remove {}", target.display()))?;
        remove_dir_if_present(&doomed)
    }

    fn contains(&self, program: &Path) -> bool {
        // A removed binary reads as `<path> (deleted)`, still under the root.
        program.starts_with(&self.root)
    }
}

/// The build in `dir`, which must be the build its name and project say.
fn read_build(dir: &Path, project: &str, name: &str) -> Result<Build> {
    let path = dir.join(METADATA);
    let text =
        fs::read_to_string(&path).with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", path.display()))?;
    let build = from_json(&text).with_context(|| format!("invalid {}", path.display()))?;
    ensure!(
        build.project == project && build.id.as_str() == name,
        "{} describes {} {}",
        path.display(),
        build.project,
        build.id
    );
    Ok(build)
}

/// The current time, for a build's date.
pub fn now() -> Timestamp {
    let seconds = SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
        .map_or(0, |elapsed| {
            i64::try_from(elapsed.as_secs()).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)
        });
    Timestamp::from_unix(seconds).unwrap_or(Timestamp::EPOCH)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn build() -> Build {
        Build {
            project: "llama.cpp".into(),
            id: BuildId::parse("b11538").unwrap(),
            commit: CommitId::parse("79e2e74eb11022c1ba2e438df7f0ca2d4c10f8b6").unwrap(),
            toolkit: Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            host_compiler: HostCompiler {
                command: "g++".into(),
                path: PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/g++"),
                version: Version::new(16, 2, 1),
            },
            cmake_flags: BTreeMap::from([
                ("CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES".into(), "120a-real".into()),
                ("GGML_CUDA".into(), "ON".into()),
            ]),
            built_at: Timestamp::parse("2026-10-09T18:02:11Z").unwrap(),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn metadata_round_trips() {
        let build = build();
        assert_eq!(from_json(&to_json(&build).unwrap()).unwrap(), build);
    }

    #[test]
    fn metadata_is_readable_json() {
        let text = to_json(&build()).unwrap();
        let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&text).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(value["format"], 1);
        assert_eq!(value["id"], "b11538");
        assert_eq!(value["toolkit"], "13.4.92");
        assert_eq!(value["host_compiler"]["path"], "/usr/bin/g++");
        assert_eq!(value["cmake_flags"]["GGML_CUDA"], "ON");
        assert_eq!(value["built_at"], "2026-10-09T18:02:11Z");
        assert!(text.ends_with("}\n"));
    }

    fn edited(edit: impl FnOnce(&mut serde_json::Value)) -> String {
        let mut value: serde_json::Value =
            serde_json::from_str(&to_json(&build()).unwrap()).unwrap();
        edit(&mut value);
        value.to_string()
    }

    fn error(text: &str) -> String {
        format!("{:#}", from_json(text).unwrap_err())
    }

    #[test]
    fn another_format_is_refused() {
        let message = error(&edited(|value| value["format"] = 2.into()));
        assert!(message.contains("format 2 is not supported"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn invalid_fields_are_refused_with_their_value() {
        let cases: [(&str, serde_json::Value, &str); 5] = [
            ("id", "../b1".into(), "`../b1` is not a build id"),
            (
                "commit",
                "79e2e74".into(),
                "`79e2e74` is not a full commit hash",
            ),
            ("toolkit", "unknown".into(), "toolkit `unknown`"),
            ("built_at", "2026-10-09".into(), "built_at `2026-10-09`"),
            ("format", "1".into(), "malformed"),
        ];
        for (field, value, expected) in cases {
            let message = error(&edited(|metadata| metadata[field] = value));
            assert!(message.contains(expected), "{field}: {message}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_and_missing_fields_are_refused() {
        let extra = edited(|value| value["extra"] = true.into());
        assert!(error(&extra).contains("unknown field `extra`"));
        let missing = edited(|value| {
            value.as_object_mut().unwrap().remove("commit");
        });
        assert!(error(&missing).contains("missing field `commit`"));
        assert!(error("").contains("malformed"));
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod store_tests {
    use super::*;

    struct Fixture {
        _root: tempfile::TempDir,
        store: FsBuildStore,
        artifact: PathBuf,
        log: PathBuf,
    }

    fn fixture() -> Fixture {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let work = root.path().join("work/bin");
        fs::create_dir_all(&work).unwrap();
        let artifact = work.join("llama-server");
        fs::write(&artifact, "binary").unwrap();
        let log = root.path().join("build-llama.cpp.log");
        fs::write(&log, "[1/1] done\n").unwrap();
        Fixture {
            store: FsBuildStore::new(root.path().join("builds")),
            _root: root,
            artifact,
            log,
        }
    }

    fn build(id: &str, at: i64) -> Build {
        Build {
            project: "llama.cpp".into(),
            id: BuildId::parse(id).unwrap(),
            commit: CommitId::parse("79e2e74eb11022c1ba2e438df7f0ca2d4c10f8b6").unwrap(),
            toolkit: Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            host_compiler: HostCompiler {
                command: "g++".into(),
                path: PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/g++"),
                version: Version::new(16, 2, 1),
            },
            cmake_flags: BTreeMap::new(),
            built_at: Timestamp::from_unix(at).unwrap(),
        }
    }

    impl Fixture {
        fn install(&self, id: &str, at: i64) -> Build {
            let build = build(id, at);
            let staged = self.store.stage(&build, &self.artifact, &self.log).unwrap();
            self.store.publish(&staged, false).unwrap();
            build
        }

        fn project_entries(&self) -> Vec<String> {
            let mut names: Vec<String> = fs::read_dir(self.store.project_dir("llama.cpp"))
                .unwrap()
                .map(|entry| entry.unwrap().file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned())
                .collect();
            names.sort();
            names
        }
    }

    fn id(text: &str) -> BuildId {
        BuildId::parse(text).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_store_contains_its_builds_binaries_removed_or_not() {
        let f = fixture();
        let binary = f.store.binary("llama.cpp", &id("b11538"), "llama-server");
        assert!(f.store.contains(&binary));
        let removed = format!("{} (deleted)", binary.display());
        assert!(f.store.contains(Path::new(&removed)));
        assert!(!f.store.contains(Path::new("/usr/bin/llama-server")));
        assert!(!f.store.contains(&f.artifact));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_staged_build_is_out_of_sight_until_published() {
        let f = fixture();
        let build = build("b2", 20);
        let staged = f.store.stage(&build, &f.artifact, &f.log).unwrap();
        assert!(
            staged
                .dir
                .file_name()
                .unwrap()
                .to_string_lossy()
                .starts_with(".staging-b2-")
        );
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(&staged.binary).unwrap(), "binary");
        assert_eq!(f.store.builds("llama.cpp").unwrap(), (vec![], vec![]));

        f.store.publish(&staged, false).unwrap();
        let (builds, unreadable) = f.store.builds("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(builds, [build]);
        assert!(unreadable.is_empty());
        let binary = f.store.binary("llama.cpp", &id("b2"), "llama-server");
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(binary).unwrap(), "binary");
        let dir = f.store.project_dir("llama.cpp").join("b2");
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(dir.join(LOG)).unwrap(), "[1/1] done\n");
        assert_eq!(f.project_entries(), ["b2"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_binary_keeps_its_permissions() {
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
        let f = fixture();
        fs::set_permissions(&f.artifact, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o755)).unwrap();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        let binary = f.store.binary("llama.cpp", &id("b1"), "llama-server");
        assert_eq!(
            fs::metadata(binary).unwrap().permissions().mode() & 0o777,
            0o755
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_installed_build_is_replaced_only_on_request() {
        let f = fixture();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        let again = build("b1", 99);
        let staged = f.store.stage(&again, &f.artifact, &f.log).unwrap();
        let message = f.store.publish(&staged, false).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.ends_with("b1 already exists"), "{message}");
        f.store.publish(&staged, true).unwrap();
        let (builds, _) = f.store.builds("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(builds, [again]);
        assert_eq!(f.project_entries(), ["b1"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_discarded_build_leaves_nothing() {
        let f = fixture();
        let staged = f
            .store
            .stage(&build("b1", 10), &f.artifact, &f.log)
            .unwrap();
        f.store.discard(&staged).unwrap();
        assert!(f.project_entries().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn leftovers_of_an_interrupted_run_go_with_the_next_build() {
        let f = fixture();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        let dir = f.store.project_dir("llama.cpp");
        fs::create_dir_all(dir.join(".staging-b2-1/sub")).unwrap();
        fs::create_dir(dir.join(".removing-b0-1")).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink("b1", dir.join(".current-b1-1")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(f.store.builds("llama.cpp").unwrap().0.len(), 1);
        f.install("b2", 20);
        assert_eq!(f.project_entries(), ["b1", "b2"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_current_link_switches_between_installed_builds() {
        let f = fixture();
        assert_eq!(f.store.current("llama.cpp").unwrap(), None);
        f.install("b1", 10);
        f.install("b2", 20);
        f.store.set_current("llama.cpp", &id("b2")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(f.store.current("llama.cpp").unwrap(), Some(id("b2")));
        f.store.set_current("llama.cpp", &id("b1")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(f.store.current("llama.cpp").unwrap(), Some(id("b1")));
        let link = f.store.project_dir("llama.cpp").join(CURRENT);
        assert_eq!(fs::read_link(&link).unwrap(), Path::new("b1"));
        assert_eq!(
            fs::read_to_string(link.join("llama-server")).unwrap(),
            "binary"
        );
        assert!(f.store.set_current("llama.cpp", &id("b9")).is_err());
        assert_eq!(f.project_entries(), ["b1", "b2", "current"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_link_pointing_elsewhere_is_reported() {
        let f = fixture();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        let link = f.store.project_dir("llama.cpp").join(CURRENT);
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink("/usr/bin", &link).unwrap();
        let message = f.store.current("llama.cpp").unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(
            message.contains("not at a build of its directory"),
            "{message}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn removing_a_build_spares_the_current_one() {
        let f = fixture();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        f.install("b2", 20);
        f.store.set_current("llama.cpp", &id("b2")).unwrap();
        f.store.remove("llama.cpp", &id("b1")).unwrap();
        let message = f
            .store
            .remove("llama.cpp", &id("b2"))
            .unwrap_err()
            .to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("is the current build"), "{message}");
        assert_eq!(f.project_entries(), ["b2", "current"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn foreign_directories_are_unreadable_builds() {
        let f = fixture();
        f.install("b1", 10);
        let dir = f.store.project_dir("llama.cpp");
        fs::create_dir(dir.join("notes")).unwrap();
        fs::create_dir(dir.join("b7")).unwrap();
        fs::write(
            dir.join("b7").join(METADATA),
            to_json(&build("b8", 1)).unwrap(),
        )
        .unwrap();
        fs::write(dir.join("README"), "a file").unwrap();
        let (builds, mut unreadable) = f.store.builds("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(builds.len(), 1);
        unreadable.sort_by(|a, b| a.dir.cmp(&b.dir));
        assert_eq!(unreadable.len(), 2);
        assert!(
            unreadable[0].reason.contains("describes llama.cpp b8"),
            "{:?}",
            unreadable[0]
        );
        assert!(
            unreadable[1].reason.contains("cannot read"),
            "{:?}",
            unreadable[1]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_second_lock_is_refused_until_the_first_is_dropped() {
        let f = fixture();
        let _exclusive = crate::infrastructure::command::exclusive();
        let first = f.store.lock().unwrap();
        let message = f.store.lock().unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("another ai-ctl"), "{message}");
        drop(first);
        f.store.lock().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn now_is_after_this_code_was_written() {
        assert!(now() > Timestamp::parse("2026-10-09T00:00:00Z").unwrap());
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/chat_api.rs">
//! Chat completions, streamed and timed: the client of the bench, over the
//! HTTP/1.0 client of [`super::http`].
//!
//! It speaks to the gateway, or to a model's own llama-server for the
//! overhead scenario. The clock starts before the connection opens. The
//! first token is the first event whose delta carries content or reasoning;
//! every later one adds the gap since the one before; the request ends at
//! `data: [DONE]`, whether or not the server closes the connection then. The
//! last events carry `usage`, asked for with `stream_options`, and
//! llama-server's `timings`.

use std::io::Read;
use std::net::SocketAddr;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

use anyhow::{Context, Result, anyhow, bail};
use serde::Deserialize;
use serde_json::{Value, json};

use super::http::{self, Body};
use crate::application::ports::{ChatClient, ChatRequest, Reply};
use crate::domain::bench::{ClientTimings, Drafts, Sample, ServerTimings, Usage};

const PATH: &str = "/v1/chat/completions";
/// The longest wait for a byte. A model loads before its first request is
/// answered, within llama-swap's 120 s health check, and nothing comes
/// meanwhile; during a long prefill, llama-server sends a ping every 30 s
/// (`--sse-ping-interval`).
const TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(300);
/// How much of an error's body is read, to quote it.
const MAX_ERROR_BODY: usize = 64 * 1024;

/// Chat completions over HTTP, OpenAI-compatible.
pub struct OpenAiChat;

impl ChatClient for OpenAiChat {
    fn stream(&self, addr: SocketAddr, request: &ChatRequest) -> Result<Reply> {
        let body = request_body(request)?;
        let start = Instant::now();
        let mut stream = http::send(addr, "POST", PATH, Some(&body), Some(TIMEOUT))?
            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("nothing listens on {addr}"))?;
        read_reply(&mut stream, &mut || start.elapsed())
            .with_context(|| format!("POST http://{addr}{PATH}"))
    }
}

/// The JSON body of `request`, streamed with its usage.
fn request_body(request: &ChatRequest) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
    let messages: Vec<Value> = request
        .messages
        .iter()
        .map(|message| {
            let mut value = json!({"role": message.role.name(), "content": message.content});
            if let Some(reasoning) = &message.reasoning {
                value["reasoning_content"] = json!(reasoning);
            }
            value
        })
        .collect();
    let mut body = json!({
        "model": request.model,
        "messages": messages,
        "max_tokens": request.max_tokens,
        "seed": request.seed,
        "stream": true,
        "stream_options": {"include_usage": true},
    });
    if request.ignore_eos {
        body["ignore_eos"] = json!(true);
    }
    serde_json::to_vec(&body).context("cannot write the request")
}

/// Reads a streamed reply from `stream`, `clock` giving the time since the
/// request was sent.
fn read_reply(stream: &mut dyn Read, clock: &mut dyn FnMut() -> Duration) -> Result<Reply> {
    let (head, early) = http::read_head(stream)?;
    let mut body = Body::new(stream, &head, early);
    if head.status != 200 {
        let text = body.read_to(MAX_ERROR_BODY)?;
        bail!("answered {}: {}", head.status, error_message(&text));
    }
    let (mut events, mut reply) = (Events::default(), Stream::default());
    while let Some(bytes) = body.next_bytes()? {
        let at = clock();
        for data in events.feed(&bytes)? {
            if reply.event(&data, at)? {
                return reply.finish(at);
            }
        }
    }
    bail!("the stream ends before [DONE]")
}

/// The message of an error body, `{"error": {"message": …}}` as llama-swap
/// and llama-server write it, or else the body itself.
fn error_message(body: &[u8]) -> String {
    serde_json::from_slice::<ErrorBody>(body)
        .map(|body| body.error.message)
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| String::from_utf8_lossy(body).trim().to_owned())
}

/// Splits an event stream into the data of its events: `data` lines joined,
/// comments such as llama-server's pings and other fields left out.
#[derive(Default)]
struct Events {
    line: Vec<u8>,
    data: Option<Vec<u8>>,
}

impl Events {
    /// The data of the events `bytes` completes.
    fn feed(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
        let mut complete = Vec::new();
        for &byte in bytes {
            if byte != b'\n' {
                self.line.push(byte);
                continue;
            }
            let line = std::mem::take(&mut self.line);
            let line = line.strip_suffix(b"\r").unwrap_or(&line);
            if line.is_empty() {
                if let Some(data) = self.data.take() {
                    complete.push(String::from_utf8(data).context("an event is not text")?);
                }
                continue;
            }
            let (field, value) = match line.iter().position(|&b| b == b':') {
                Some(colon) => (&line[..colon], &line[colon + 1..]),
                None => (line, &[][..]),
            };
            if field == b"data" {
                let value = value.strip_prefix(b" ").unwrap_or(value);
                let data = self.data.get_or_insert_with(Vec::new);
                if !data.is_empty() {
                    data.push(b'\n');
                }
                data.extend_from_slice(value);
            }
        }
        Ok(complete)
    }
}

/// A reply as its events arrive.
#[derive(Default)]
struct Stream {
    first: Option<Duration>,
    last: Option<Duration>,
    gaps: Vec<Duration>,
    content: String,
    reasoning: String,
    timings: Option<ServerTimings>,
    usage: Option<Usage>,
}

impl Stream {
    /// Takes the data of one event, arrived `at`; `true` at `[DONE]`.
    fn event(&mut self, data: &str, at: Duration) -> Result<bool> {
        if data == "[DONE]" {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        let chunk: Chunk =
            serde_json::from_str(data).with_context(|| format!("malformed event {data:?}"))?;
        if let Some(error) = chunk.error {
            bail!("the server reported an error: {}", error.message);
        }
        let mut token = false;
        for delta in chunk.choices.into_iter().map(|choice| choice.delta) {
            for (text, into) in [
                (delta.reasoning_content, &mut self.reasoning),
                (delta.content, &mut self.content),
            ] {
                if let Some(text) = text.filter(|text| !text.is_empty()) {
                    into.push_str(&text);
                    token = true;
                }
            }
        }
        if token {
            match self.last {
                None => self.first = Some(at),
                Some(last) => self.gaps.push(at.saturating_sub(last)),
            }
            self.last = Some(at);
        }
        if let Some(timings) = chunk.timings {
            self.timings = Some(timings.into());
        }
        if let Some(usage) = chunk.usage {
            self.usage = Some(Usage {
                prompt_tokens: usage.prompt_tokens,
                completion_tokens: usage.completion_tokens,
            });
        }
        Ok(false)
    }

    /// The reply, its stream ended `total` after the request.
    fn finish(self, total: Duration) -> Result<Reply> {
        let ttft = self
            .first
            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("the stream ends without a token"))?;
        Ok(Reply {
            sample: Sample {
                client: ClientTimings {
                    ttft,
                    total,
                    gaps: self.gaps,
                },
                server: self.timings,
                usage: self.usage,
            },
            content: self.content,
            reasoning: self.reasoning,
        })
    }
}

/// An event of a streamed chat completion.
#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct Chunk {
    #[serde(default)]
    choices: Vec<Choice>,
    error: Option<ErrorDetail>,
    timings: Option<Timings>,
    usage: Option<UsageBody>,
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct Choice {
    #[serde(default)]
    delta: Delta,
}

#[derive(Deserialize, Default)]
struct Delta {
    content: Option<String>,
    reasoning_content: Option<String>,
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct ErrorBody {
    error: ErrorDetail,
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct ErrorDetail {
    message: String,
}

/// llama-server's `timings` (`server_slot_stats::to_json` in
/// `tools/server/server-common.cpp`): the draft counts only when
/// speculative decoding proposed a token.
#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct Timings {
    cache_n: u64,
    prompt_n: u64,
    prompt_ms: f64,
    predicted_n: u64,
    predicted_ms: f64,
    draft_n: Option<u64>,
    draft_n_accepted: Option<u64>,
}

impl From<Timings> for ServerTimings {
    fn from(timings: Timings) -> Self {
        Self {
            cache_n: timings.cache_n,
            prompt_n: timings.prompt_n,
            prompt_ms: timings.prompt_ms,
            predicted_n: timings.predicted_n,
            predicted_ms: timings.predicted_ms,
            drafts: timings.draft_n.map(|proposed| Drafts {
                proposed,
                accepted: timings.draft_n_accepted.unwrap_or(0),
            }),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct UsageBody {
    prompt_tokens: u64,
    completion_tokens: u64,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::domain::bench::Message;
    use crate::infrastructure::http::{Trickle, serve};

    /// A reply streamed through llama-swap: 24 reasoning tokens, MTP on.
    const GATEWAY: &[u8] = include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-chat-stream.http");
    /// A reply streamed by llama-server itself, in chunks: 6 content tokens,
    /// 14 prompt tokens from the cache.
    const DIRECT: &[u8] =
        include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-server-b11538-chat-stream.http");
    const UNKNOWN: &[u8] =
        include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-chat-unknown-model.http");
    const BAD_REQUEST: &[u8] =
        include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-chat-bad-request.http");

    /// A clock that moves 10 ms at each reading.
    fn ticking() -> impl FnMut() -> Duration {
        let mut now = Duration::ZERO;
        move || {
            now += Duration::from_millis(10);
            now
        }
    }

    fn reply_to(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Reply> {
        read_reply(&mut Trickle(bytes), &mut ticking())
    }

    fn request() -> ChatRequest {
        ChatRequest {
            model: "qwen3.8-27b".into(),
            messages: vec![
                Message::system("Be brief."),
                Message::user("Hello"),
                Message::assistant("Hi.", "Greet back."),
                Message::user("Say hello in five words."),
            ],
            max_tokens: 24,
            ignore_eos: true,
            seed: 42,
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_reply_through_the_gateway_keeps_its_tokens_and_timings() {
        let reply = reply_to(GATEWAY).unwrap();
        assert!(
            reply
                .reasoning
                .starts_with("The user wants me to say hello in exactly five words.")
        );
        assert!(reply.reasoning.ends_with("\"Hello there, my dear"));
        assert_eq!(reply.content, "");
        let sample = &reply.sample;
        assert_eq!(sample.client.gaps.len(), 23);
        assert!(sample.client.ttft < sample.client.total);
        assert_eq!(
            sample.server,
            Some(ServerTimings {
                cache_n: 0,
                prompt_n: 46,
                prompt_ms: 620.099,
                predicted_n: 24,
                predicted_ms: 402.573,
                drafts: Some(Drafts {
                    proposed: 18,
                    accepted: 13
                }),
            })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            sample.usage,
            Some(Usage {
                prompt_tokens: 46,
                completion_tokens: 24
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_chunked_reply_from_llama_server_reads_alike() {
        let reply = reply_to(DIRECT).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(reply.content, "Hello there, my friend.");
        assert_eq!(reply.reasoning, "");
        assert_eq!(reply.sample.client.gaps.len(), 5);
        let server = reply.sample.server.unwrap();
        assert_eq!((server.cache_n, server.prompt_n), (14, 4));
        assert_eq!(
            server.drafts,
            Some(Drafts {
                proposed: 6,
                accepted: 3
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_clock_marks_the_first_token_and_the_gaps_after_it() {
        let mut stream = Stream::default();
        let at = Duration::from_millis;
        let role = r#"{"choices":[{"delta":{"role":"assistant","content":null}}]}"#;
        assert!(!stream.event(role, at(5)).unwrap());
        for (text, ms) in [("Hel", 120), ("lo", 140), ("", 150), (" you", 175)] {
            let data = format!(r#"{{"choices":[{{"delta":{{"content":"{text}"}}}}]}}"#);
            assert!(!stream.event(&data, at(ms)).unwrap());
        }
        assert!(stream.event("[DONE]", at(180)).unwrap());
        let reply = stream.finish(at(180)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(reply.content, "Hello you");
        let client = reply.sample.client;
        assert_eq!((client.ttft, client.total), (at(120), at(180)));
        assert_eq!(client.gaps, [at(20), at(35)]);
        assert_eq!(reply.sample.server, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pings_comments_and_carriage_returns_are_read_past() {
        let mut events = Events::default();
        let first = events
            .feed(b":\n\nevent: x\r\ndata: {\"a\":\r\ndata:1}\r\n\r\n: ping\n\nda")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(first, ["{\"a\":\n1}"]);
        assert_eq!(
            events.feed(b"ta: [DONE]\n\n").unwrap(),
            ["[DONE]".to_owned()]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_error_response_gives_its_message() {
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(UNKNOWN).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "answered 404: no router for requested model"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(BAD_REQUEST).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "answered 400: Expected 'messages' to be an array"
        );
        let plain = b"HTTP/1.0 502 Bad Gateway\r\nContent-Length: 4\r\n\r\nEOF\n";
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(plain).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "answered 502: EOF"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_error_in_the_stream_ends_it() {
        let stream = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\n\r\ndata: {\"choices\":[{\"delta\":{\"content\":\"Hi\"}}]}\n\n\
            data: {\"error\":{\"code\":500,\"message\":\"context full\",\"type\":\"server_error\"}}\n\n";
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(stream).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "the server reported an error: context full"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_stream_cut_short_or_without_a_token_is_an_error() {
        let cut = &GATEWAY[..GATEWAY.len() - 20];
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(cut).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "the stream ends before [DONE]"
        );
        let empty = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\n\r\ndata: {\"choices\":[]}\n\ndata: [DONE]\n\n";
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(empty).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "the stream ends without a token"
        );
        let garbled = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\n\r\ndata: {nope\n\n";
        assert_eq!(
            reply_to(garbled).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "malformed event \"{nope\""
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_request_streams_its_usage_and_sends_reasoning_back() {
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&request_body(&request()).unwrap()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            body,
            json!({
                "model": "qwen3.8-27b",
                "messages": [
                    {"role": "system", "content": "Be brief."},
                    {"role": "user", "content": "Hello"},
                    {"role": "assistant", "content": "Hi.", "reasoning_content": "Greet back."},
                    {"role": "user", "content": "Say hello in five words."},
                ],
                "max_tokens": 24,
                "seed": 42,
                "stream": true,
                "stream_options": {"include_usage": true},
                "ignore_eos": true,
            })
        );
        let mut free = request();
        free.ignore_eos = false;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&request_body(&free).unwrap()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(body.get("ignore_eos"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_request_reaches_the_server_and_times_its_reply() {
        let (addr, server) = serve(GATEWAY);
        let reply = OpenAiChat.stream(addr, &request()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(reply.sample.usage.unwrap().completion_tokens, 24);
        let sent = server.join().unwrap();
        assert!(sent.starts_with("POST /v1/chat/completions HTTP/1.0\r\n"));
        assert!(sent.contains("\r\nContent-Type: application/json\r\n"));
        assert!(sent.ends_with("\"stream_options\":{\"include_usage\":true}}"));
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/infrastructure/cmake.rs">
//! Compiles a CMake project: a fresh build tree per build, the output of
//! CMake and the compilers streamed to a build log, and the build's progress
//! read from the step counters Ninja and Make print.

use std::collections::{BTreeMap, VecDeque};
use std::fs::{self, File};
use std::io::{self, BufRead, BufReader, BufWriter, Write};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Command, Stdio};

use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};

use crate::application::ports::{Cmake, CompileJob, Compiled, Progress};

use super::command;
use super::files::remove_dir_if_present;

/// Lines of the log quoted when a step fails: the compiler's error and
/// the build tool's summary.
const TAIL: usize = 20;

/// Build trees under `work_root`, one per project; build logs under
/// `log_dir`, one per project, replaced by each build.
pub struct CmakeBuilder {
    work_root: PathBuf,
    log_dir: PathBuf,
}

impl CmakeBuilder {
    pub fn new(work_root: PathBuf, log_dir: PathBuf) -> Self {
        Self { work_root, log_dir }
    }

    fn build_tree(&self, project: &str) -> PathBuf {
        self.work_root.join(project)
    }

    pub fn log_file(&self, project: &str) -> PathBuf {
        self.log_dir.join(format!("build-{project}.log"))
    }
}

impl Cmake for CmakeBuilder {
    fn compile(&self, job: &CompileJob, progress: &mut dyn FnMut(Progress)) -> Result<Compiled> {
        let tree = self.build_tree(job.project);
        remove_dir_if_present(&tree)?;
        fs::create_dir_all(&tree).with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", tree.display()))?;
        fs::create_dir_all(&self.log_dir)
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", self.log_dir.display()))?;
        let log_path = self.log_file(job.project);
        let file = File::create(&log_path)
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", log_path.display()))?;
        let mut log = Log::new(BufWriter::new(file), &log_path);

        progress(Progress::Configuring);
        let mut configure = Command::new("cmake");
        configure
            .arg("-S")
            .arg(job.source)
            .arg("-B")
            .arg(&tree)
            .args(generator())
            .args(definitions(job.flags));
        log.stream("configure", configure, &mut |_| {})?;

        let jobs = job.jobs.unwrap_or_else(|| {
            std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, std::num::NonZero::get)
        });
        let mut build = Command::new("cmake");
        build.arg("--build").arg(&tree).args([
            "--target",
            job.target,
            "--parallel",
            &jobs.to_string(),
        ]);
        log.stream("build", build, &mut |line| {
            if let Some((done, total)) = parse_progress(line) {
                progress(Progress::Building { done, total });
            }
        })?;
        log.finish()?;

        let artifact = tree.join(job.artifact);
        if !artifact.is_file() {
            bail!(
                "the build succeeded but {} is missing; see {}",
                artifact.display(),
                log_path.display()
            );
        }
        Ok(Compiled {
            artifact,
            log: log_path,
        })
    }

    fn clean(&self, project: &str) -> Result<()> {
        remove_dir_if_present(&self.build_tree(project))
    }
}

/// Ninja when it is installed: its `[n/total]` counters give an exact
/// progress, and it schedules the CUDA compilations better than Make.
fn generator() -> Vec<&'static str> {
    let on_path = |name: &str| {
        std::env::var_os("PATH")
            .is_some_and(|path| std::env::split_paths(&path).any(|dir| dir.join(name).is_file()))
    };
    if on_path("ninja") || on_path("ninja-build") {
        vec!["-G", "Ninja"]
    } else {
        vec![]
    }
}

fn definitions(flags: &BTreeMap<String, String>) -> Vec<String> {
    flags
        .iter()
        .map(|(name, value)| format!("-D{name}={value}"))
        .collect()
}

/// The build log, and the last lines written to it.
struct Log<'a, W: Write> {
    out: W,
    path: &'a Path,
    tail: VecDeque<String>,
}

impl<'a, W: Write> Log<'a, W> {
    fn new(out: W, path: &'a Path) -> Self {
        Self {
            out,
            path,
            tail: VecDeque::with_capacity(TAIL),
        }
    }

    fn write_line(&mut self, line: &str) -> Result<()> {
        writeln!(self.out, "{line}")
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot write {}", self.path.display()))?;
        if self.tail.len() == TAIL {
            self.tail.pop_front();
        }
        self.tail.push_back(line.to_owned());
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Runs `command` with its stdout and stderr merged into the log, in
    /// the order they were printed, and hands each line to `on_line`.
    fn stream(
        &mut self,
        step: &str,
        mut command: Command,
        on_line: &mut dyn FnMut(&str),
    ) -> Result<()> {
        self.write_line(&format!("$ {}", render(&command)))?;
        let (reader, writer) = io::pipe().context("cannot create a pipe")?;
        let stderr = writer.try_clone().context("cannot create a pipe")?;
        command
            .env("LC_ALL", "C")
            .stdin(Stdio::null())
            .stdout(writer)
            .stderr(stderr);
        let mut child = command::spawn(&mut command).map_err(|err| match err.kind() {
            io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
                anyhow::anyhow!("cmake not found; run `sudo dnf install cmake`")
            }
            _ => anyhow::anyhow!("cannot run cmake: {err}"),
        })?;
        // The command holds the pipe's write ends: the reader sees the end
        // of the output only once they are closed.
        drop(command);

        let mut lines = BufReader::new(reader);
        let mut buffer = Vec::new();
        let mut written = Ok(());
        loop {
            buffer.clear();
            match lines.read_until(b'\n', &mut buffer) {
                Ok(0) => break,
                Ok(_) => {
                    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buffer);
                    let line = text.trim_end_matches(['\n', '\r']);
                    on_line(line);
                    // Keep reading after a write error, so the child never
                    // blocks on a full pipe; the error is reported after.
                    if written.is_ok() {
                        written = self.write_line(line);
                    }
                }
                Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => {}
                Err(err) => {
                    written = Err(err).context("cannot read the output of cmake");
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        let status = child.wait().context("cannot wait for cmake")?;
        written?;
        if !status.success() {
            self.out.flush().ok();
            bail!(
                "cmake {step} failed ({status}); the last lines of {}:\n{}",
                self.path.display(),
                self.tail.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n")
            );
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn finish(mut self) -> Result<()> {
        self.out
            .flush()
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot write {}", self.path.display()))
    }
}

/// The command line, for the log.
fn render(command: &Command) -> String {
    std::iter::once(command.get_program())
        .chain(command.get_args())
        .map(|arg| arg.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join(" ")
}

/// A build step counter: Ninja's `[12/356] Building CUDA object …` gives
/// 12 of 356; Make's `[ 45%] Building CXX object …` gives 45 of 100.
pub fn parse_progress(line: &str) -> Option<(u32, u32)> {
    let inside = line.strip_prefix('[')?.split_once(']')?.0;
    if let Some((done, total)) = inside.split_once('/') {
        let (done, total) = (done.trim().parse().ok()?, total.trim().parse().ok()?);
        return (total > 0 && done <= total).then_some((done, total));
    }
    let percent: u32 = inside.strip_suffix('%')?.trim().parse().ok()?;
    (percent <= 100).then_some((percent, 100))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn ninja_counters_give_the_progress() {
        let excerpt = include_str!("../../tests/fixtures/ninja-build-excerpt.txt");
        let progress: Vec<(u32, u32)> = excerpt.lines().filter_map(parse_progress).collect();
        assert_eq!(
            progress,
            [
                (1, 356),
                (2, 356),
                (3, 356),
                (178, 356),
                (179, 356),
                (354, 356),
                (355, 356),
                (356, 356)
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn make_percentages_give_the_progress() {
        assert_eq!(
            parse_progress("[ 45%] Building CXX object src/llama.cpp.o"),
            Some((45, 100))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_progress("[100%] Built target llama-server"),
            Some((100, 100))
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn other_lines_give_no_progress() {
        for line in [
            "",
            "-- Found CUDAToolkit",
            "/src/common/hf-cache.cpp:236:50: warning: [-Wdeprecated-declarations]",
            "[0/0] nothing",
            "[5/3] backwards",
            "[x/356] word",
            "[101%] too far",
            "ninja: build stopped: subcommand failed.",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(parse_progress(line), None, "{line:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn flags_become_cmake_definitions() {
        let flags = BTreeMap::from([
            ("GGML_CUDA".to_owned(), "ON".to_owned()),
            (
                "CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES".to_owned(),
                "120a-real".to_owned(),
            ),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(
            definitions(&flags),
            ["-DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=120a-real", "-DGGML_CUDA=ON"]
        );
    }

    fn shell(script: &str) -> Command {
        let mut command = Command::new("sh");
        command.args(["-c", script]);
        command
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_log_holds_both_streams_in_order() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("build.log");
        let mut lines = Vec::new();
        {
            let mut log = Log::new(File::create(&path).unwrap(), &path);
            log.stream(
                "build",
                shell("echo '[1/2] one'; echo oops >&2; echo '[2/2] two'"),
                &mut |line| lines.push(line.to_owned()),
            )
            .unwrap();
            log.finish().unwrap();
        }
        assert_eq!(lines, ["[1/2] one", "oops", "[2/2] two"]);
        let text = fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(text.starts_with("$ sh -c "), "{text}");
        assert!(text.ends_with("[1/2] one\noops\n[2/2] two\n"), "{text}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_failed_step_quotes_the_end_of_the_log() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("build.log");
        let mut log = Log::new(File::create(&path).unwrap(), &path);
        let script = "for i in $(seq 1 30); do echo line $i; done; echo 'error: boom' >&2; exit 2";
        let message = log
            .stream("build", shell(script), &mut |_| {})
            .unwrap_err()
            .to_string();
        assert!(
            message.starts_with("cmake build failed (exit status: 2)"),
            "{message}"
        );
        assert!(message.contains(&path.display().to_string()), "{message}");
        assert!(message.ends_with("line 30\nerror: boom"), "{message}");
        assert!(!message.contains("line 11\n"), "{message}");
        assert!(message.contains("line 12\n"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_line_without_a_newline_at_the_end_is_kept() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("build.log");
        let mut lines = Vec::new();
        let mut log = Log::new(Vec::new(), &path);
        log.stream("build", shell("printf 'a\\r\\nb'"), &mut |line| {
            lines.push(line.to_owned())
        })
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(lines, ["a", "b"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn cleaning_a_missing_build_tree_is_not_an_error() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let builder = CmakeBuilder::new(dir.path().join("work"), dir.path().join("logs"));
        builder.clean("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        fs::create_dir_all(dir.path().join("work/llama.cpp/bin")).unwrap();
        builder.clean("llama.cpp").unwrap();
        assert!(!dir.path().join("work/llama.cpp").exists());
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/command.rs">
//! Runs an external command and collects its output: C locale, no input.
//! Probes run with a bounded time; other commands take as long as their work.

use std::ffi::OsStr;
use std::io::{self, Read};
use std::path::Path;
use std::process::{Child, Command, Stdio};
use std::thread;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

use anyhow::{Result, bail};

use crate::application::ports::{ProgramOutput, Programs};

/// A probe command that has not exited after this long is killed.
const TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15);
/// A program run by a use case, such as a new build's smoke test, gets more
/// time: a CUDA program initialises the driver first.
const PROGRAM_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(60);
const POLL: Duration = Duration::from_millis(10);

/// What a command printed, and whether it succeeded.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Output {
    pub success: bool,
    pub stdout: String,
    pub stderr: String,
}

impl Output {
    /// The first non-empty line of stderr, or a generic message: why the
    /// command failed, in one line.
    pub fn failure(&self) -> String {
        self.stderr
            .lines()
            .map(str::trim)
            .find(|line| !line.is_empty())
            .unwrap_or("exited with an error and printed nothing")
            .to_owned()
    }
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum RunError {
    /// The program is not installed.
    NotFound,
    Failed(String),
}

/// Runs a probe: `program` with `args`, killed if it has not exited within
/// 15 s.
pub fn run(program: impl AsRef<OsStr>, args: &[&str]) -> Result<Output, RunError> {
    let mut command = Command::new(program);
    command.args(args);
    execute(command, Some(TIMEOUT))
}

/// Runs `command`, prepared by the caller with its arguments, directory and
/// environment, and collects its output; `timeout` bounds its run time. The
/// C locale keeps the output parseable whatever the user's language.
pub fn execute(mut command: Command, timeout: Option<Duration>) -> Result<Output, RunError> {
    let program = command.get_program().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    command
        .env("LC_ALL", "C")
        .stdin(Stdio::null())
        .stdout(Stdio::piped())
        .stderr(Stdio::piped());
    let mut child = spawn(&mut command).map_err(|err| match err.kind() {
        io::ErrorKind::NotFound => RunError::NotFound,
        _ => RunError::Failed(format!("{program}: {err}")),
    })?;

    let stdout = child.stdout.take().map(drain);
    let stderr = child.stderr.take().map(drain);

    let deadline = timeout.map(|timeout| (Instant::now() + timeout, timeout));
    let status = loop {
        match child.try_wait() {
            Ok(Some(status)) => break status,
            Ok(None) => match deadline {
                Some((deadline, timeout)) if Instant::now() >= deadline => {
                    // Best effort: the error below is what matters.
                    let _ = child.kill();
                    let _ = child.wait();
                    return Err(RunError::Failed(format!(
                        "{program} did not answer within {} s",
                        timeout.as_secs()
                    )));
                }
                _ => thread::sleep(POLL),
            },
            Err(err) => return Err(RunError::Failed(err.to_string())),
        }
    };

    Ok(Output {
        success: status.success(),
        stdout: collect(stdout),
        stderr: collect(stderr),
    })
}

/// Starts `command`; every child process of ai-ctl starts here. In the
/// crate's tests, a start waits while a test holds [`exclusive`].
pub fn spawn(command: &mut Command) -> io::Result<Child> {
    #[cfg(test)]
    let _spawning = SPAWNS
        .read()
        .unwrap_or_else(std::sync::PoisonError::into_inner);
    command.spawn()
}

/// Held for reading while a child starts, until it runs its program.
#[cfg(test)]
static SPAWNS: std::sync::RwLock<()> = std::sync::RwLock::new(());

/// For the crate's tests: holds every [`spawn`] back until the guard is
/// dropped. Tests run in parallel threads, and a child forked by one shares
/// every file another holds open until it runs its program: a script being
/// written is then busy (`ETXTBSY`), and a lock or a port just released is
/// still held. A test writing an executable, or releasing a lock or a socket,
/// holds this guard.
#[cfg(test)]
pub fn exclusive() -> std::sync::RwLockWriteGuard<'static, ()> {
    SPAWNS
        .write()
        .unwrap_or_else(std::sync::PoisonError::into_inner)
}

/// Runs programs for the use cases, with a bounded time.
pub struct SystemPrograms;

impl Programs for SystemPrograms {
    fn run(&self, program: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> Result<ProgramOutput> {
        let mut command = Command::new(program);
        command.args(args);
        match execute(command, Some(PROGRAM_TIMEOUT)) {
            Ok(output) => Ok(ProgramOutput {
                success: output.success,
                stdout: output.stdout,
                stderr: output.stderr,
            }),
            Err(RunError::NotFound) => bail!("{} not found", program.display()),
            Err(RunError::Failed(message)) => bail!(message),
        }
    }
}

fn drain(mut pipe: impl Read + Send + 'static) -> thread::JoinHandle<String> {
    thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut bytes = Vec::new();
        // A read error leaves what was read so far, which the parsers reject.
        let _ = pipe.read_to_end(&mut bytes);
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes).into_owned()
    })
}

fn collect(reader: Option<thread::JoinHandle<String>>) -> String {
    reader
        .and_then(|handle| handle.join().ok())
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_program_is_not_found() {
        assert!(matches!(
            run("ai-ctl-no-such-program", &[]),
            Err(RunError::NotFound)
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn output_and_status_are_collected() {
        let output = run("sh", &["-c", "echo out; echo err >&2; exit 3"]).unwrap();
        assert!(!output.success);
        assert_eq!(output.stdout, "out\n");
        assert_eq!(output.failure(), "err");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_locale_is_c() {
        let output = run("sh", &["-c", "echo $LC_ALL"]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(output.stdout.trim(), "C");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_prepared_command_keeps_its_directory_and_environment() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let mut command = Command::new("sh");
        command
            .args(["-c", "pwd; echo $GREETING $LC_ALL"])
            .current_dir(dir.path())
            .env("GREETING", "hello");
        let output = execute(command, None).unwrap();
        let canonical = dir.path().canonicalize().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(output.stdout, format!("{}\nhello C\n", canonical.display()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_command_past_its_timeout_is_killed() {
        let mut command = Command::new("sleep");
        command.arg("5");
        match execute(command, Some(Duration::ZERO)) {
            Err(RunError::Failed(message)) => {
                assert!(message.contains("did not answer"), "{message}")
            }
            other => panic!("{other:?}"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn programs_report_their_output_and_absence() {
        let output = SystemPrograms
            .run(Path::new("sh"), &["-c", "echo out; exit 4"])
            .unwrap();
        assert!(!output.success);
        assert_eq!(output.stdout, "out\n");
        let message = SystemPrograms
            .run(Path::new("/nonexistent/llama-server"), &["--version"])
            .unwrap_err()
            .to_string();
        assert_eq!(message, "/nonexistent/llama-server not found");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_silent_failure_still_explains_itself() {
        let output = run("sh", &["-c", "exit 1"]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(output.failure(), "exited with an error and printed nothing");
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/download.rs">
//! Downloads over HTTPS with `curl`, which every Fedora installs.

use std::path::Path;
use std::process::Command;
use std::time::Duration;

use anyhow::{Result, bail};

use crate::application::ports::Downloads;

use super::command::{RunError, execute};

/// A text file is small: past this, the server is not answering.
const TEXT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(60);

/// Fetches files with `curl`: HTTPS only, redirects included, retried on
/// transient errors, and abandoned when the transfer stalls.
pub struct Curl {
    /// The protocols `curl` may use, for the URL and its redirects.
    protocols: &'static str,
}

impl Curl {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            protocols: "=https",
        }
    }

    fn command(&self) -> Command {
        let mut command = Command::new("curl");
        command
            .args(["--fail", "--silent", "--show-error", "--location"])
            .args(["--proto", self.protocols, "--proto-redir", self.protocols])
            .args(["--retry", "3", "--connect-timeout", "30"])
            // Slower than 1 KiB/s for a minute: the transfer is stalled.
            .args(["--speed-limit", "1024", "--speed-time", "60"]);
        command
    }

    fn run(command: Command, url: &str, timeout: Option<Duration>) -> Result<String> {
        match execute(command, timeout) {
            Ok(output) if output.success => Ok(output.stdout),
            Ok(output) => bail!("cannot download {url}: {}", output.failure()),
            Err(RunError::NotFound) => bail!("cannot download {url}: curl is not installed"),
            Err(RunError::Failed(message)) => bail!("cannot download {url}: {message}"),
        }
    }
}

impl Default for Curl {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl Downloads for Curl {
    fn download(&self, url: &str, to: &Path) -> Result<()> {
        let mut command = self.command();
        command.arg("--output").arg(to).arg("--").arg(url);
        Self::run(command, url, None).map(drop)
    }

    fn text(&self, url: &str) -> Result<String> {
        let mut command = self.command();
        command.args(["--", url]);
        Self::run(command, url, Some(TEXT_TIMEOUT))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::fs;

    use super::*;

    /// `curl` allowed to read local files, so no test needs the network.
    fn local() -> Curl {
        Curl {
            protocols: "=https,file",
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_file_is_saved_where_asked() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let source = root.path().join("archive.tar.gz");
        fs::write(&source, "archive").unwrap();
        let target = root.path().join("staging/archive.tar.gz");
        fs::create_dir(root.path().join("staging")).unwrap();
        local()
            .download(&format!("file://{}", source.display()), &target)
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(target).unwrap(), "archive");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_text_file_is_returned() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let source = root.path().join("checksums.txt");
        fs::write(&source, "digest  archive\n").unwrap();
        let text = local()
            .text(&format!("file://{}", source.display()))
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(text, "digest  archive\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_file_is_an_error_naming_the_url() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let url = format!("file://{}/absent.txt", root.path().display());
        let message = local().text(&url).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(
            message.starts_with(&format!("cannot download {url}: curl: (37)")),
            "{message}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_https_is_allowed() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let target = root.path().join("file");
        for url in ["http://127.0.0.1:9/file", "file:///etc/hostname"] {
            let message = Curl::new().download(url, &target).unwrap_err().to_string();
            assert!(
                message.starts_with(&format!("cannot download {url}: curl: (1)")),
                "{message}"
            );
        }
        assert!(!target.exists());
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/files.rs">
//! File system helpers the adapters share.

use std::any::Any;
use std::fs::{self, File, TryLockError};
use std::io;
use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::{Context, Result, anyhow};

/// Removes `dir` and its content; a missing `dir` is not an error.
pub fn remove_dir_if_present(dir: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    match fs::remove_dir_all(dir) {
        Err(err) if err.kind() != io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot remove {}", dir.display()))
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}

/// Takes the lock file `.lock` of `dir`, creating both if needed; the lock is
/// released when the returned guard is dropped. Fails at once when another
/// process holds it, saying that another ai-ctl is `busy`.
pub fn lock(dir: &Path, busy: &str) -> Result<Box<dyn Any>> {
    fs::create_dir_all(dir).with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", dir.display()))?;
    let path = dir.join(".lock");
    let file = File::options()
        .create(true)
        .truncate(false)
        .write(true)
        .open(&path)
        .with_context(|| format!("cannot open {}", path.display()))?;
    match file.try_lock() {
        Ok(()) => Ok(Box::new(file)),
        Err(TryLockError::WouldBlock) => Err(anyhow!(
            "another ai-ctl is {busy} ({} is locked)",
            path.display()
        )),
        Err(TryLockError::Error(err)) => {
            Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot lock {}", path.display()))
        }
    }
}

/// Points the symbolic link `link` at `target` by renaming `temporary`, a
/// link made beside it, over it: the link is never missing.
pub fn replace_link(link: &Path, target: &str, temporary: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    let _ = fs::remove_file(temporary);
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(target, temporary)
        .with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", temporary.display()))?;
    if let Err(err) = fs::rename(temporary, link) {
        let _ = fs::remove_file(temporary);
        return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot update {}", link.display()));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Removes the hidden entries of `dir` — files, links and directories whose
/// name starts with `.` — but not the lock file. A missing `dir` has none.
pub fn remove_hidden(dir: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    let entries = match fs::read_dir(dir) {
