    fn compiler_cache_wrappers_are_skipped_for_the_compiler_behind_them() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let (wrappers, bin) = (root.path().join("ccache"), root.path().join("bin"));
        fs::create_dir_all(&wrappers).unwrap();
        fs::create_dir_all(&bin).unwrap();
        script(&bin.join("ccache"), "echo 16.2.1");
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(bin.join("ccache"), wrappers.join("g++")).unwrap();
        script(&bin.join("g++"), "echo 16.2.1");
        script(&bin.join("g++-15"), "echo 15.3.1");

        let host = SystemHost {
            path: Some(std::env::join_paths([&wrappers, &bin]).unwrap()),
            power_supplies: root.path().join("power"),
            os_release: root.path().join("os-release"),
        };
        let Probed::Found(compilers) = host.host_compilers() else {
            panic!("no compiler found");
        };
        let found: Vec<_> = compilers
            .iter()
            .map(|c| (c.command.as_str(), c.path.clone(), c.version))
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(
            found,
            [
                ("g++", bin.join("g++"), Version::new(16, 2, 1)),
                ("g++-15", bin.join("g++-15"), Version::new(15, 3, 1)),
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_fedora_release_comes_from_os_release() {
        assert_eq!(
            parse_fedora_release(&fixture("os-release-fedora44.txt")),
            Some(44)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_fedora_release("ID=\"fedora\"\nVERSION_ID='45'\n"),
            Some(45)
        );
        assert_eq!(parse_fedora_release("ID=ubuntu\nVERSION_ID=24.04\n"), None);
        assert_eq!(
            parse_fedora_release("ID=fedora\nVERSION_ID=rawhide\n"),
            None
        );
        assert_eq!(parse_fedora_release(""), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn os_release_is_read_from_its_file() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let host = SystemHost {
            path: None,
            power_supplies: root.path().join("power"),
            os_release: root.path().join("os-release"),
        };
        assert_eq!(host.fedora_release(), Probed::Missing);
        fs::write(
            root.path().join("os-release"),
            fixture("os-release-fedora44.txt"),
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(host.fedora_release(), Probed::Found(44));
    }

    #[test]
    fn repolist_gives_the_repository_ids() {
        let ids = parse_repolist(&fixture("dnf-repolist-enabled.txt"));
        assert_eq!(ids.len(), 14);
        assert_eq!(ids[1], "copr:copr.fedorainfracloud.org:phracek:PyCharm");
        let cuda: Vec<&String> = ids.iter().filter(|id| id.starts_with("cuda-")).collect();
        assert_eq!(cuda, ["cuda-fedora44-x86_64"]);
        assert!(parse_repolist("").is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn package_names_are_sorted_once_each() {
        let names = parse_package_names(&fixture("dnf-repoquery-cuda-driver-packages.txt"));
        assert_eq!(names.len(), 12);
        assert_eq!(names[0], "kmod-nvidia-latest-dkms");
        assert_eq!(parse_package_names("b\na\n\nb\n"), ["a", "b"]);
        let installed = parse_package_names(&fixture("rpm-cuda-toolkit-packages.txt"));
        assert_eq!(
            installed,
            [
                "cuda-toolkit-13-4",
                "cuda-toolkit-13-4-config-common",
                "cuda-toolkit-13-config-common",
                "cuda-toolkit-config-common"
            ]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn gxx_names_are_recognised_and_ordered() {
        let mut names = vec![
            "g++-14",
            "g++",
            "g++-15",
            "g++-x",
            "gcc",
            "g++-",
            "x86_64-redhat-linux-g++-15",
        ];
        names.retain(|name| is_gxx(name));
        names.sort_by_key(|name| gxx_order(name));
        assert_eq!(names, ["g++", "g++-15", "g++-14"]);
    }

    fn supply(root: &Path, name: &str, kind: &str, online: Option<&str>) {
        let dir = root.join(name);
        fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        fs::write(dir.join("type"), format!("{kind}\n")).unwrap();
        if let Some(online) = online {
            fs::write(dir.join("online"), format!("{online}\n")).unwrap();
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn ac_is_online_when_any_external_supply_is() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        supply(root.path(), "ADP0", "Mains", Some("0"));
        supply(root.path(), "BAT0", "Battery", None);
        supply(root.path(), "ucsi-source-psy-USBC000:001", "USB", Some("1"));
        assert_eq!(ac_online(root.path()), Probed::Found(true));
    }

    #[test]
    fn ac_is_offline_on_battery() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        supply(root.path(), "ADP0", "Mains", Some("0"));
        supply(root.path(), "BAT0", "Battery", Some("1"));
        assert_eq!(ac_online(root.path()), Probed::Found(false));
    }

    #[test]
    fn ac_is_unknown_without_external_supplies() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        supply(root.path(), "BAT0", "Battery", None);
        assert_eq!(ac_online(root.path()), Probed::Missing);
        assert_eq!(ac_online(&root.path().join("absent")), Probed::Missing);
    }

    #[test]
    fn host_config_is_found_in_either_layout() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(read_max_gcc_major(root.path()), Probed::Missing);
        let header = root.path().join("include/crt/host_config.h");
        fs::create_dir_all(header.parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
        fs::write(&header, "#if __GNUC__ > 14\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(read_max_gcc_major(root.path()), Probed::Found(14));
        fs::write(&header, "nothing\n").unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(read_max_gcc_major(root.path()), Probed::Failed(_)));
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/http.rs">
//! A minimal HTTP/1.0 client for the servers of the loopback: llama-swap,
//! and the llama-servers it starts. One request per connection; a body runs
//! to its `Content-Length`, to its last chunk, or until the server closes the
//! connection. llama-swap answers HTTP/1.0 without chunks, a log stream
//! included; llama-server answers HTTP/1.1 and chunks a stream even to an
//! HTTP/1.0 request, so a body read as it arrives is decoded. Both listen on
//! the loopback, in plain HTTP, so the standard library's sockets suffice.

use std::io::{self, Read, Write};
use std::net::{SocketAddr, TcpStream};
use std::time::Duration;

use anyhow::{Context, Result, anyhow, bail, ensure};

/// Connecting to a local port succeeds or is refused at once; longer means
/// a firewall or an address that does not route.
const CONNECT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2);
/// Headers longer than this are not those of a server ai-ctl talks to.
const MAX_HEAD: usize = 64 * 1024;
/// A chunk-size line longer than this is not one.
const MAX_CHUNK_LINE: usize = 1024;

/// A response: its status code and its body.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Response {
    pub status: u16,
    pub body: Vec<u8>,
}

/// The status line and headers of a response.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Head {
    pub status: u16,
    /// The length of the body, when a `Content-Length` gives it.
    pub length: Option<usize>,
    /// Whether the body comes in chunks.
    pub chunked: bool,
}

/// A connection to `addr`, `None` when nothing listens there; the request
/// sent, with `json` as its body, waiting `timeout` at most for each read
/// and write, or forever.
pub fn send(
    addr: SocketAddr,
    method: &str,
    path: &str,
    json: Option<&[u8]>,
    timeout: Option<Duration>,
) -> Result<Option<TcpStream>> {
    let mut stream = match TcpStream::connect_timeout(&addr, CONNECT_TIMEOUT) {
        Ok(stream) => stream,
        Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::ConnectionRefused => return Ok(None),
        Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot connect to {addr}")),
    };
    let mut write = || -> io::Result<()> {
        stream.set_read_timeout(timeout)?;
        stream.set_write_timeout(timeout)?;
        let body = json.unwrap_or_default();
        let content_type = if json.is_some() {
            "Content-Type: application/json\r\n"
        } else {
            ""
        };
        let head = format!(
            "{method} {path} HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: {addr}\r\nConnection: close\r\n{content_type}Content-Length: {}\r\n\r\n",
            body.len()
        );
        stream.write_all(&[head.as_bytes(), body].concat())
    };
    write().with_context(|| format!("{method} http://{addr}{path}"))?;
    Ok(Some(stream))
}

/// Sends `method path` to `addr` and reads the response within `timeout`;
/// `None` when nothing listens on `addr`.
pub fn request(
    addr: SocketAddr,
    method: &str,
    path: &str,
    timeout: Duration,
) -> Result<Option<Response>> {
    let Some(mut stream) = send(addr, method, path, None, Some(timeout))? else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    stream
        .read_to_end(&mut bytes)
        .with_context(|| format!("{method} http://{addr}{path}"))?;
    parse(&bytes)
        .map(Some)
        .with_context(|| format!("{method} http://{addr}{path}"))
}

/// Where the headers of `bytes` end, before the blank line.
fn head_end(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<usize> {
    bytes.windows(4).position(|window| window == b"\r\n\r\n")
}

/// Parses the status line and the headers of a response, without the blank
/// line.
fn parse_head(head: &[u8]) -> Result<Head> {
    let head = std::str::from_utf8(head).context("the response headers are not text")?;
    let mut lines = head.split("\r\n");
    let status_line = lines.next().unwrap_or_default();
    let status = match status_line.split(' ').collect::<Vec<_>>()[..] {
        [version, code, ..] if version.starts_with("HTTP/1.") => code.parse::<u16>().ok(),
        _ => None,
    }
    .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("malformed status line {status_line:?}"))?;
    let mut parsed = Head {
        status,
        length: None,
        chunked: false,
    };
    for line in lines {
        let Some((name, value)) = line.split_once(':') else {
            bail!("malformed header {line:?}");
        };
        let value = value.trim();
        if name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("transfer-encoding") {
            if value.eq_ignore_ascii_case("chunked") {
                parsed.chunked = true;
            } else if !value.eq_ignore_ascii_case("identity") {
                bail!("unexpected transfer encoding {value:?}");
            }
        }
        if name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("content-length") {
            parsed.length = Some(
                value
                    .parse()
                    .map_err(|_| anyhow!("malformed Content-Length {value:?}"))?,
            );
        }
    }
    Ok(parsed)
}

/// Parses a whole HTTP/1.0 response, as llama-swap sends it: a
/// `Content-Length` bounds the body, and chunks are refused.
pub fn parse(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Response> {
    let end = head_end(bytes).ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("the response ends before its headers do"))?;
    let head = parse_head(&bytes[..end])?;
    ensure!(
        !head.chunked,
        "unexpected transfer encoding \"chunked\" in an HTTP/1.0 response"
    );
    let mut body = bytes[end + 4..].to_vec();
    if let Some(length) = head.length {
        if body.len() < length {
            bail!(
                "the response ends after {} of its {length} bytes",
                body.len()
            );
        }
        body.truncate(length);
    }
    Ok(Response {
        status: head.status,
        body,
    })
}

/// Reads the head of a response from `stream`: the head, and the bytes of
/// the body read with it.
pub fn read_head(stream: &mut dyn Read) -> Result<(Head, Vec<u8>)> {
    let mut received = Vec::new();
    let mut buffer = [0; 8192];
    let end = loop {
        if let Some(end) = head_end(&received) {
            break end;
        }
        ensure!(
            received.len() <= MAX_HEAD,
            "the response headers exceed {MAX_HEAD} bytes"
        );
        let read = stream
            .read(&mut buffer)
            .context("cannot read the response")?;
        ensure!(read > 0, "the response ends before its headers do");
        received.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..read]);
    };
    let head = parse_head(&received[..end])?;
    Ok((head, received.split_off(end + 4)))
}

/// The body of a response, read as it arrives.
pub struct Body<'a> {
    stream: &'a mut dyn Read,
    /// Bytes read with the head, not given yet.
    early: Vec<u8>,
    framing: Framing,
}

enum Framing {
    /// Until the server closes the connection.
    Close,
    /// This many bytes still to come.
    Length(usize),
    Chunked(Dechunker),
}

impl<'a> Body<'a> {
    /// The body that follows `head` on `stream`, `early` its first bytes.
    pub fn new(stream: &'a mut dyn Read, head: &Head, early: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
        let framing = if head.chunked {
            Framing::Chunked(Dechunker::default())
        } else {
            head.length.map_or(Framing::Close, Framing::Length)
        };
        Self {
            stream,
            early,
            framing,
        }
    }

    /// The next bytes of the body, as they arrive; `None` at its end.
    pub fn next_bytes(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>> {
        loop {
            if matches!(self.framing, Framing::Length(0))
                || matches!(&self.framing, Framing::Chunked(chunks) if chunks.is_done())
            {
                return Ok(None);
            }
            let mut raw = std::mem::take(&mut self.early);
            if raw.is_empty() {
                let mut buffer = [0; 8192];
                let read = self
                    .stream
                    .read(&mut buffer)
                    .context("cannot read the response")?;
                raw.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..read]);
            }
            let end = raw.is_empty();
            match &mut self.framing {
                Framing::Close if end => return Ok(None),
                Framing::Close => return Ok(Some(raw)),
                Framing::Length(remaining) => {
                    ensure!(!end, "the response ends {remaining} bytes before its end");
                    raw.truncate(*remaining);
                    *remaining -= raw.len();
                    return Ok(Some(raw));
                }
                Framing::Chunked(chunks) => {
                    ensure!(!end, "the response ends inside its chunks");
                    let mut data = Vec::new();
                    chunks.feed(&raw, &mut data)?;
                    if !data.is_empty() {
                        return Ok(Some(data));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /// The rest of the body, `limit` bytes at most.
    pub fn read_to(&mut self, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
        let mut body = Vec::new();
        while body.len() < limit {
            match self.next_bytes()? {
                Some(bytes) => body.extend_from_slice(&bytes),
                None => break,
            }
        }
        body.truncate(limit);
        Ok(body)
    }
}

/// Decodes a chunked body as it arrives.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct Dechunker {
    state: Chunk,
    /// The line being read: a chunk's size, the end of its data, a trailer.
    line: Vec<u8>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Chunk {
    #[default]
    Size,
    /// This many bytes of data still to come.
    Data(usize),
    /// The line break that ends a chunk's data.
    DataEnd,
    /// The trailer after the last chunk, until a blank line.
    Trailer,
    Done,
}

impl Dechunker {
    /// Appends the data that `input` carries to `out`.
    pub fn feed(&mut self, mut input: &[u8], out: &mut Vec<u8>) -> Result<()> {
        while !input.is_empty() && self.state != Chunk::Done {
            if let Chunk::Data(remaining) = &mut self.state {
                let taken = input.len().min(*remaining);
                out.extend_from_slice(&input[..taken]);
                input = &input[taken..];
                *remaining -= taken;
                if *remaining == 0 {
                    self.state = Chunk::DataEnd;
                }
                continue;
            }
            let Some(newline) = input.iter().position(|&b| b == b'\n') else {
                self.line.extend_from_slice(input);
                ensure!(
                    self.line.len() <= MAX_CHUNK_LINE,
                    "malformed chunk: a line exceeds {MAX_CHUNK_LINE} bytes"
                );
                return Ok(());
            };
            self.line.extend_from_slice(&input[..newline]);
            input = &input[newline + 1..];
            let line = std::mem::take(&mut self.line);
            let line = line.strip_suffix(b"\r").unwrap_or(&line);
            self.state = match self.state {
                Chunk::Size => match chunk_size(line)? {
                    0 => Chunk::Trailer,
                    size => Chunk::Data(size),
                },
                Chunk::DataEnd if line.is_empty() => Chunk::Size,
                Chunk::DataEnd => bail!("malformed chunk: its data runs past its size"),
                Chunk::Trailer if line.is_empty() => Chunk::Done,
                _ => Chunk::Trailer,
            };
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Whether the last chunk and the trailer have been read.
    pub fn is_done(&self) -> bool {
        self.state == Chunk::Done
    }
}

/// The size a chunk-size line gives, in hexadecimal before any extension.
fn chunk_size(line: &[u8]) -> Result<usize> {
    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(line);
    let digits = text.split(';').next().unwrap_or_default().trim();
    usize::from_str_radix(digits, 16).map_err(|_| anyhow!("malformed chunk size {text:?}"))
}

/// Reads a response from `stream`, copying its body to `out` as it comes
/// until the server closes the connection. `None` once a 200 response is
/// copied; any other response is returned instead, its body cut at
/// `error_limit` bytes.
pub fn copy_stream(
    stream: &mut dyn Read,
    out: &mut dyn Write,
    error_limit: usize,
) -> Result<Option<Response>> {
    let (head, early) = read_head(stream)?;
    let mut body = Body::new(stream, &head, early);
    if head.status != 200 {
        let body = body.read_to(error_limit)?;
        return Ok(Some(Response {
            status: head.status,
            body,
        }));
    }
    while let Some(bytes) = body.next_bytes()? {
        out.write_all(&bytes)
            .and_then(|()| out.flush())
            .context("cannot write the stream")?;
    }
    Ok(None)
}

/// A server for tests: it answers one request on a free port of the
/// loopback with `response`, and returns the request.
#[cfg(test)]
pub fn serve(response: &'static [u8]) -> (SocketAddr, std::thread::JoinHandle<String>) {
    use std::net::TcpListener;

    let listener = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
    let addr = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
    let server = std::thread::spawn(move || {
        let (mut stream, _) = listener.accept().unwrap();
        let (mut request, mut buffer) = (Vec::new(), [0; 4096]);
        let expected = loop {
            if let Some(end) = head_end(&request) {
                let head = String::from_utf8_lossy(&request[..end]).to_lowercase();
                let length = head
                    .lines()
                    .find_map(|line| line.strip_prefix("content-length:"))
                    .map_or(0, |value| value.trim().parse::<usize>().unwrap());
                break end + 4 + length;
            }
            let n = stream.read(&mut buffer).unwrap();
            request.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..n]);
        };
        while request.len() < expected {
            let n = stream.read(&mut buffer).unwrap();
            request.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..n]);
        }
        stream.write_all(response).unwrap();
        String::from_utf8(request).unwrap()
    });
    (addr, server)
}

/// A reader for tests that gives `bytes` a few at a time, as a socket may.
#[cfg(test)]
pub struct Trickle<'a>(pub &'a [u8]);

#[cfg(test)]
impl Read for Trickle<'_> {
    fn read(&mut self, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
        let n = self.0.len().min(buffer.len()).min(3);
        buffer[..n].copy_from_slice(&self.0[..n]);
        self.0 = &self.0[n..];
        Ok(n)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::net::TcpListener;

    use super::*;

    const HEALTH: &[u8] = include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-health.http");
    const RUNNING: &[u8] = include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-running-empty.http");
    const UNLOAD: &[u8] = include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-unload.http");
    /// `/logs/stream/fake` of a gateway whose model `fake` answered one
    /// request, read for a second, then disconnected.
    const STREAM: &[u8] = include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-logs-stream.http");
    /// `/logs/stream/nope`, for a model the gateway does not have.
    const UNKNOWN: &[u8] =
        include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-swap-263-logs-stream-unknown.http");
    const STREAMED: &str = "fake: loading model\n\
         fake: \"GET /health HTTP/1.1\" 200 -\n\
         fake: \"POST /v1/chat/completions HTTP/1.1\" 200 -\n\
         fake: \"GET /v1/models HTTP/1.1\" 200 -\n";
    /// A streamed chat completion from llama-server, in chunks.
    const CHUNKED: &[u8] =
        include_bytes!("../../tests/fixtures/llama-server-b11538-chat-stream.http");

    #[test]
    fn a_health_response_parses() {
        let response = parse(HEALTH).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(response.status, 200);
        assert_eq!(response.body, b"OK");
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unload_response_parses() {
        let response = parse(UNLOAD).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(response.status, 200);
        assert_eq!(response.body, b"{\"msg\":\"ok\"}\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_body_stops_at_its_content_length() {
        let response = parse(RUNNING).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(response.body, b"{\"running\":[]}\n");
        let mut padded = RUNNING.to_vec();
        padded.extend_from_slice(b"trailing");
        assert_eq!(parse(&padded).unwrap().body, response.body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_truncated_or_malformed_response_is_an_error() {
        let cases: [(&[u8], &str); 6] = [
            (&HEALTH[..20], "the response ends before its headers do"),
            (
                &HEALTH[..HEALTH.len() - 1],
                "the response ends after 1 of its 2 bytes",
            ),
            (
                b"SSH-2.0-OpenSSH\r\n\r\n",
                "malformed status line \"SSH-2.0-OpenSSH\"",
            ),
            (
                b"HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\nTransfer-Encoding: chunked\r\n\r\n2\r\nOK\r\n0\r\n\r\n",
                "unexpected transfer encoding \"chunked\" in an HTTP/1.0 response",
            ),
            (
                b"HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\nTransfer-Encoding: gzip\r\n\r\n",
                "unexpected transfer encoding \"gzip\"",
            ),
            (
                b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-Length: two\r\n\r\nOK",
                "malformed Content-Length \"two\"",
            ),
        ];
        for (bytes, expected) in cases {
            assert_eq!(parse(bytes).unwrap_err().to_string(), expected);
        }
    }

    /// The whole body of `bytes`, read as it arrives.
    fn body_of(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
        let mut stream = Trickle(bytes);
        let (head, early) = read_head(&mut stream)?;
        Body::new(&mut stream, &head, early).read_to(usize::MAX)
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_chunked_body_is_decoded_as_it_arrives() {
        let body = String::from_utf8(body_of(CHUNKED).unwrap()).unwrap();
        assert!(body.starts_with("data: {\"choices\":[{\"finish_reason\":null"));
        assert!(body.ends_with("data: [DONE]\n\n"));
        assert!(!body.contains("\r\n"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_body_runs_to_its_length_or_to_the_close() {
        let sized = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-Length: 2\r\n\r\nOKtrailing";
        assert_eq!(body_of(sized).unwrap(), b"OK");
        let closed = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\n\r\nall of it";
        assert_eq!(body_of(closed).unwrap(), b"all of it");
        let empty = b"HTTP/1.0 204 No Content\r\nContent-Length: 0\r\n\r\n";
        assert_eq!(body_of(empty).unwrap(), b"");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_body_cut_short_is_an_error() {
        let sized = b"HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-Length: 5\r\n\r\nOK";
        assert_eq!(
            body_of(sized).unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "the response ends 3 bytes before its end"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            body_of(&CHUNKED[..CHUNKED.len() - 5])
                .unwrap_err()
                .to_string(),
            "the response ends inside its chunks"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn chunks_split_anywhere_decode_alike() {
        let encoded = b"4;name=value\r\nWiki\r\n5\r\npedia\r\nE\r\n in\r\n\r\nchunks.\r\n0\r\nExpires: never\r\n\r\n";
        for split in 0..encoded.len() {
            let (mut chunks, mut out) = (Dechunker::default(), Vec::new());
            chunks.feed(&encoded[..split], &mut out).unwrap();
            chunks.feed(&encoded[split..], &mut out).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(out, b"Wikipedia in\r\n\r\nchunks.", "split at {split}");
            assert!(chunks.is_done());
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn malformed_chunks_are_errors() {
        let cases: [(&[u8], &str); 3] = [
            (b"zz\r\n", "malformed chunk size \"zz\""),
            (
                b"2\r\nOKK\r\n",
                "malformed chunk: its data runs past its size",
            ),
            (&[b'1'; 2000], "malformed chunk: a line exceeds 1024 bytes"),
        ];
        for (bytes, expected) in cases {
            let error = Dechunker::default()
                .feed(bytes, &mut Vec::new())
                .unwrap_err();
            assert_eq!(error.to_string(), expected);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_stream_is_copied_until_the_server_closes_it() {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        assert_eq!(
            copy_stream(&mut Trickle(STREAM), &mut out, 1024).unwrap(),
            None
        );
        assert_eq!(String::from_utf8(out).unwrap(), STREAMED);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_stream_refused_gives_its_response() {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let response = copy_stream(&mut Trickle(UNKNOWN), &mut out, 1024)
            .unwrap()
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(response.status, 400);
        assert!(
            String::from_utf8(response.body)
                .unwrap()
                .contains("invalid logger. Use 'proxy', 'upstream', 'http' or a model's ID")
        );
        assert!(out.is_empty());
        let cut = copy_stream(&mut Trickle(UNKNOWN), &mut out, 10)
            .unwrap()
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(cut.body.len(), 10);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_stream_cut_before_its_headers_is_an_error() {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        assert_eq!(
            copy_stream(&mut Trickle(&STREAM[..30]), &mut out, 1024)
                .unwrap_err()
                .to_string(),
            "the response ends before its headers do"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn nothing_listening_is_not_an_error() {
        // A child forked while the port is bound would keep it listening.
        let _releasing = crate::infrastructure::command::exclusive();
        let addr = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
            .unwrap()
            .local_addr()
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            request(addr, "GET", "/health", Duration::from_secs(2)).unwrap(),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_request_reaches_the_server_and_reads_its_answer() {
        let (addr, server) = serve(HEALTH);
        let response = request(addr, "GET", "/health", Duration::from_secs(2))
            .unwrap()
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(response.body, b"OK");
        assert_eq!(
            server.join().unwrap(),
            format!(
                "GET /health HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: {addr}\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: 0\r\n\r\n"
            )
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_json_body_is_sent_with_its_type_and_length() {
        let (addr, server) = serve(HEALTH);
        let mut stream = send(addr, "POST", "/v1/x", Some(b"{\"a\":1}"), None)
            .unwrap()
            .unwrap();
        stream.read_to_end(&mut Vec::new()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            server.join().unwrap(),
            format!(
                "POST /v1/x HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: {addr}\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Type: application/json\r\nContent-Length: 7\r\n\r\n{{\"a\":1}}"
            )
        );
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/infrastructure/inventory_file.rs">
//! The inventory as a TOML file: parsing, and installing the shipped one.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};
use std::io::{self, Write};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use anyhow::{Context, Result, anyhow, bail, ensure};
use serde::Deserialize;

use crate::application::ports::{Installed, InventoryStore};
use crate::domain::inventory::{
    Backend, BackendKind, BackendSource, FlagValue, Flags, Gateway, Inventory, InventoryParts,
    Model,
};

/// The inventory shipped with ai-ctl, `deploy/inventory.toml`.
pub const SHIPPED: &str = include_str!("../../deploy/inventory.toml");
/// Replaced by the models directory when the shipped inventory is installed.
const PLACEHOLDER: &str = "${MODELS_DIR}";
/// The format version this ai-ctl reads.
const VERSION: u32 = 1;

/// The installed inventory, at a fixed path.
pub struct InventoryFile {
    path: PathBuf,
}

impl InventoryFile {
    pub fn new(path: PathBuf) -> Self {
        Self { path }
    }
}

impl InventoryStore for InventoryFile {
    fn location(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.path
    }

    fn load(&self) -> Result<Option<Inventory>> {
        let text = match fs::read_to_string(&self.path) {
            Ok(text) => text,
            Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(None),
            Err(err) => {
                return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", self.path.display()));
            }
        };
        parse(&text)
            .map(Some)
            .with_context(|| format!("invalid inventory {}", self.path.display()))
    }

    fn install(&self, models_dir: &Path) -> Result<Installed> {
        let models_dir = std::path::absolute(models_dir)
            .with_context(|| format!("cannot resolve {}", models_dir.display()))?;
        match fs::metadata(&models_dir) {
            Ok(metadata) if metadata.is_dir() => {}
            Ok(_) => bail!("{} is not a directory", models_dir.display()),
            Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => bail!(
                "{} does not exist; create it first (models are downloaded into it)",
                models_dir.display()
            ),
            Err(err) => {
                return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot read {}", models_dir.display()));
            }
        }
        let text = render(SHIPPED, &models_dir)?;
        parse(&text).context("the shipped inventory is invalid")?;
        write_new(&self.path, &text)
    }
}

/// Fills the models directory into `template`, escaped for a TOML string.
pub fn render(template: &str, models_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    let dir = models_dir
        .to_str()
        .with_context(|| format!("{} is not valid UTF-8", models_dir.display()))?;
    ensure!(
        template.contains(PLACEHOLDER),
        "the inventory template has no {PLACEHOLDER} placeholder"
    );
    Ok(template.replace(PLACEHOLDER, &toml_escape(dir)))
}

/// Escapes `text` for the inside of a TOML basic string.
fn toml_escape(text: &str) -> String {
    let mut escaped = String::with_capacity(text.len());
    for c in text.chars() {
        match c {
            '\\' => escaped.push_str(r"\\"),
            '"' => escaped.push_str("\\\""),
            c if c.is_control() => escaped.push_str(&format!("\\u{:04X}", u32::from(c))),
            c => escaped.push(c),
        }
    }
    escaped
}

/// Writes `text` to `path` unless a file is already there. The content goes
/// to a temporary file first, then a hard link publishes it: the link fails
/// if `path` exists, so a concurrent `init` or a crash never leaves a partial
/// or overwritten inventory.
fn write_new(path: &Path, text: &str) -> Result<Installed> {
    if path.symlink_metadata().is_ok() {
        return Ok(Installed::AlreadyExists);
    }
    let dir = path
        .parent()
        .with_context(|| format!("{} has no parent directory", path.display()))?;
    fs::create_dir_all(dir).with_context(|| format!("cannot create {}", dir.display()))?;

    let name = path
        .file_name()
        .with_context(|| format!("{} has no file name", path.display()))?;
    let temporary = dir.join(format!(
        ".{}.{}.tmp",
        name.to_string_lossy(),
        std::process::id()
    ));
    let written = write_synced(&temporary, text);
    let published = written.and_then(|()| fs::hard_link(&temporary, path));
    // The temporary file is never needed afterwards; failing to remove it
    // leaves a stray hidden file, not a wrong inventory.
    let _ = fs::remove_file(&temporary);
    match published {
        Ok(()) => Ok(Installed::Written),
        Err(err) if err.kind() == io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => Ok(Installed::AlreadyExists),
        Err(err) => Err(err).with_context(|| format!("cannot write {}", path.display())),
    }
}

fn write_synced(path: &Path, text: &str) -> io::Result<()> {
    let mut file = OpenOptions::new()
        .write(true)
        .create(true)
        .truncate(true)
        .open(path)?;
    file.write_all(text.as_bytes())?;
    file.sync_all()
}

/// Parses and validates an inventory.
pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Inventory> {
    let file: FileDto = toml::from_str(text)?;
    ensure!(
        file.version == VERSION,
        "unsupported inventory version {} (this ai-ctl reads version {VERSION})",
        file.version
    );

    let gateway = file.gateway.unwrap_or_default();
    let default = Gateway::default();
    let gateway = Gateway {
        listen: match gateway.listen {
            Some(listen) => listen.parse().map_err(|_| {
                anyhow!("gateway.listen `{listen}` is not an address such as 127.0.0.1:8080")
            })?,
            None => default.listen,
        },
        vram_margin_mib: gateway.vram_margin_mib.unwrap_or(default.vram_margin_mib),
    };

    let mut defaults = BTreeMap::new();
    for (kind, flags) in file.defaults {
        let kind = backend_kind(&kind).with_context(|| format!("[defaults.{kind}]"))?;
        defaults.insert(kind, flag_values(flags, &format!("defaults.{kind}"))?);
    }

    let backends = file
        .backends
        .into_iter()
        .map(|backend| {
            let kind =
                backend_kind(&backend.kind).with_context(|| format!("backend `{}`", backend.id))?;
            Ok(Backend {
                id: backend.id,
                kind,
                source: match backend.source {
                    SourceDto::Build(project) => BackendSource::Build(project),
                    SourceDto::Image(reference) => BackendSource::Image(reference),
                },
            })
        })
        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?;

    let models = file
        .models
        .into_iter()
        .map(|model| {
            let flags = flag_values(model.flags, &format!("model `{}`", model.id))?;
            Ok(Model {
                id: model.id,
                backend: model.backend,
                repo: model.repo,
                dir: model.dir,
                files: model.files,
                flags,
                ctx_size: model.ctx_size,
                vram_mib: model.vram_mib,
                ttl: model.ttl,
                preload: model.preload,
            })
        })
        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?;

    Ok(Inventory::new(InventoryParts {
        models_dir: file.models_dir,
        gateway,
        defaults,
        backends,
        models,
    })?)
}

fn backend_kind(name: &str) -> Result<BackendKind> {
    BackendKind::from_name(name).ok_or_else(|| {
        let known: Vec<&str> = BackendKind::ALL.iter().map(|kind| kind.name()).collect();
        anyhow!(
            "unknown backend kind `{name}` (known: {})",
            known.join(", ")
        )
    })
}

fn flag_values(flags: BTreeMap<String, toml::Value>, scope: &str) -> Result<Flags> {
    flags
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(name, value)| {
            let value = match value {
                toml::Value::Boolean(value) => FlagValue::Bool(value),
                toml::Value::Integer(value) => FlagValue::Int(value),
                toml::Value::Float(value) => FlagValue::Float(value),
                toml::Value::String(value) => FlagValue::Text(value),
                other => bail!(
                    "{scope}: flag `{name}` is a {}; a flag takes a string, a number or a boolean",
                    other.type_str()
                ),
            };
            Ok((name, value))
        })
        .collect()
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct FileDto {
    version: u32,
    models_dir: PathBuf,
    gateway: Option<GatewayDto>,
    #[serde(default)]
    defaults: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, toml::Value>>,
    #[serde(default)]
    backends: Vec<BackendDto>,
    #[serde(default)]
    models: Vec<ModelDto>,
}

#[derive(Deserialize, Default)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct GatewayDto {
    listen: Option<String>,
    vram_margin_mib: Option<u32>,
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct BackendDto {
    id: String,
    kind: String,
    source: SourceDto,
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase", deny_unknown_fields)]
enum SourceDto {
    Build(String),
    Image(String),
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct ModelDto {
    id: String,
    backend: String,
    repo: Option<String>,
    dir: PathBuf,
    #[serde(default)]
    files: BTreeMap<String, PathBuf>,
    #[serde(default)]
    flags: BTreeMap<String, toml::Value>,
    ctx_size: Option<u32>,
    vram_mib: u32,
    ttl: Option<u32>,
    #[serde(default)]
    preload: bool,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn shipped(models_dir: &str) -> Inventory {
        parse(&render(SHIPPED, Path::new(models_dir)).unwrap()).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_shipped_inventory_has_one_placeholder() {
        assert_eq!(SHIPPED.matches(PLACEHOLDER).count(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_shipped_inventory_is_valid_once_rendered() {
        let inventory = shipped("/data/models");
        assert_eq!(inventory.models_dir(), Path::new("/data/models"));
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.gateway().listen,
            "127.0.0.1:8080".parse().unwrap()
        );
        let ids: Vec<&str> = inventory.models().iter().map(|m| m.id.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(ids, ["qwen3.8-27b", "qwen3.6-35b-a3b"]);
        let backends: Vec<&str> = inventory.backends().iter().map(|b| b.id.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(backends, ["llama-native", "llama-cuda12", "llama-cuda13"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_shipped_inventory_carries_the_presets() {
        let inventory = shipped("/data/models");
        let defaults = inventory.defaults(BackendKind::LlamaServer).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(defaults["flash-attn"], FlagValue::Text("on".into()));
        assert_eq!(defaults["cache-ram"], FlagValue::Int(16384));
        assert_eq!(defaults["no-mmproj-offload"], FlagValue::Bool(true));
        assert_eq!(defaults["temp"], FlagValue::Float(0.6));
        assert_eq!(defaults.len(), 13);

        let main = &inventory.models()[0];
        assert_eq!(main.backend, "llama-native");
        assert_eq!(main.ctx_size, Some(98304));
        assert_eq!(main.ttl, Some(1800));
        assert!(main.preload);
        assert_eq!(main.flags["spec-type"], FlagValue::Text("draft-mtp".into()));
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.model_files(main),
            [
                PathBuf::from("/data/models/llm/qwen3.8-27b/mmproj-F16.gguf"),
                PathBuf::from("/data/models/llm/qwen3.8-27b/Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q4_K_M.gguf"),
                PathBuf::from("/data/models/llm/qwen3.8-27b/MTP/mtp-Qwen3.8-27B-Q4_0.gguf"),
            ]
        );
        let fast = &inventory.models()[1];
        assert!(!fast.preload);
        assert!(fast.flags.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_readme_example_is_valid() {
        let readme = include_str!("../../README.md");
        let section = &readme[readme.find("## The Inventory").unwrap()..];
        let start = section.find("```toml\n").unwrap() + "```toml\n".len();
        let end = start + section[start..].find("```").unwrap();
        let inventory = parse(&section[start..end]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(inventory.models()[0].id, "qwen3.8-27b");
        assert_eq!(inventory.backends().len(), 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_unrendered_template_is_rejected() {
        let message = format!("{:#}", parse(SHIPPED).unwrap_err());
        assert!(message.contains("ai-ctl init"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn rendering_escapes_the_directory() {
        let rendered = render(SHIPPED, Path::new(r#"/data/odd "dir"\x"#)).unwrap();
        let inventory = parse(&rendered).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(inventory.models_dir(), Path::new(r#"/data/odd "dir"\x"#));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rendering_needs_a_placeholder() {
        assert!(render("version = 1\n", Path::new("/data")).is_err());
    }

    const MINIMAL: &str = r#"
        version = 1
        models_dir = "/models"

        [[backends]]
        id = "native"
        kind = "llama-server"
        source = { build = "llama.cpp" }

        [[models]]
        id = "small"
        backend = "native"
        dir = "small"
        ctx_size = 4096
        vram_mib = 2048
        files = { model = "small.gguf" }
    "#;

    fn error(text: &str) -> String {
        format!("{:#}", parse(text).unwrap_err())
    }

    #[test]
    fn optional_sections_have_defaults() {
        let inventory = parse(MINIMAL).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(inventory.gateway(), Gateway::default());
        assert_eq!(inventory.defaults(BackendKind::LlamaServer), None);
        let model = &inventory.models()[0];
        assert_eq!(
            (model.repo.as_deref(), model.ttl, model.preload),
            (None, None, false)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn image_backends_parse() {
        let text = MINIMAL.replace(
            r#"source = { build = "llama.cpp" }"#,
            r#"source = { image = "ghcr.io/ggml-org/llama.cpp:server-cuda13" }"#,
        );
        let inventory = parse(&text).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            inventory.backends()[0].source,
            BackendSource::Image("ghcr.io/ggml-org/llama.cpp:server-cuda13".into())
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_fields_are_rejected() {
        let message = error(&MINIMAL.replace("vram_mib", "vram_mb"));
        assert!(message.contains("vram_mb"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_versions_are_rejected() {
        let message = error(&MINIMAL.replace("version = 1", "version = 2"));
        assert!(
            message.contains("unsupported inventory version 2"),
            "{message}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_backend_kinds_are_rejected() {
        let message = error(&MINIMAL.replace(r#"kind = "llama-server""#, r#"kind = "whisper""#));
        assert!(
            message.contains("unknown backend kind `whisper`"),
            "{message}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_source_is_a_build_or_an_image() {
        let message = error(&MINIMAL.replace("{ build =", "{ binary ="));
        assert!(message.contains("binary"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_gateway_section_takes_each_setting_alone() {
        let text = format!("{MINIMAL}\n[gateway]\nvram_margin_mib = 2048\n");
        let gateway = parse(&text).unwrap().gateway();
        assert_eq!(gateway.vram_margin_mib, 2048);
        assert_eq!(gateway.listen, Gateway::default().listen);
    }

    #[test]
    fn listen_must_be_an_address() {
        let text = format!("{MINIMAL}\n[gateway]\nlisten = \"localhost\"\n");
        assert!(error(&text).contains("gateway.listen `localhost`"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn flags_take_scalars_only() {
        let text = format!("{MINIMAL}\n[models.flags]\nlora = [\"a\", \"b\"]\n");
        let message = error(&text);
        assert!(message.contains("flag `lora` is a array"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn validation_errors_are_reported() {
        let text = MINIMAL.replace("vram_mib = 2048", "vram_mib = 0");
        assert!(error(&text).contains("VRAM budget above zero"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn syntax_errors_are_reported() {
        assert!(parse("version = ").is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn install_writes_once_and_never_overwrites() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let models = root.path().join("models");
        fs::create_dir(&models).unwrap();
        let path = root.path().join("config/ai-ctl/inventory.toml");
        let store = InventoryFile::new(path.clone());

        assert_eq!(store.load().unwrap(), None);
        assert_eq!(store.install(&models).unwrap(), Installed::Written);
        let installed = store.load().unwrap().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(installed.models_dir(), models);

        fs::write(&path, "edited by hand").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(store.install(&models).unwrap(), Installed::AlreadyExists);
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap(), "edited by hand");

        let leftovers: Vec<_> = fs::read_dir(path.parent().unwrap())
            .unwrap()
            .map(|entry| entry.unwrap().file_name())
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(leftovers, ["inventory.toml"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn install_needs_an_existing_models_directory() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let store = InventoryFile::new(root.path().join("inventory.toml"));
        let message = store
            .install(&root.path().join("absent"))
            .unwrap_err()
            .to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("does not exist"), "{message}");

        let file = root.path().join("file");
        fs::write(&file, "").unwrap();
        let message = store.install(&file).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("is not a directory"), "{message}");
        assert!(!root.path().join("inventory.toml").exists());
    }

    #[test]
    fn install_makes_a_relative_models_directory_absolute() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let store = InventoryFile::new(root.path().join("inventory.toml"));
        store.install(Path::new(".")).unwrap();
        let installed = store.load().unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(installed.models_dir().is_absolute());
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unreadable_inventory_names_its_path() {
        let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = root.path().join("inventory.toml");
        fs::write(&path, "version = 1\n").unwrap();
        let message = format!("{:#}", InventoryFile::new(path).load().unwrap_err());
        assert!(message.starts_with("invalid inventory "), "{message}");
        assert!(message.contains("models_dir"), "{message}");
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/nvml.rs">
//! The GPU probe, through NVML.
//!
//! `nvml-wrapper` loads `libnvidia-ml.so` at run time, so a machine without
//! the NVIDIA driver gets a `Missing` GPU instead of a binary that fails to
//! start.

use std::collections::HashSet;
use std::path::Path;

use nvml_wrapper::enums::device::UsedGpuMemory;
use nvml_wrapper::error::NvmlError;
use nvml_wrapper::{Nvml, cuda_driver_version_major, cuda_driver_version_minor};

use crate::application::ports::GpuProbe;
use crate::domain::machine::{Gpu, GpuProcess, Probed, Version};

/// Longest process name NVML is asked for.
const PROCESS_NAME_LEN: usize = 256;

/// Reads the first NVIDIA GPU. NVML is initialised on each call, so commands
/// that never read the GPU never load the library.
pub struct NvmlProbe;

impl GpuProbe for NvmlProbe {
    fn gpu(&self) -> Probed<Gpu> {
        let nvml = match Nvml::init() {
            Ok(nvml) => nvml,
            Err(err) => return init_failure(&err),
        };
        match read_gpu(&nvml) {
            Ok(Some(gpu)) => Probed::Found(gpu),
            Ok(None) => Probed::Failed("NVML sees no GPU".to_owned()),
            Err(err) => Probed::Failed(err.to_string()),
        }
    }
}

/// A library that cannot be loaded means no driver; any other error means a
/// driver that does not answer.
fn init_failure(err: &NvmlError) -> Probed<Gpu> {
    match err {
        NvmlError::LibloadingError(_) | NvmlError::LibraryNotFound => Probed::Missing,
        other => Probed::Failed(other.to_string()),
    }
}

fn read_gpu(nvml: &Nvml) -> Result<Option<Gpu>, NvmlError> {
    if nvml.device_count()? == 0 {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    let device = nvml.device_by_index(0)?;
    let memory = device.memory_info()?;
    let capability = device.cuda_compute_capability()?;
    let cuda = nvml.sys_cuda_driver_version()?;

    let mut seen = HashSet::new();
    let mut processes = Vec::new();
    let running = device
        .running_compute_processes()?
        .into_iter()
        .chain(device.running_graphics_processes()?);
    for info in running {
        if !seen.insert(info.pid) {
            continue;
        }
        let name = nvml
            .sys_process_name(info.pid, PROCESS_NAME_LEN)
            .map(|name| base_name(&name))
            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_owned());
        let memory = match info.used_gpu_memory {
            UsedGpuMemory::Used(bytes) => Some(bytes),
            UsedGpuMemory::Unavailable => None,
        };
        processes.push(GpuProcess {
            pid: info.pid,
            name,
            memory,
        });
    }

    Ok(Some(Gpu {
        name: device.name()?,
        compute_capability: (
            non_negative(capability.major),
            non_negative(capability.minor),
        ),
        memory_total: memory.total,
        memory_reserved: memory.reserved,
        memory_used: memory.used,
        driver_version: nvml.sys_driver_version()?,
        cuda_version: Version::new(
            non_negative(cuda_driver_version_major(cuda)),
            non_negative(cuda_driver_version_minor(cuda)),
            0,
        ),
        processes,
    }))
}

fn non_negative(value: i32) -> u32 {
    u32::try_from(value).unwrap_or(0)
}

/// NVML may return a full path: keep the program name.
fn base_name(name: &str) -> String {
    Path::new(name).file_name().map_or_else(
        || name.to_owned(),
        |base| base.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
    )
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn a_machine_without_nvml_has_no_gpu() {
        assert_eq!(init_failure(&NvmlError::LibraryNotFound), Probed::Missing);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_driver_that_does_not_answer_is_a_failure() {
        assert_eq!(
            init_failure(&NvmlError::DriverNotLoaded),
            Probed::Failed(NvmlError::DriverNotLoaded.to_string())
        );
        assert!(matches!(
            init_failure(&NvmlError::NoPermission),
            Probed::Failed(_)
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn process_names_lose_their_directory() {
        assert_eq!(base_name("/usr/bin/gnome-shell"), "gnome-shell");
        assert_eq!(base_name("Xwayland"), "Xwayland");
    }

    #[test]
    fn negative_values_become_zero() {
        assert_eq!(non_negative(-1), 0);
        assert_eq!(non_negative(12), 12);
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/infrastructure/paths.rs">
//! Where ai-ctl keeps its files, after the XDG base directory specification.

use std::ffi::OsString;
use std::path::PathBuf;

use anyhow::{Context, Result};

const APP: &str = "ai-ctl";

/// ai-ctl's directories: configuration (the inventory), data (builds) and
/// state (the gateway's PID and logs).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Paths {
    pub config_dir: PathBuf,
    pub data_dir: PathBuf,
    pub state_dir: PathBuf,
}

impl Paths {
    /// Resolves the directories from the process environment.
    pub fn from_env() -> Result<Self> {
        Self::resolve(|name| std::env::var_os(name))
    }

    /// Resolves the directories from `var`. An `XDG_*` variable counts only
    /// when it holds an absolute path, as the specification requires;
    /// otherwise the default under `HOME` applies.
    pub fn resolve(var: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<OsString>) -> Result<Self> {
        let absolute = |name: &str| {
            var(name)
                .map(PathBuf::from)
                .filter(|path| path.is_absolute())
        };
        let base = |xdg: &str, default: &str| -> Result<PathBuf> {
            match absolute(xdg) {
                Some(path) => Ok(path),
                None => absolute("HOME")
                    .map(|home| home.join(default))
                    .with_context(|| format!("neither {xdg} nor HOME is set to an absolute path")),
            }
        };
        Ok(Self {
            config_dir: base("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", ".config")?.join(APP),
            data_dir: base("XDG_DATA_HOME", ".local/share")?.join(APP),
            state_dir: base("XDG_STATE_HOME", ".local/state")?.join(APP),
        })
    }

    pub fn inventory_file(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.config_dir.join("inventory.toml")
    }

    /// The projects' git clones.
    pub fn sources_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.data_dir.join("sources")
    }

    /// The CMake build trees, removed after each successful build.
    pub fn work_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.data_dir.join("work")
    }

    /// The installed builds.
    pub fn builds_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.data_dir.join("builds")
    }

    /// The installed releases of the gateway, llama-swap.
    pub fn gateway_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.data_dir.join("llama-swap")
    }

    /// The running gateway's record and configuration.
    pub fn gateway_state_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.state_dir.join("gateway")
    }

    /// The build logs and the gateway's.
    pub fn logs_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.state_dir.join("logs")
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::collections::HashMap;

    use super::*;

    fn resolve(vars: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Result<Paths> {
        let vars: HashMap<String, OsString> = vars
            .iter()
            .map(|(name, value)| ((*name).to_owned(), OsString::from(value)))
            .collect();
        Paths::resolve(|name| vars.get(name).cloned())
    }

    #[test]
    fn defaults_live_under_home() {
        let paths = resolve(&[("HOME", "/home/user")]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(paths.config_dir, PathBuf::from("/home/user/.config/ai-ctl"));
        assert_eq!(
            paths.data_dir,
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.state_dir,
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/state/ai-ctl")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.inventory_file(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.config/ai-ctl/inventory.toml")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.builds_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl/builds")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.sources_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl/sources")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.work_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl/work")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.gateway_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl/llama-swap")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.gateway_state_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/state/ai-ctl/gateway")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.logs_dir(),
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/state/ai-ctl/logs")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn xdg_variables_take_precedence() {
        let paths = resolve(&[
            ("HOME", "/home/user"),
            ("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "/cfg"),
            ("XDG_DATA_HOME", "/data"),
            ("XDG_STATE_HOME", "/state"),
        ])
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(paths.config_dir, PathBuf::from("/cfg/ai-ctl"));
        assert_eq!(paths.data_dir, PathBuf::from("/data/ai-ctl"));
        assert_eq!(paths.state_dir, PathBuf::from("/state/ai-ctl"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_or_relative_xdg_variables_are_ignored() {
        let paths = resolve(&[
            ("HOME", "/home/user"),
            ("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", ""),
            ("XDG_DATA_HOME", "relative/data"),
        ])
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(paths.config_dir, PathBuf::from("/home/user/.config/ai-ctl"));
        assert_eq!(
            paths.data_dir,
            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.local/share/ai-ctl")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_or_relative_home_is_an_error() {
        assert!(resolve(&[]).is_err());
        let message = resolve(&[("HOME", "home/user")]).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("XDG_CONFIG_HOME"), "{message}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn xdg_variables_alone_are_enough() {
        let paths = resolve(&[
            ("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "/cfg"),
            ("XDG_DATA_HOME", "/data"),
            ("XDG_STATE_HOME", "/state"),
        ])
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(paths.config_dir, PathBuf::from("/cfg/ai-ctl"));
    }
}

<file path="src/infrastructure/podman.rs">
//! Containers and their images, through podman driven as commands.

use std::process::Command;

use anyhow::{Result, anyhow, bail};

use crate::application::ports::Containers;

use super::command::{self, Output, RunError};

const MISSING: &str = "podman not found; run `sudo dnf install podman`";

/// podman, run by the user: images go to their rootless storage.
pub struct Podman;

impl Containers for Podman {
    fn image_exists(&self, image: &str) -> Result<bool> {
        let output = checked(command::run("podman", &["image", "exists", image]))?;
        // A missing image exits with an error and prints nothing.
        match (output.success, output.stderr.trim().is_empty()) {
            (true, _) => Ok(true),
            (false, true) => Ok(false),
            (false, false) => bail!("podman image exists {image}: {}", output.failure()),
        }
    }

    fn pull(&self, image: &str) -> Result<()> {
        let mut pull = Command::new("podman");
        pull.args(["pull", "--quiet", image]);
        // An image of several GB takes as long as the network does.
        let output = checked(command::execute(pull, None))?;
        if !output.success {
            bail!("podman pull {image}: {}", output.failure());
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn pids(&self, prefix: &str) -> Result<Vec<u32>> {
        let filter = format!("name=^{prefix}");
        let output = match command::run(
            "podman",
            &["ps", "--filter", &filter, "--format", "{{.Pid}}"],
        ) {
            // Without podman, no container runs.
            Err(RunError::NotFound) => return Ok(Vec::new()),
            result => checked(result)?,
        };
        if !output.success {
            bail!("podman ps: {}", output.failure());
        }
        parse_pids(&output.stdout)
    }
}

/// One PID per line, as `podman ps --format {{.Pid}}` prints them.
fn parse_pids(text: &str) -> Result<Vec<u32>> {
    text.lines()
        .map(str::trim)
        .filter(|line| !line.is_empty())
        .map(|line| {
            line.parse()
                .map_err(|_| anyhow!("podman ps printed {line:?} for a PID"))
        })
        .collect()
}

fn checked(result: Result<Output, RunError>) -> Result<Output> {
    result.map_err(|err| match err {
        RunError::NotFound => anyhow!(MISSING),
        RunError::Failed(message) => anyhow!(message),
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn pids_are_read_one_per_line() {
        assert_eq!(parse_pids("99159\n4021\n").unwrap(), [99159, 4021]);
        assert_eq!(parse_pids("").unwrap(), Vec::<u32>::new());
        assert_eq!(
            parse_pids("<no value>\n").unwrap_err().to_string(),
            "podman ps printed \"<no value>\" for a PID"
        );
    }
}
</file>

<file path="src/presentation/build.rs">
//! `ai-ctl build`: runs a build with its progress on stderr, and renders the
//! installed builds.

use std::fmt::Write as _;
use std::io::{self, IsTerminal, Write};
use std::process::ExitCode;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

use anyhow::{Context, Result};

use crate::application::build::{self, Builder, Built, Event, Listing, Pruned, Request};
use crate::application::ports::Progress;
use crate::domain::build::BuildId;

use super::Services;
use super::cli::{BuildAction, BuildCommand};

/// Runs `ai-ctl build` and its subcommands.
pub fn run(command: BuildCommand, services: &Services) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    let store = services.builds.as_ref();
    let output = match command.action {
        Some(BuildAction::List { project }) => {
            render_list(&build::list(store, project.as_deref())?)
        }
        Some(BuildAction::Use { project, id }) => {
            let previous = build::use_build(store, &project, &id)?;
            render_use(&project, &id, previous.as_ref())
        }
        Some(BuildAction::Prune { keep, project }) => {
            render_pruned(&build::prune(store, project.as_deref(), keep)?)
        }
        None => {
            let project = command
                .project
                .context("a project to build is required, such as llama.cpp")?;
            let builder = Builder {
                gpu: services.gpu.as_ref(),
                host: services.host.as_ref(),
                sources: services.sources.as_ref(),
                cmake: services.cmake.as_ref(),
                store,
                programs: services.programs.as_ref(),
                clock: &services.clock,
            };
            let request = Request {
                project: &project,
                reference: Some(&command.reference),
                force: command.force,
                jobs: command.jobs.map(std::num::NonZero::get),
            };
            let started = Instant::now();
            let mut reporter = Reporter::new(io::stderr(), io::stderr().is_terminal());
            let built = builder.build(&request, &mut |event| reporter.event(event));
            reporter.end_line();
            render_built(&built?, started.elapsed())
        }
    };
    super::print(&output)?;
    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
}

/// Follows a build: one line per step, and on a terminal a progress line
/// rewritten in place. Write errors are ignored: the build goes on.
pub struct Reporter<W: Write> {
    out: W,
    terminal: bool,
    /// A progress line is on screen without its newline.
    open_line: bool,
    /// The last quarter reported off a terminal.
    quarter: Option<u32>,
}

impl<W: Write> Reporter<W> {
    pub fn new(out: W, terminal: bool) -> Self {
        Self {
            out,
            terminal,
            open_line: false,
            quarter: None,
        }
    }

    pub fn event(&mut self, event: Event) {
        let line = match event {
            Event::Resolving => "Looking up the latest release".to_owned(),
            Event::Fetching { reference } => format!("Fetching the sources at {reference}"),
            Event::CheckedOut { id, commit } => {
                format!("Checked out {} as build {id}", &commit.as_str()[..10])
            }
            Event::Compile(Progress::Configuring) => "Configuring".to_owned(),
            Event::Compile(Progress::Building { done, total }) => {
                self.progress(done, total);
                return;
            }
            Event::Testing => "Testing the new binary".to_owned(),
            Event::Warning(message) => format!("warning: {message}"),
        };
        self.end_line();
        let _ = writeln!(self.out, "{line}");
    }

    fn progress(&mut self, done: u32, total: u32) {
        let percent = done * 100 / total.max(1);
        if self.terminal {
            let _ = write!(self.out, "\r\x1b[2KCompiling {done}/{total} ({percent}%)");
            let _ = self.out.flush();
            self.open_line = true;
        } else {
            let quarter = percent / 25;
            if self.quarter != Some(quarter) {
                self.quarter = Some(quarter);
                let _ = writeln!(self.out, "Compiling {done}/{total} ({percent}%)");
            }
        }
    }

    /// Ends a progress line left open, before other output.
    pub fn end_line(&mut self) {
        if self.open_line {
            let _ = writeln!(self.out);
            self.open_line = false;
        }
    }
}

pub fn render_built(built: &Built, elapsed: Duration) -> String {
    let build = &built.build;
    let mut out = format!(
        "Built {} {} in {}: commit {}, CUDA {}, {} {}\n",
        build.project,
        build.id,
        duration(elapsed),
        &build.commit.as_str()[..10],
        build.toolkit,
        build.host_compiler.command,
        build.host_compiler.version
    );
    let _ = write!(out, "Current build: {}", built.binary.display());
    match &built.previous {
        Some(previous) => {
            let _ = writeln!(out, " (was {previous})");
        }
        None => out.push('\n'),
    }
    out
}

/// `4 min 10 s`, or `52 s` under a minute.
fn duration(elapsed: Duration) -> String {
    let seconds = elapsed.as_secs();
    if seconds < 60 {
        format!("{seconds} s")
    } else {
        format!("{} min {} s", seconds / 60, seconds % 60)
    }
}

pub fn render_list(listings: &[Listing]) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for listing in listings {
        if listing.builds.is_empty() && listing.unreadable.is_empty() {
            let _ = writeln!(
                out,
                "{}: no build yet; `ai-ctl build {}` makes one",
                listing.project, listing.project
            );
            continue;
        }
        let _ = writeln!(out, "{}", listing.project);
        let width = listing
            .builds
            .iter()
            .map(|build| build.id.as_str().len())
            .max()
            .unwrap_or(0);
        for build in &listing.builds {
            let marker = if listing.current.as_ref() == Some(&build.id) {
                '*'
            } else {
                ' '
            };
            let _ = writeln!(
                out,
                "  {marker} {:<width$}  {}  {}  CUDA {}  {} {}",
                build.id.as_str(),
                build.built_at.minutes(),
                &build.commit.as_str()[..10],
                build.toolkit,
                build.host_compiler.command,
                build.host_compiler.version
            );
        }
        if let Some(current) = &listing.current
            && !listing.builds.iter().any(|build| &build.id == current)
        {
            let _ = writeln!(out, "  current build {current} is not installed");
        }
        for entry in &listing.unreadable {
            let _ = writeln!(
                out,
                "  not a build: {} ({})",
                entry.dir.display(),
                entry.reason
            );
        }
    }
    out
}

pub fn render_use(project: &str, id: &str, previous: Option<&BuildId>) -> String {
    match previous {
        Some(previous) => format!("{project}: current build is now {id} (was {previous})\n"),
        None => format!("{project}: current build is {id}\n"),
    }
}

pub fn render_pruned(pruned: &[Pruned]) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for project in pruned {
        let removed: Vec<&str> = project.removed.iter().map(BuildId::as_str).collect();
        let what = if removed.is_empty() {
            "nothing to remove".to_owned()
        } else {
            format!("removed {}", removed.join(", "))
        };
        let _ = writeln!(out, "{}: {what}; {} kept", project.project, project.kept);
        for entry in &project.unreadable {
            let _ = writeln!(
                out,
                "  left alone, not a build: {} ({})",
                entry.dir.display(),
                entry.reason
            );
        }
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::collections::BTreeMap;
    use std::path::PathBuf;

    use super::*;
    use crate::application::ports::Unreadable;
    use crate::domain::build::{Build, CommitId};
    use crate::domain::machine::{HostCompiler, Version};
    use crate::domain::timestamp::Timestamp;

    fn build(id: &str, at: &str) -> Build {
        Build {
            project: "llama.cpp".into(),
            id: BuildId::parse(id).unwrap(),
            commit: CommitId::parse("79e2e74eb11022c1ba2e438df7f0ca2d4c10f8b6").unwrap(),
            toolkit: Version::new(13, 4, 92),
            host_compiler: HostCompiler {
                command: "g++".into(),
                path: PathBuf::from("/usr/bin/g++"),
                version: Version::new(16, 2, 1),
            },
            cmake_flags: BTreeMap::new(),
            built_at: Timestamp::parse(at).unwrap(),
        }
    }

    fn id(text: &str) -> BuildId {
        BuildId::parse(text).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_build_reports_what_it_made_and_what_it_replaced() {
        let built = Built {
            build: build("b11538", "2026-10-09T20:36:00Z"),
            binary: "/data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server".into(),
            previous: Some(id("b11400")),
        };
        assert_eq!(
            render_built(&built, Duration::from_secs(250)),
            "Built llama.cpp b11538 in 4 min 10 s: commit 79e2e74eb1, CUDA 13.4.92, g++ 16.2.1\n\
             Current build: /data/builds/llama.cpp/b11538/llama-server (was b11400)\n"
        );
        let first = Built {
            previous: None,
            ..built
        };
        assert!(render_built(&first, Duration::from_secs(52)).contains(" in 52 s: "));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_list_marks_the_current_build() {
        let listing = Listing {
            project: "llama.cpp",
            builds: vec![
                build("b11538", "2026-10-09T20:36:00Z"),
                build("79e2e74eb1", "2026-10-08T09:05:00Z"),
            ],
            current: Some(id("b11538")),
            unreadable: vec![Unreadable {
                dir: "/data/builds/llama.cpp/notes".into(),
                reason: "cannot read build.json".into(),
            }],
        };
        assert_eq!(
            render_list(&[listing]),
            "llama.cpp\n\
             \x20 * b11538      2026-10-09 20:36 UTC  79e2e74eb1  CUDA 13.4.92  g++ 16.2.1\n\
             \x20   79e2e74eb1  2026-10-08 09:05 UTC  79e2e74eb1  CUDA 13.4.92  g++ 16.2.1\n\
             \x20 not a build: /data/builds/llama.cpp/notes (cannot read build.json)\n"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_list_says_how_to_build() {
        let listing = Listing {
            project: "llama.cpp",
            builds: vec![],
            current: None,
            unreadable: vec![],
        };
        assert_eq!(
            render_list(&[listing]),
            "llama.cpp: no build yet; `ai-ctl build llama.cpp` makes one\n"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_dangling_current_link_is_shown() {
        let listing = Listing {
            project: "llama.cpp",
            builds: vec![build("b1", "2026-10-09T20:36:00Z")],
            current: Some(id("b9")),
            unreadable: vec![],
        };
        assert!(render_list(&[listing]).ends_with("  current build b9 is not installed\n"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn use_and_prune_say_what_changed() {
        assert_eq!(
            render_use("llama.cpp", "b1", Some(&id("b2"))),
            "llama.cpp: current build is now b1 (was b2)\n"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            render_use("llama.cpp", "b1", None),
            "llama.cpp: current build is b1\n"
        );
        let pruned = [Pruned {
            project: "llama.cpp",
            removed: vec![id("b1"), id("b2")],
            kept: 2,
            unreadable: vec![],
        }];
        assert_eq!(
            render_pruned(&pruned),
            "llama.cpp: removed b1, b2; 2 kept\n"
        );
        let nothing = [Pruned {
            project: "llama.cpp",
            removed: vec![],
            kept: 1,
            unreadable: vec![],
        }];
        assert_eq!(
            render_pruned(&nothing),
            "llama.cpp: nothing to remove; 1 kept\n"
        );
    }

    fn report(terminal: bool, events: Vec<Event>) -> String {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let mut reporter = Reporter::new(&mut out, terminal);
        for event in events {
            reporter.event(event);
        }
        reporter.end_line();
        String::from_utf8(out).unwrap()
    }

    fn compiling(done: u32) -> Event {
        Event::Compile(Progress::Building { done, total: 356 })
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_terminal_sees_one_progress_line_rewritten() {
        let text = report(
            true,
            vec![
                Event::Compile(Progress::Configuring),
                compiling(1),
                compiling(356),
                Event::Testing,
            ],
        );
        assert_eq!(
            text,
            "Configuring\n\r\x1b[2KCompiling 1/356 (0%)\r\x1b[2KCompiling 356/356 (100%)\nTesting the new binary\n"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_log_sees_the_progress_by_quarters() {
        let events = (1..=356).map(compiling).collect();
        let text = report(false, events);
        assert_eq!(
            text,
            "Compiling 1/356 (0%)\nCompiling 89/356 (25%)\nCompiling 178/356 (50%)\nCompiling 267/356 (75%)\nCompiling 356/356 (100%)\n"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn steps_name_the_reference_and_the_build() {
        let text = report(
            false,
            vec![
                Event::Resolving,
                Event::Fetching {
                    reference: "b11538".into(),
                },
                Event::CheckedOut {
                    id: id("b11538"),
                    commit: CommitId::parse("79e2e74eb11022c1ba2e438df7f0ca2d4c10f8b6").unwrap(),
                },
                Event::Warning("cannot remove /work".into()),
            ],
        );
        assert_eq!(
            text,
            "Looking up the latest release\nFetching the sources at b11538\nChecked out 79e2e74eb1 as build b11538\nwarning: cannot remove /work\n"
        );
    }
}

//! `ai-ctl completions <shell>`: prints a shell completion script.

use std::io::Write;

use clap::CommandFactory;
use clap_complete::{Shell, generate};

use super::cli::Cli;

/// Writes the completion script for `shell` to `out`.
pub fn write(shell: Shell, out: &mut impl Write) {
    let mut command = Cli::command();
    let name = command.get_name().to_owned();
    generate(shell, &mut command, name, out);
}
